We use a smooth approximation of an indicator for bounding the probability of a given event; see a standard usage in e.g. the proof of Theorem 3.3 in
[
14
]
.

Lemma 15

(Lemma 34 of
[
35
]
)
.

Fix any
m\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
,
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
and
\delta>0
. Then there exists an
m
-times differentiable
\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
function
h_{m;\tau;\delta}
such that
h_{m;\tau+\delta;\delta}(x)\;\leq\;\mathbb{I}_{\{x>\tau\}}\leq h_{m;\tau;\delta}(x)
. For
0\leq r\leq m
, the
r
-th derivative
h^{(r)}_{m;\tau;\delta}
is continuous and bounded above by
\delta^{-r}
. Moreover, for every
\epsilon\in[0,1]
,
h^{(m)}
satisfies that

\displaystyle|h^{(m)}_{m;\tau;\delta}(x)-h^{(m)}_{m;\tau;\delta}(y)|\;\leq\;C_{m,\epsilon}\,\delta^{-(m+\epsilon)}\,|x-y|^{\epsilon}\;,

with respect to the constant
C_{m,\epsilon}=\binom{m}{\lfloor m/2\rfloor}(m+1)^{m+\epsilon}
.

The next result allows us to control the difference in expectation of the above approximation functions evaluated at independent random quantities:

Lemma 16
.

Fix
t\in\mathbb{R}
,
\delta>0
and define
h_{t;\delta}\equiv h_{2;t;\delta}
as in
Lemma

15
. Let
\mathbf{V},\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{W}
be some random vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{b}
and
Y
be a random variable in
\mathbb{R}
, with dependence allowed. Then there exists an absolute constant
C
such that for any
\nu\in(2,3]
and
\delta>0
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[h_{t;\delta}(\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{V}+Y)-h_{t;\delta}(\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}+Y)]|\;\leq\;Q_{1}+Q_{2}+Q_{3}\;,

where

\displaystyle Q_{1}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\big|\mathbb{E}\big[h^{\prime}_{t;\delta}(Y)\,\mathbf{W}^{\top}(\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{Z})\big]\big|\;,\quad\quad Q_{2}\;\coloneqq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\,\big|\mathbb{E}\big[h^{\prime\prime}_{t;\delta}(Y)\,\big(\mathbf{W}^{\top}\,\big(\mathbf{V}\mathbf{V}^{\top}-\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\big)\,\mathbf{W}\big)\big]\big|\;,

\displaystyle Q_{3}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;54\delta^{-\nu}(\|\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{V}\|_{L_{\nu}}^{\nu}+\|\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\|_{L_{\nu}}^{\nu})\;.

Assume additionally that
(\mathbf{V},\mathbf{Z})
is independent of
(\mathbf{W},Y)
. If
\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{V}]=\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{Z}]
, then
Q_{1}=0
. If
\text{\rm Var}[\mathbf{V}]=\text{\rm Var}[\mathbf{Z}]
, then
Q_{2}=0
.

Since
h_{t;\delta}
is twice continuously differentiable, by a second-order Taylor expansion with the integral remainder, we get that for any
w,r\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle h_{t;\delta}(u)\;=

\displaystyle\;h_{t;\delta}(r)+h^{\prime}_{t;\delta}(r)\,(u-r)+\mint_{r}^{u}h^{\prime\prime}_{t;\delta}(t)\,(u-t)\,dt

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;h_{t;\delta}(r)+h^{\prime}_{t;\delta}(r)\,(u-r)+\mint_{0}^{1}h^{\prime\prime}_{t;\delta}\big((u-r)\theta+r\big)\,(u-r)^{2}(1-\theta)\,d\theta

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;h_{t;\delta}(r)+h^{\prime}_{t;\delta}(r)\,(u-r)+\mathbb{E}\Big[h^{\prime\prime}_{t;\delta}\big((u-r)\Theta+r\big)\,(u-r)^{2}(1-\Theta)\Big]\;,

(13)

where
\Theta\sim\textrm{Uniform}[0,1]
. By applying this expansion once with
u=\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{V}+Y,r=Y
and once with
u=\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}+Y,r=Y
, we get that

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\big[h_{t;\delta}(\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{V}+Y)-h_{t;\delta}(\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}+Y)\big]\big|

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[h^{\prime}_{t;\delta}(Y)\,(\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{V})-h^{\prime}_{t;\delta}(Y)\,(\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{Z})+h^{\prime\prime}_{t;\delta}\big(\Theta\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{V}+Y\big)\,(\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{V})^{2}(1-\Theta)

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;\;\;\;-h^{\prime\prime}_{t;\delta}\big(\Theta\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}+Y\big)\,(\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{Z})^{2}(1-\Theta)\Big]\Big|\;.

By adding and subtracting two second derivative terms evaluated at
t
, we can further obtain

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}\big[h_{t;\delta}

\displaystyle(\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{V}+Y)-h_{t;\delta}(\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}+Y)\big]\big|

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\Big|\mathbb{E}\big[h^{\prime}_{t;\delta}(Y)\,\mathbf{W}^{\top}(\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{Z})\big]+\mathbb{E}\big[h^{\prime\prime}_{t;\delta}(Y)\,\big((\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{V})^{2}-(\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{Z})^{2}\big)(1-\Theta)\big]

\displaystyle\;\,+\mathbb{E}\big[\big(h^{\prime\prime}_{t;\delta}\big(\Theta\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{V}+Y\big)-h^{\prime\prime}_{t;\delta}(Y)\big)\,(\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{V})^{2}(1-\Theta)\big]

\displaystyle\;\,-\mathbb{E}\big[\big(h^{\prime\prime}_{t;\delta}\big(\Theta\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}+Y\big)-h^{\prime\prime}_{t;\delta}(Y)\big)\,(\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{Z})^{2}(1-\Theta)\big]\Big|

\displaystyle\;\eqqcolon

\displaystyle\;|Q_{1}+Q_{2}+Q_{V}-Q_{Z}|\;\leq\;|Q_{1}|+|Q_{2}|+|Q_{V}|+|Q_{Z}|\;.

(14)

We handle the four terms individually.
Q_{1}
is already in the desired form.
Q_{2}
can be simplified by noting that
\Theta
is independent of all other variables:

\displaystyle Q_{2}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\,\mathbb{E}\big[h^{\prime\prime}_{t;\delta}(Y)\,\big(\mathbf{W}^{\top}\,\big(\mathbf{V}\mathbf{V}^{\top}-\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\big)\,\mathbf{W}\big)\big]\;.

Now to handle
Q_{V}
and
Q_{Z}
, we use a Jensen’s inequality to move the absolute sign inside the expectation and note that
1-\Theta
is bounded in norm by
1
:

\displaystyle|Q_{V}|\;=

\displaystyle\;\big|\mathbb{E}\big[\big(h^{\prime\prime}_{t;\delta}\big(\Theta\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{V}+Y\big)-h^{\prime\prime}_{t;\delta}(Y)\big)\,(\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{V})^{2}(1-\Theta)\big]\big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\big[\big|h^{\prime\prime}_{t;\delta}\big(\Theta\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{V}+Y\big)-h^{\prime\prime}_{t;\delta}(Y)\big|\times\big(\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{V}\big)^{2}\big]\;.

Recall the Hölder property of
h^{\prime\prime}_{t;\delta}
from
Lemma

15
: For any
\epsilon\in[0,1]
and
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\big|h^{\prime\prime}_{t;\delta}(x)-h^{\prime\prime}_{t;\delta}(y)\big|\;\leq

\displaystyle\;54\delta^{-2-\epsilon}\,|x-y|^{\epsilon}\,\;,

Applying this to the bound above with
\epsilon=\nu-2\in(0,1]
,
x=\Theta\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{V}+Y
and
y=Y
, while noting that
|\Theta|
is bounded above by
1
almost surely, we get that

\displaystyle|Q_{V}|\;\leq

\displaystyle\;54\,\delta^{-\nu}\mathbb{E}\big[|\Theta\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{V}|^{\nu-2}\,(\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{V})^{2}\big]\;\leq\;54\,\delta^{-\nu}\,\big\|\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{V}\big\|_{L_{\nu}}^{\nu}\;.

To deal with
Q_{Z}
, the argument is the same except
\mathbf{V}
is replaced by
\mathbf{Z}
:

\displaystyle|Q_{Z}|\;\leq\;54\,\delta^{-\nu}\,\big\|\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\big\|_{L_{\nu}}^{\nu}\;.

Applying the bounds to (
14
) then gives the first desired bound:

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}\big[h_{t;\delta}(\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{V}+Y)

\displaystyle-h_{t;\delta}(\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}+Y)\big]\big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;|Q_{1}|+|Q_{2}|+54\,\delta^{-\nu}\big(\big\|\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{V}\big\|_{L_{\nu}}^{\nu}+\big\|\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\big\|_{L_{\nu}}^{\nu}\big)\;.

In the case where
\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{V}]=\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{Z}]
and
(\mathbf{V},\mathbf{Z})
is independent of
(\mathbf{W},Y)
, we get that

\displaystyle Q_{1}\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\big[h^{\prime}_{t;\delta}(Y)\,\mathbf{W}^{\top}(\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{Z})\big]\;=\;\mathbb{E}\big[h^{\prime}_{t;\delta}(Y)\,\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{Z}]\big]\;=\;0\;.

Similarly in the case where
\text{\rm Var}[\mathbf{V}]=\text{\rm Var}[\mathbf{Z}]
and
(\mathbf{V},\mathbf{Z})
is independent of
(\mathbf{W},Y)
, we have

Q_{2}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\,\mathbb{E}\big[h^{\prime\prime}_{t;\delta}(Y)\,\big(\mathbf{W}^{\top}\,\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{V}\mathbf{V}^{\top}-\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{Z}^{\top}]\,\mathbf{W}\big)\big]\;=\;0\;.\qed

The next lemma is convenient for simplifying moment terms involving Gaussians:

Lemma 17
.

Consider a zero-mean
\mathbb{R}^{b}
-valued Gaussian vector
\eta
. Suppose
\text{\rm Var}[\eta]=\text{\rm Var}[V]
for some
\mathbb{R}^{b}
zero-mean random vector
V
and let
W
be a random vector in
\mathbb{R}^{b}
independent of
\eta
and
V
. Then for any real number
\nu\geq 2
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big|W^{\top}\eta\big|^{\nu}\big]\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2^{\nu/2} \, \Gamma\big( \frac{\nu+1}{2}\big) }{\sqrt{\pi}}$}}\,\mathbb{E}\big[|W^{\top}V|^{\nu}\big]\;,

where
\Gamma
represents the Gamma function. In the case
\nu=2
, we have
\text{\rm Var}[W^{\top}\eta]=\text{\rm Var}[W^{\top}V]
.

Note that
W
is independent of
\eta
and
V
. Conditioning on
W
,
W^{\top}\eta
is a zero-mean normal random variable with variance given by

\displaystyle W^{\top}\text{\rm Var}[\eta]W\;=

\displaystyle\;W^{\top}\text{\rm Var}[V]W\;=\;\mathbb{E}\big[W^{\top}VV^{\top}W\big|W\big]\;=\;\mathbb{E}\big[(W^{\top}V)^{2}\big|W\big]\;.

Applying the formula of the
\nu
-th moment of a Gaussian random variable followed by a Jensen’s inequality with
\nu\geq 2
, we get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big|W^{\top}\eta\big|^{\nu}\big]\;=\;\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}\big[\big|W^{\top}\eta\big|^{\nu}\big|W\big]\big]\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2^{\nu/2} \, \Gamma\big( \frac{\nu+1}{2}\big) }{\sqrt{\pi}}$}}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}\big[(W^{\top}V)^{2}\big|W\big]^{\nu/2}\big]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2^{\nu/2} \, \Gamma\big( \frac{\nu+1}{2}\big) }{\sqrt{\pi}}$}}\mathbb{E}\big[|W^{\top}V|^{\nu}\big]\;.

For
\nu=2
, the above becomes an equality and we get
\text{\rm Var}[W^{\top}\eta]=\text{\rm Var}[W^{\top}V]
.
∎

We also restate the Carbery-Wright inequality (Theorem 8 of
[
11
]
) with our notation:

Lemma 18

(Carbery-Wright inequality)
.

Let
Q(\mathbf{x})
be a polynomial of
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
of degree at most
m
taking values in
\mathbb{R}
, and
\eta
be a standard Gaussian vector taking values in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Then there exists a constant
C
independent of
Q
,
d
or
m
such that, for every
t\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(|Q(\eta)|\leq t\big)\;\leq\;C\,m\,t^{1/m}(\mathbb{E}[|Q(\eta)|^{2}])^{-1/2m}\;.

We first note that since
q_{m}
is affine in each argument,
\mathbb{E}[q_{m}(\Xi)]=\mathbb{E}[q_{m}(\mathbf{X})]=0
. Meanwhile, by applying
Lemma

17
repeatedly,
\text{\rm Var}[q_{m}(\mathbf{X})]=\text{\rm Var}[q_{m}(\Xi)]
. This proves the second part of
Theorem

1
.

To prove the first part, we denote
\tilde{q}_{m}\coloneqq\sigma^{-1}q_{m}
for simplicity. We first approximate the probability terms. For
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
and
\delta>0
, let
h_{\tau;\delta}\equiv h_{2;\tau;\delta}
be the twice continuously differentiable function defined in
Lemma

15
, which satisfies that
h_{\tau+\delta;\delta}(x)\leq\mathbb{I}_{\{x>\tau\}}\leq h_{\tau;\delta}(x)
for any
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
. This allows us to bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\tilde{q}_{m}(\mathbf{X})>t)-\mathbb{P}(\tilde{q}_{m}(\Xi)>t-\delta)\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{I}_{\{\tilde{q}_{m}(\mathbf{X})>t\}}-\mathbb{I}_{\{\tilde{q}_{m}(\Xi)>t-\delta\}}\big]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\big[h_{t;\delta}\big(\tilde{q}_{m}(\mathbf{X})\big)-h_{t;\delta}\big(\tilde{q}_{m}(\Xi)\big)\big]\;,

and similarly

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\tilde{q}_{m}(\Xi)>t+\delta)-\mathbb{P}(\tilde{q}_{m}(\mathbf{X})>t)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\big[h_{t+\delta;\delta}\big(\tilde{q}_{m}(\Xi)\big)-h_{t+\delta;\delta}\big(\tilde{q}_{m}(\mathbf{X})\big)\big]\;.

By expressing

\mathbb{P}(\tilde{q}_{m}(\Xi)>t-\delta)\;=\;\mathbb{P}(\tilde{q}_{m}(\Xi)>t)+\mathbb{P}(t-\delta<\tilde{q}_{m}(\Xi)\leq t)

and performing a similar decomposition for
\mathbb{P}(\tilde{q}_{m}(\Xi)>t+\delta)
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{P}(\sigma^{-1}q_{m}(\mathbf{X})\leq

\displaystyle t)-\mathbb{P}(\sigma^{-1}q_{m}(\Xi)\leq t)\big|\;=\;|\mathbb{P}(\tilde{q}_{m}(\mathbf{X})>t)-\mathbb{P}(\tilde{q}_{m}(\Xi)>t)|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\max\{\mathbb{P}(t-\delta<\tilde{q}_{m}(\Xi)\leq t)\,,\,\mathbb{P}(t\leq\tilde{q}_{m}(\Xi)<t+\delta)\}+\max\{E_{t},E_{t+\delta}\}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}(t-\delta<\tilde{q}_{m}(\Xi)<t+\delta)+E_{t}+E_{t+\delta}\;,

(15)

where we have defined
E_{\tau}\coloneqq|\mathbb{E}[h_{\tau;\delta}(\tilde{q}_{m}(\mathbf{X}))-h_{\tau;\delta}(\tilde{q}_{m}(\Xi))]|
for
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
. The next step is to control quantities of the form
E_{\tau}
by Lindeberg’s principle. We recall
\mathbf{W}_{i}=(\mathbf{X}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{X}_{i-1},\mathbf{0},\xi_{i+1},\ldots,\xi_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{nD}
and define
\mathbf{W}_{i}^{*}=(\mathbf{X}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{X}_{i-1},\mathbf{X}_{i},\xi_{i+1},\ldots,\xi_{n})
. Then by expanding the difference into a telescoping sum and applying a triangle inequality,

\displaystyle E_{\tau}\;=

\displaystyle\;|\mathbb{E}[h_{\tau;\delta}(\tilde{q}_{m}(\mathbf{X}))-h_{\tau;\delta}(\tilde{q}_{m}(\Xi))]|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\msum_{i=1}^{n}|\mathbb{E}[h_{\tau;\delta}(\tilde{q}_{m}(\mathbf{W}_{i}^{*}))-h_{\tau;\delta}(\tilde{q}_{m}(\mathbf{W}_{i-1}^{*}))]|

\displaystyle\;\overset{(a)}{=}

\displaystyle\;\msum_{i=1}^{n}|\mathbb{E}[h_{\tau;\delta}(\tilde{q}_{m}(\mathbf{W}_{i}^{*}))-h_{\tau;\delta}(\tilde{q}_{m}(\mathbf{W}_{i-1}^{*}))]|\;\eqqcolon\;\msum_{i=1}^{n}E_{\tau;i}\;.

We focus on bounding each
E_{\tau;i}
. Since
q_{m}
is affine in the
i
-th argument, we get

\displaystyle\tilde{q}_{m}(\mathbf{W}_{i}^{*})\;=

\displaystyle\;\partial_{i}\tilde{q}_{m}(\mathbf{W}_{i})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}+\tilde{q}_{m}(\mathbf{W}_{i})

and

\displaystyle\tilde{q}_{m}(\mathbf{W}_{i-1}^{*})\;=

\displaystyle\;\partial_{i}\tilde{q}_{m}(\mathbf{W}_{i})^{\top}\xi_{i}+\tilde{q}_{m}(\mathbf{W}_{i})\;.

Note that
\mathbf{X}_{i}
and
\xi_{i}
are zero-mean with the same variance, and are independent of
\mathbf{W}_{i}
. This allows us to apply
Lemma

16
: For any
\nu\in(2,3]
and
\delta>0
, we have

\displaystyle E_{\tau;i}\;=

\displaystyle\;\big|\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\tau;\delta}\big(\partial_{i}\tilde{q}_{m}(\mathbf{W}_{i})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}+\tilde{q}_{m}(\mathbf{W}_{i})\big)-h_{\tau;\delta}\big(\partial_{i}\tilde{q}_{m}(\mathbf{W}_{i})^{\top}\xi_{i}+\tilde{q}_{m}(\mathbf{W}_{i})\big)\big]\big|

(16)

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;54\,\delta^{-\nu}\,\big(\big\|\partial_{i}\tilde{q}_{m}(\mathbf{W}_{i})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}\big\|_{L_{\nu}}^{\nu}+\big\|\partial_{i}\tilde{q}_{m}(\mathbf{W}_{i})^{\top}\xi_{i}\big\|_{L_{\nu}}^{\nu}\big)\;.

Since
\xi_{i}
is a Gaussian with the same mean and variance as
\mathbf{X}_{i}
and both are independent of
\partial_{i}f(\mathbf{W}_{i})
, by Lemma
17
and noting that
\nu\leq 3
, there is an absolute constant
C^{\prime}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[|\partial_{i}\tilde{q}_{m}(\mathbf{W}_{i})^{\top}\xi_{i}|^{\nu}\big]\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime}\,\big\|\partial_{i}\tilde{q}_{m}(\mathbf{W}_{i})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}\big\|_{L_{\nu}}^{\nu}\;.

By additionally noting that
M_{\nu;i}=\sigma\|\partial_{i}\tilde{q}_{m}(\mathbf{W}_{i})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}\|_{L_{\nu}}
by definition, we get that

\displaystyle E_{\tau;i}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;54(C^{\prime}+1)\,\delta^{-\nu}\sigma^{-\nu}M_{\nu;i}^{\nu}\;.

Summing over
i=1,\ldots,n
then gives

\displaystyle E_{\tau}

\displaystyle\;\leq\;\msum_{i=1}^{n}E_{\tau;i}r\;\leq\;54(C^{\prime}+1)\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sum_{i=1}^n M_{\nu;i}^\nu}{ \delta^\nu\sigma^\nu}$}}\;.

(17)

On the other hand, by Lemma
18
, there exists an absolute constant
C^{*}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(t-\delta<\tilde{q}_{m}(\Xi)<

\displaystyle t+\delta)\;\leq\;C^{*}m\,\delta^{1/m}(\mathbb{E}[|\tilde{q}_{m}(\Xi)-t|^{2}])^{-1/2m}

\displaystyle\;\overset{(a)}{=}

\displaystyle\;C^{*}m\,\delta^{1/m}(\sigma^{-2}\text{\rm Var}[q_{m}(\Xi)]+t^{2})^{-1/2m}\;\overset{(b)}{=}\;C^{*}m\,\delta^{1/m}(1+t^{2})^{-1/2m}\;.

(18)

In
(a)
and
(b)
, we have used that
\mathbb{E}[f(\Xi)]=0
and
\text{\rm Var}[f(\Xi)]=\text{\rm Var}[f(\mathbf{X})]=\sigma^{2}
. Substituting (
17
) and (
18
) into (
15
), we get that there exists some absolute constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{P}(\sigma^{-1}q_{m}(\mathbf{X})\leq t)-\mathbb{P}(\sigma^{-1}q_{m}(\Xi)\leq t)\big|\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{C m}{2}$}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\delta^{1/m}}{(1 + t^2)^{1/2m}}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sum_{i=1}^n M_{\nu;i}^\nu}{ \delta^\nu\sigma^\nu}$}}\Big)\;.

Finally by choosing
\delta=(1+t^{2})^{\frac{1}{2+2\nu m}}\big(\sigma^{-\nu}\sum_{i=1}^{n}M_{\nu;i}^{\nu}\big)^{\frac{m}{\nu m+1}}
, we get the desired bound

\big|\mathbb{P}(\sigma^{-1}f(\mathbf{X})\leq t)-\mathbb{P}(\sigma^{-1}f(\Xi)\leq t)\big|\;\leq\;Cm\,\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \sum_{i=1}^n M_{\nu;i}^\nu}{ (1+t^2)^{\nu/2} \sigma^\nu}$}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{\nu m+1}}\;.\qed

Constructions for Theorem
3

The key ingredient of the proof of
Theorem

3
is a suitable sequence of heavy-tailed probability measures
\mu^{(n)}_{\nu,\sigma_{0}}
and statistic
p^{*}_{m}
. Before stating the exact constructions, we first motivate them by discussing our proof technique.

Adapting an asymmetry argument from Senatov
[
54
]
.
The overall idea is to construct a mixture of an average of a heavy-tailed random variable, which is poorly approximated via Gaussian universality, and a degree-
m
V-statistic of Gaussians, which has good approximation by the same V-statistic of an i.i.d. copy of Gaussians at small
m
but poor approximation at large
m
. The technical steps to obtain a tight lower bound, however, are non-trivial. Our strategy is inspired by Example 9.1.3 of Senatov
[
54
]
: They construct a sequence of probability measures on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
to demonstrate how a multivariate normal approximation bound on a chosen sequence of Euclidean balls may depend on eigenvalues of the covariance matrix. Two key mathematical ingredients of their work are

(i)

a heavy-tailed univariate variable, whose i.i.d. average is poorly approximated by a Gaussian, and

(ii)

an asymmetry argument by considering Euclidean balls not centred at the origin while studying averages of mean-zero random vectors.

To adapt the construction in
[
54
]
for our problem, we make one key observation: The probability of a mean-zero average lying in a radius-
r
Euclidean ball centred at the origin is exactly the probability of a degree-2 V-statistic taking values in
[0,r^{2})
. In other words, (i) gives the heavy-tailed variable we need, and (ii) is almost our V-statistic except that the Euclidean balls in Senatov
[
54
]
are not centred at the origin. Unfortunately, while the asymmetry in (ii) is key to the proof by Senatov
[
54
]
, it breaks the connection to V-statistics, and a naive application of Senatov’s results gives very loose bounds. Instead, we create a different asymmetry by asking
p^{*}_{m}(X)
to be a mixture of an odd-degree V-statistic and an even-degree V-statistic, which allows Senatov’s argument to be adapted. An additional distinction of our proof from
[
54
]
is that, to accommodate a growing degree
m
, we need to derive finer moment controls.

We are ready to state the constructions. For
m=m(n)
even, consider the polynomial

\displaystyle p^{*}_{m}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{n}}$}}\msum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i1}+\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{n}}$}}\msum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i_{j}2}\Big)^{m}\;\;\text{ for }x_{i}=(x_{i1},x_{i2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\;.

The distribution
\mu^{(n)}_{\nu,\sigma_{0}}
is the law of a bivariate random vector with a heavy-tailed coordinate and a Gaussian coordinate. We first construct the heavy-tailed variable.

A heavy-tailed univariate distribution
\tilde{\mu}_{\nu,\sigma}
.
For
\nu>2
and
\sigma\in(0,1]
, we define

\displaystyle p\;=

\displaystyle\;\sigma^{2\nu/(\nu-2)}\in(0,1]

and

\displaystyle x_{0}\;=

\displaystyle\;\sigma/\sqrt{6p}\;=\;x\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{6}}$}}\sigma^{-\frac{2}{\nu-2}}\;,

and let
U_{1}
be a discrete random variable taking values in
\{-x_{0},0,2x_{0}\}
with

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(U_{1}=-x_{0})\;=\;2p\;,\qquad\mathbb{P}(U_{1}=0)\;=\;1-3p\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathbb{P}(U_{1}=2x_{0})\;=\;p\;.

Let
Z_{1}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
and define a smoothed version of
U
as

\displaystyle V_{1}\;\coloneqq\;2^{-1/2}U_{1}+2^{-1/2}Z_{1}\;.

Note that the only role
Z_{1}
plays in the proof is for
V_{1}
to have a continuous density function, which allows us to apply results relating distribution functions to characteristic functions.
Write
\tilde{\mu}_{\nu,\sigma}
as the law of
V_{1}
. When
\nu=3
, this is the heavy-tailed distribution constructed in Example 9.1.3 of
[
54
]
. Roughly speaking, the construction is such that if we set
\sigma=\sigma_{n}\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, then
\text{\rm Var}[V_{1}]=\sigma_{n}^{2}\rightarrow 0
, but the
\nu
-th central moment of
V_{1}
remains
\Theta(1)
.

Let
V_{2},\ldots,V_{n}\overset{i.i.d.}{\sim}\tilde{\mu}_{\nu,\sigma}
and draw an independent
Z^{\prime}_{1}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
. We now list three results on
\tilde{\mu}_{\nu,\sigma}
, which extend the results by
[
54
]
to a general
\nu
and admit similar proofs. Since the original proofs are highly condensed, we include proofs for all lemmas with more detailed steps and intuitions in
Section

B.2
.
Lemma

19
below controls moments of
V_{1}
. In particular,
\mathbb{E}|V_{1}|^{\nu}=O(1)
and the upper bound on
\mathbb{E}|V_{1}|^{\omega}
diverges for
\omega>\nu
. This upper bound will allow us to handle moments of polynomials of
V_{i}
’s of growing degrees.

Lemma 19
.

\mathbb{E}\,V_{1}=0
and
\text{\rm Var}\,V_{1}=\sigma^{2}
. Moreover, there exist absolute constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
such that for all
\omega\geq 1
,
\mathbb{E}|V_{1}|^{\omega}\leq c_{1}^{\omega}\sigma^{-\frac{2(\omega-\nu)}{\nu-2}}+c_{2}^{\omega}\sigma^{\omega}\omega^{\omega/2}
.

Lemma

20
below provides a finer control on the normal approximation error of an empirical average, by performing a higher-order Taylor expansion in the space of characteristic functions. This higher-order Taylor term is then inverted back to the space of distribution functions and captured by
F_{q}
below.

Lemma 20
.

There exists some absolute constant
A>0
such that, defining the function
F_{q}(x)\coloneqq\frac{A}{n^{1/2}\sigma^{\nu/(\nu-2)}}\big(1-\frac{x^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\big)e^{-x^{2}/(2\sigma^{2})}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup\nolimits_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\Big|\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{n}}$}}\msum_{i=1}^{n}V_{i}<x\Big)-\mathbb{P}(Z^{\prime}_{1}<x)-F_{q}(x)\Big|\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2}{n \sigma^{2\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}\;.

Lemma

21
below provides a finer upper bound on the normal approximation error of an average by exploiting the distribution of
V_{i}
’s we constructed.

Lemma 21
.

Suppose there exists some constant
M\geq 10
such that
\sigma^{\nu/(\nu-2)}\geq Mn^{-1/2}
and
n\geq 6M^{2}
. Then there is some constant
C_{M}>0
that depends only on
M
such that, for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{n}}$}}\msum_{i=1}^{n}V_{i}<x\Big)-\mathbb{P}(Z^{\prime}_{1}<x)\Big|\;\leq\;C_{M}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \max\{1, \sigma^3\} }{ n^{1/2} \sigma^{\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{16\sigma^{2}}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1 }{n^{3/2} x^4 \sigma^{(8-\nu)/(\nu-2)} }$}}\Big)\;.

The bivariate distribution
\mu^{(n)}_{\nu,\sigma_{0}}
.
Now for a fixed
\nu\in(2,3]
and some absolute constant
\sigma_{0}>0
, set a standard deviation parameter

\displaystyle\sigma_{n}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\min\big\{\sigma_{0}\,n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2\nu}}\,,\,1\big\}\;\in\;(0,1]\;.

With a slight abuse of notation, we consider

\displaystyle V_{1},\ldots,V_{n}\;\overset{i.i.d.}{\sim}

\displaystyle\;\tilde{\mu}_{\nu,\sigma_{n}}

and

\displaystyle Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{n}\;\overset{i.i.d.}{\sim}

\displaystyle\;\mathcal{N}(0,1)\;,

and define
\mu^{(n)}_{\nu,\sigma_{0}}
as the law of
(V_{1},Y_{1})
.

In summary, the statistic we consider is a mixture of a heavy-tailed average and an even-degree V-statistic of Gaussian random variables:

\displaystyle p^{*}_{m}(X)\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{n}}$}}\msum_{i=1}^{n}V_{i}+\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{n}}$}}\msum_{i=1}^{n}Y_{i}\Big)^{m}\;=\;v^{*}_{1}(V)+v^{*}_{m}(Y)\;,

where we have denoted
V\coloneqq(V_{i})_{i\leq n}
,
Y\coloneqq(Y_{i})_{i\leq n}
and the rescaled degree-
m^{\prime}
V-statistic as

\displaystyle v^{*}_{m^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,x^{\prime}_{n})\;\coloneqq\;\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{n}}$}}\msum_{i=1}^{n}x^{\prime}_{i}\Big)^{m^{\prime}}\quad\text{ for }x^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,x^{\prime}_{n}\in\mathbb{R}\;.

Proof for Theorem
3

Recall that
q^{*}_{m}
is the multilinear representation of
p^{*}_{m}
with respect to
X
. The proof consists of three main steps:

(i)

By carefully exploiting the heavy tail of
V_{i}
’s and the asymmetry of
v^{*}_{m^{\prime}}
, we can obtain the first lower bound for the approximation of
p^{*}_{m}(X)
by
p^{*}_{m}(Z)
(
Lemma

22
);

(ii)

By applying
Theorem

1
to approximate
p^{*}_{m}(X)=q^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{X})+\mathbb{E}[p^{*}_{m}(X)]
by
q^{*}_{m}(\Xi)+\mathbb{E}[p^{*}_{m}(X)]
, the upper bound involving
q^{*}_{m}
in
Theorem

3
reduces to a moment control (
Lemma

24
);

(iii)

By verifying
\delta
-regularity and modifying the argument of
Proposition

6
, we can approximate
q^{*}_{m}(\Xi)+\mathbb{E}[p^{*}_{m}(X)]
further by
p^{*}_{m}(Z)
(
Lemma

25
).

We introduce some more notation. Note that we can express
X_{i}=(V_{i},Y_{i})
and
\mathbf{X}_{i}=(V_{i},\mathbf{Y}_{i})
,
where
\mathbf{Y}_{i}=(Y_{i},\ldots,Y_{i}^{m}-\mathbb{E}[Y_{i}^{m}])
. Correspondingly, we express

\displaystyle Z_{i}\;=

\displaystyle\;(Z_{V;i},Z_{Y;i})

where

\displaystyle Z_{V;i}\;\sim\;\mathcal{N}(0,\text{\rm Var}\,V_{i})\;\text{ and }\;

\displaystyle Z_{Y;i}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\text{\rm Var}\,Y_{i})\;,

\displaystyle\xi_{i}\;=

\displaystyle\;(\xi_{V;i},\xi_{\mathbf{Y};i})

where

\displaystyle\xi_{V;i}\;\sim\;\mathcal{N}(0,\text{\rm Var}\,V_{i})\;\text{ and }\;

\displaystyle\xi_{\mathbf{Y};i}\sim\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{0},\text{\rm Var}\,\mathbf{Y}_{i})\;.

Denote the collections
Z_{V}\coloneqq(Z_{V;i})_{i\leq n}
,
Z_{Y}\coloneqq(Z_{Y;i})_{i\leq n}
and
\Xi_{\mathbf{Y}}\coloneqq(\xi_{\mathbf{Y};i})_{i\leq n}
. It is also convenient to denote
q^{*}_{v;m}
as the multilinear representation of
v^{*}_{m}
with respect to
Y
, since

\displaystyle q^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{X})\;=

\displaystyle\;v^{*}_{1}(V)+q^{*}_{v;m}(\mathbf{Y})

and

\displaystyle q^{*}_{m}(\Xi)\;\overset{d}{=}

\displaystyle\;v^{*}_{1}(Z_{V})+q^{*}_{v;m}(\Xi_{\mathbf{Y}})\;.

Lemma 22
.

Fix
\nu>2
. Assume that
m
is even with
m=o(\log n)
. Then there exist some absolute constants
c,\sigma_{0}>0
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that, for any
n\geq N
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(p^{*}_{m}(Z)<-2\sigma_{n}

\displaystyle\big)-\mathbb{P}\big(p^{*}_{m}(X)<-2\sigma_{n}\big)\;\geq\;cn^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2\nu m}}\;.

Since the second coordinate of
X_{i}
is already Gaussian, the main hurdle is to approximate the heavy-tailed average by a Gaussian. Let
F_{V}
be the c.d.f. of
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{i}
– an empirical average of the heavy-tailed coordinates – and
F_{Z}
be the c.d.f. of
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}
, where
Z_{i}
’s are i.i.d. zero-mean random variables with the same variance as
V_{1}
. Write
\varphi
be the p.d.f. of
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Y_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. Recalling that
m
is even and denoting

\displaystyle I(x)\;\coloneqq\;F_{Z}(-2\sigma_{n}-x^{m})-F_{V}(-2\sigma_{n}-x^{m})\;,

we can bound the quantity of interest as

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(p^{*}_{m}(Z)<-2\sigma_{n}\big)-\mathbb{P}\big(p^{*}_{m}(X)

\displaystyle<-2\sigma_{n}\big)\;=\;\mint_{0\leq x^{m}<\infty}I(x)\varphi(x)dx

\displaystyle\;\geq

\displaystyle\;\mint_{0\leq x^{m}\leq\kappa^{m}\sigma_{n}}I(x)\varphi(x)dx-\Big|\mint_{|x|>\kappa\sigma_{n}^{1/m}}I(x)\varphi(x)dx\Big|

\displaystyle\;\eqqcolon

\displaystyle\;J_{1}-J_{2}\;,

for some
\kappa\geq 1
to be chosen later.

Bounding
J_{1}
.
Recall that for
y\in\mathbb{R}
,
Lemma

20
provides an error bound for approximating the difference
F_{Z}(y)-F_{n}(y)
by

\displaystyle-F_{q}(y)\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{A}{n^{1/2} \sigma_n^{\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}\,\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{y^2}{\sigma_n^2}$}}-1\Big)\,e^{-y^{2}/(2\sigma_{n}^{2})}

for some absolute constant
A>0
. Therefore

\displaystyle J_{1}\;\geq

\displaystyle\;\mint_{x^{m}\leq\kappa^{m}\sigma_{n}}-F_{q}(-2\sigma_{n}-x^{m})\varphi(x)dx-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2}{n \sigma_n^{2\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}\mint_{x^{m}\leq\kappa^{m}\sigma_{n}}\varphi(x)dx

\displaystyle\;\overset{(a)}{\geq}

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{A}{n^{1/2} \sigma_n^{\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}\mint_{x^{m}\leq\sigma_{n}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(2\sigma_n+x^m)^2}{\sigma_n^2}$}}-1\Big)\,e^{-(2\sigma_{n}+x^{m})^{2}/(2\sigma_{n}^{2})}\varphi(x)dx

\displaystyle\;-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2}{n \sigma_n^{2\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}\mint_{x^{m}\leq\kappa^{m}\sigma_{n}}\varphi(x)dx

\displaystyle\;\overset{(b)}{\geq}

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3 e^{-9/2} A}{n^{1/2} \sigma_n^{\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}\mint_{x^{m}\leq\sigma_{n}}\varphi(x)dx-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2}{n \sigma_n^{2\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}\mint_{x^{m}\leq\kappa^{m}\sigma_{n}}\varphi(x)dx\;.

In
(a)
, we have restricted the first integral to a smaller ball by noting that
\kappa\geq 1
; in
(b)
, we have noted in the first integral that
4\sigma_{n}^{2}\leq(2\sigma_{n}+x^{m})^{2}\leq 9\sigma_{n}^{2}
in the domain of integration. Since both integrals involve c.d.f. of a standard normal, we obtain

\displaystyle J_{1}\;\geq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3 e^{-9/2} A}{n^{1/2} \sigma_n^{\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 \sigma_n^{1/m}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}$}}\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_n^{2/m}}{2}$}}\Big)-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4 \kappa\sigma_n^{1/m} }{n \sigma_n^{2\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}

\displaystyle\;\geq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_n^{1/m} }{ n^{1/2} \sigma_n^{\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}\Big(A_{1}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{n}^{2/m}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{A_2 \kappa}{n^{1/2} \sigma_n^{\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}\Big)

for some absolute constants
A_{1},A_{2}>0
.

Bounding
J_{2}
.
Recall that
\sigma_{n}=\min\big\{\sigma_{0}n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2\nu}}\,,\,1\big\}
, which implies

\displaystyle\sigma_{n}^{\frac{\nu}{\nu-2}}\;\geq\;\min\big\{\sigma_{0}^{\frac{\nu}{\nu-2}}n^{-1/2}\,,\,1\big\}\;\geq\;\sigma_{0}^{\frac{\nu}{\nu-2}}n^{-1/2}\;.

Suppose we choose
\sigma_{0}
such that
\sigma_{0}^{\frac{\nu}{\nu-2}}>6^{-1/2}
. Since
6(6^{-1/2})^{2}=1\leq n
, by applying
Lemma

21
with
M=6^{-1/2}
, there exists some absolute constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle J_{2}\;=

\displaystyle\;\Big|\mint_{x^{m}>\kappa^{m}\sigma_{n}}\big(F_{Z}(-2\sigma_{n}-x^{m})-F_{n}(-2\sigma_{n}-x^{m})\big)\varphi(x)dx\Big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{C}{ n^{1/2} \sigma_n^{\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}\mint_{x^{m}>\kappa^{m}\sigma_{n}}e^{-\frac{(2\sigma_{n}+x^{m})^{2}}{16\sigma_{n}^{2}}}\varphi(x)dx

\displaystyle\qquad\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{C}{n^{3/2} \sigma_n^{(8-\nu)/(\nu-2)} }$}}\mint_{x^{m}>\kappa^{m}\sigma_{n}}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{(2\sigma_n+x^m)^4}$}}\varphi(x)dx

\displaystyle\;\eqqcolon

\displaystyle\;J_{21}+J_{22}\;.

By applying a change-of-variable, we get that

\displaystyle J_{21}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{C}{ n^{1/2} \sigma_n^{\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}\mint_{x^{m}>\kappa^{m}\sigma_{n}}e^{-\frac{x^{2m}}{16\sigma_{n}^{2}}}\varphi(x)dx

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{C\sigma_n^{1/m}}{ n^{1/2} \sigma_n^{\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}\mint_{x^{m}>\kappa^{m}}e^{-\frac{x^{2m}}{16}}\varphi\big(\sigma_{n}^{1/m}x\big)dx

\displaystyle\;\overset{(a)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 C \sigma_n^{1/m}}{ n^{1/2} \sigma_n^{\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}\mint_{x>\kappa}e^{-\frac{x^{2m}}{16}}dx

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 C \sigma_n^{1/m}}{ n^{1/2} \sigma_n^{\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}\mint_{x>\kappa}(2m)\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{x}{\kappa}$}}\Big)^{2m-1}e^{-\frac{x^{2m}}{16}}dx

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{A_3 \, \sigma_n^{1/m}}{ n^{1/2} \sigma_n^{\nu/(\nu-2)} \kappa^{ 2 m - 1}}$}}

for some absolute constant
A_{3}>0
. In
(a)
, we have noted that
\varphi(x)\leq 1
and the integrand is symmetric in
x
. Similarly, we can bound

\displaystyle J_{22}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{C}{n^{3/2} \sigma_n^{(8-\nu)/(\nu-2)} }$}}\mint_{x^{m}>\kappa^{m}\sigma_{n}}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{x^{4m}}$}}\varphi(x)dx

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{C}{n^{3/2} \sigma_n^{(8-\nu)/(\nu-2)} }$}}\mint_{x^{m}>\kappa^{m}\sigma_{n}}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{x^{4m}}$}}dx

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(4m-1) C }{n^{3/2} \sigma_n^{(8-\nu)/(\nu-2)} \, \sigma_n^{4 - 1 / m} \, \kappa^{4m - 1} }$}}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{A_4 \, \sigma_n^{1/m} (4m-1) }{n^{3/2} \sigma_n^{3\nu/(\nu-2)} \kappa^{4m - 1} }$}}

for some absolute constant
A_{4}>0
.

Combining the bounds.
Combining the bounds, we get that if
\sigma_{0}^{\frac{\nu}{\nu-2}}>6^{-1/2}
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(

\displaystyle p^{*}_{m}(Z)<-2\sigma_{n}\big)-\mathbb{P}\big(p^{*}_{m}(X)<-2\sigma_{n}\big)\;\geq\;J_{1}-J_{2}

\displaystyle\;\geq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_n^{1/m}}{ n^{1/2} \sigma_n^{\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}\Bigg(A_{1}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{n}^{2/m}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{A_2 \kappa}{n^{1/2} \sigma_n^{\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{A_3}{\kappa^{2m - 1} }$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{A_4 (4m-1) }{n \,\sigma_n^{2\nu/(\nu-2)} \, \kappa^{4m - 1} }$}}\Bigg)\;.

Recall that
\sigma_{n}=\min\big\{\sigma_{0}n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2\nu}}\,,\,1\big\}
and
m=o(\log n)
. Take the absolute constant
N_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large such that

\sigma_{N_{2}}=\sigma_{0}N_{2}^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2\nu}}<1
and therefore

\displaystyle N_{2}^{1/2}\sigma^{\nu/(\nu-2)}_{N_{2}}\;=\;\sigma_{0}^{\nu/(\nu-2)}\;.

Also take the absolute constants

\kappa>2
sufficiently large such that
\frac{A_{3}}{\kappa^{2m-1}}<\frac{1}{9}A_{1}
,
\sigma_{0}>0
sufficiently large such that

\frac{A_{2}\kappa}{\sigma_{0}^{\nu/(\nu-2)}}<\frac{1}{9}A_{1}

and

\frac{A_{4}}{\sigma_{0}^{2\nu/(\nu-2)}}\frac{4m-1}{\kappa^{4m-1}}<\frac{1}{9}A_{1}
, and finally
N_{1}\geq N_{2}
sufficiently large such that
A_{1}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{N_{1}}^{2/m}}=A_{1}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{0}^{2/m}N_{1}^{-\frac{\nu-2}{m\nu}}}\geq\frac{1}{2}A_{1}
.
Then we get the desired bound that, for some absolute constant
c>0
and all
n\geq N\coloneqq N_{1}
,

\mathbb{P}\big(p^{*}_{m}(Z)<-2\sigma_{n}\big)-\mathbb{P}\big(p^{*}_{m}(X)<-2\sigma_{n}\big)\;\geq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{A_1}{6}$}}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \sigma_n^{1/m}}{ n^{1/2} \sigma_n^{\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}\;\geq\;cn^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2\nu m}}\;.\qed

To obtain the desired moment controls, we will need to use tight bounds on the moments of a univariate Gaussian. The proof of the following result is included in
Section

B.1
.

Lemma 23
.

Let
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. Then there exist some absolute constants
C,c>0
such that

(i)

for any
\nu\geq 1
,
\mathbb{E}|Z|^{\nu}\leq C\nu^{\nu/2}
;

(ii)

for any
m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
with different parities,
\text{\rm Cov}[Z^{m_{1}},Z^{m_{2}}]=0
;

(iii)

for
m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
with the same parity,
\text{\rm Cov}[Z^{m_{1}},Z^{m_{2}}]>c^{m_{1}+m_{2}}(m_{1}+m_{2})^{(m_{1}+m_{2})/2}
.

The next result controls the moment ratio arising from
Theorem

1
.

Lemma 24
.

Fix
\nu\in(2,3]
and assume that
n\geq 2m^{2}
. Then for some absolute constant
C>0
,

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \sum_{i=1}^n \big\| \partial_i \,q_m^*(\bW_i)^\top\bX_i \big\|_{L_\nu}^\nu}{ \big(\Var[p_m^*(X)]\big)^{\nu/2}
}$}}\leq C^{m}n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2}},\quad\text{ where }\mathbf{W}_{i}\coloneqq\big(\mathbf{X}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{X}_{i-1},0,\xi_{i+1},\ldots,\xi_{n}\big)\;.

First note that by independence,

\displaystyle\sigma\;=\;\sqrt{\text{\rm Var}[q_{m}^{*}(\mathbf{X})]}\;=\;\sqrt{\text{\rm Var}[v^{*}_{1}(V)]+\text{\rm Var}[q^{*}_{v;m}(\Xi_{\mathbf{Y}})]}\;\geq\;\sqrt{\text{\rm Var}[q^{*}_{v;m}(\Xi_{\mathbf{Y}})]}\;.

Next to bound the
\nu
-th moment, denote
W_{V;i}\coloneqq(V_{1},\ldots,V_{i-1},0,Z_{V;i+1},\ldots,Z_{V;n})
and
\mathbf{W}_{\mathbf{Y};i}\coloneqq(\mathbf{Y}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{Y}_{i-1},0,\xi_{\mathbf{Y};i+1},\ldots,\xi_{\mathbf{Y};n})
. By a triangle inequality, we get that

\displaystyle\big\|\partial_{i}\,q_{m}^{*}(\mathbf{W}_{i})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}\big\|_{L_{\nu}}\;=

\displaystyle\;\big\|\partial_{i}\,v^{*}_{1}(W_{V;i})V_{i}+\partial_{i}\,q^{*}_{v;m}(\mathbf{W}_{Y;i})^{\top}\mathbf{Y}_{i}\big\|_{L_{\nu}}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;n^{-1/2}\|V_{i}\|_{L_{\nu}}+\big\|\partial_{i}\,q^{*}_{v;m}(\mathbf{W}_{Y;i})^{\top}\mathbf{Y}_{i}\big\|_{L_{\nu}}\;.

Since
\nu\in(2,3]
, the quantity to control can be bounded for some absolute constant
C_{1}>0
as

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \sum_{i=1}^n \big\| \partial_i \,q_m^*(\bW_i)^\top\bX_i \big\|_{L_\nu}^\nu}{ \big(\Var[p_m^*(X)]\big)^{\nu/2}
}$}}\;\overset{(a)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ 4 n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2}} \| V_1 \|_{L_\nu} + 4 \msum_{i=1}^n \big\| \partial_i \,q^*_{v;m}(\bW_{Y;i})^\top\bY_i \big\|_{L_\nu}^\nu}{ (\Var[q^*_{v;m}(\Xi_\bY)])^{\nu/2} }$}}

\displaystyle\;\overset{(b)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ C_1 n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2}}}{ (\Var[v^*_m(Y)])^{\nu/2} }$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ 4 \msum_{i=1}^n \big\| \partial_i \,q^*_{v;m}(\bW_{Y;i})^\top\bY_i \big\|_{L_\nu}^\nu}{ (\Var[v^*_m(Y)])^{\nu/2} }$}}\;.

(19)

In
(a)
, we have used a Jensen’s inequality to note that
(a+b)^{\nu}\leq 2^{\nu-1}(a^{\nu}+b^{\nu})\leq 4(a^{\nu}+b^{\nu})
and noted that
V_{i}
’s are i.i.d.; in
(b)
, we have used the moment bound on
V_{1}
from
Lemma

19
and noted that
\text{\rm Var}[q^{*}_{v;m}(\Xi_{\mathbf{Y}})]=\text{\rm Var}[q^{*}_{v;m}(\mathbf{Y})]=\text{\rm Var}[v^{*}_{m}(Y)]
by a repeated application of
Lemma

17
and property of the multilinear representation. The first term can be controlled by using the lower bound from
Lemma

23
:

\displaystyle\text{\rm Var}[v^{*}_{m}(Y)]\;=\;\text{\rm Var}\Big[\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{n}}$}}\msum_{i=1}^{n}Y_{i}\Big)^{m}\Big]\;=\;\text{\rm Var}[Y_{1}^{m}]\;\geq\;(c_{2}^{\prime})^{m}(2m)^{m}\;\geq\;c_{2}

(20)

for some absolute constants
c^{\prime}_{2},c_{2}>0
. The second term can be controlled by applying
Lemma

8
: Since
n\geq 2m^{2}
, there exists some absolute constant
C_{3}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
\msum_{i=1}^n \big\| \partial_i \,q^*_{v;m}(\bW_{Y;i})^\top\bY_i \big\|_{L_\nu}^\nu}{ (\Var[q^*_{v;m}(\Xi_\bY)])^{\nu/2} }$}}\;\leq\;(C_{3})^{m}\,n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2}}\,\Bigg(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
\sum_{k=1}^m
\binom{n}{k}
(\alpha_\nu^*(k))^2
}{
\sum_{k=1}^m
\binom{n}{k}
(\alpha_2^*(k))^2
}$}}\Bigg)^{\frac{\nu}{2}}\;,

(21)

where

\displaystyle\alpha_{\nu}^{*}(k)\;\coloneqq\;\bigg\|\msum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}+\ldots+p_{k}=m\\
p_{1},\ldots,p_{k}\geq 1\end{subarray}}\,\mprod_{l=1}^{k}\big(Y_{l}^{p_{l}}-\mathbb{E}Y_{l}^{p_{l}}\big)\bigg\|_{L_{\nu}}\;.

We are left with bounding
\alpha_{2}^{*}(k)
and
\alpha_{\nu}^{*}(k)
. Since
Y_{i}
’s are i.i.d. standard normals, the proof is down to using the moment bounds for standard normals from
Lemma

23
. For convenience, we denote
\overline{Y_{l}^{p_{l}}}\coloneqq Y_{l}^{p_{l}}-\mathbb{E}Y_{l}^{p_{l}}
from now on. We can then express

\displaystyle(\alpha_{2}^{*}(k))^{2}\;=\;\msum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}+\ldots+p_{k}=m\\
p_{1},\ldots,p_{k}\geq 1\end{subarray}}\msum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q_{1}+\ldots+q_{k}=m\\
q_{1},\ldots,q_{k}\geq 1\end{subarray}}\,\text{\rm Cov}\bigg[\mprod_{l=1}^{k}\overline{Y_{l}^{p_{l}}}\,,\,\mprod_{l=1}^{k}\overline{Y_{l}^{p_{l}}}\bigg]\,.

We now show by induction that for some absolute constant
c>0
,

\displaystyle\text{\rm Cov}\bigg[\mprod_{l=1}^{k}\overline{Y_{l}^{p_{l}}}\,,\,\mprod_{l=1}^{k}\overline{Y_{l}^{p_{l}}}\bigg]\;\geq\;\mprod_{l=1}^{k}\mathbb{I}_{\{p_{l}\equiv q_{l}\,(\textrm{mod }2)\}}\,c^{p_{l}+q_{l}}\,(p_{l}+q_{l})^{(p_{l}+q_{l})/2}\;.

For
k=1
, this directly holds for all
p_{l},q_{l}\in[m]
by the lower bound of
Lemma

23
. Suppose this holds for
k-1
. By the total law of covariance and noting that all variables are centred and independent,

\displaystyle\text{\rm Cov}\bigg[\mprod_{l=1}^{k}\overline{Y_{l}^{p_{l}}}\,,\,\mprod_{l=1}^{k}\overline{Y_{l}^{p_{l}}}\bigg]\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\,\text{\rm Cov}\bigg[\mprod_{l=1}^{k}\overline{Y_{l}^{p_{l}}}\,,\,\mprod_{l=1}^{k}\overline{Y_{l}^{p_{l}}}\,\bigg|\,Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{k-1}\bigg]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\overline{Y_{k}^{p_{k}}}\times\overline{Y_{k}^{q_{k}}}\Big]\,\text{\rm Cov}\bigg[\mprod_{l=1}^{k-1}\overline{Y_{l}^{p_{l}}}\,,\,\mprod_{l=1}^{k-1}\overline{Y_{l}^{p_{l}}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\;\geq

\displaystyle\;\text{\rm Cov}[Y_{k}^{p_{k}}\,,\,Y_{k}^{q_{k}}]\mprod_{l=1}^{k-1}\mathbb{I}_{\{p_{l}\equiv q_{l}\,(\textrm{mod }2)\}}\,c^{p_{l}+q_{l}}\,(p_{l}+q_{l})^{(p_{l}+q_{l})/2}

\displaystyle\;\geq

\displaystyle\;\mprod_{l=1}^{k}\mathbb{I}_{\{p_{l}\equiv q_{l}\,(\textrm{mod }2)\}}\,c^{p_{l}+q_{l}}\,(p_{l}+q_{l})^{(p_{l}+q_{l})/2}\;.

This finishes the induction, and in particular implies

\displaystyle(\alpha_{2}^{*}(k))^{2}\;\geq

\displaystyle\;c^{2m}\msum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}+\ldots+p_{k}=m\\
p_{1},\ldots,p_{k}\geq 1\end{subarray}}\msum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q_{1}+\ldots+q_{k}=m\\
q_{1},\ldots,q_{k}\geq 1\end{subarray}}\mprod_{l=1}^{k}\mathbb{I}_{\{p_{l}\equiv q_{l}\,(\textrm{mod }2)\}}\,(p_{l}+q_{l})^{(p_{l}+q_{l})/2}

\displaystyle\;\overset{(a)}{\geq}

\displaystyle\;c^{2m}\msum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}+\ldots+p_{k}=m\\
p_{1},\ldots,p_{k}\geq 1\end{subarray}}\msum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q_{1}+\ldots+q_{k}=m\\
q_{1},\ldots,q_{k}\geq 1\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\mprod_{l=1}^{k}\mathbb{I}_{\{p_{l}\equiv q_{l}\,(\textrm{mod }2)\}}\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
(p_l+q_l)^{(p_l+q_l)/2}
+
(c')^{p_l+q_l} (p_l+q_l+1)^{(p_l+q_l+1)/2}
}{2}$}}

\displaystyle\;\geq

\displaystyle\;(c^{\prime\prime})^{2m}\msum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}+\ldots+p_{k}=m\\
p_{1},\ldots,p_{k}\geq 1\end{subarray}}\msum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q_{1}+\ldots+q_{k}=m\\
q_{1},\ldots,q_{k}\geq 1\end{subarray}}(p_{l}+q_{l})^{(p_{l}+q_{l})/2}

for some absolute constant
c^{\prime\prime}>0
. In
(a)
, we have noted that

\displaystyle(p_{l}+q_{l})^{(p_{l}+q_{l})/2}\;=

\displaystyle\;(p_{l}+q_{l})^{-1/2}(p_{l}+q_{l})^{(p_{l}+q_{l}+1)/2}

\displaystyle\;\geq

\displaystyle\;\big((p_{l}+q_{l})^{-1/2}2^{-(p_{l}+q_{l}+1)/2}\big)(p_{l}+q_{l}+1)^{(p_{l}+q_{l}+1)/2}

\displaystyle\;\geq

\displaystyle\;(c^{\prime})^{p_{l}+q_{l}}(p_{l}+q_{l}+1)^{(p_{l}+q_{l}+1)/2}

for some absolute constant
c^{\prime}>0
. On the other hand, by a triangle inequality and noting that
Y_{i}
’s are i.i.d., we have

\displaystyle\big(\alpha_{\nu}^{*}(k)\big)^{2}\;=

\displaystyle\;\bigg\|\msum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}+\ldots+p_{k}=m\\
p_{1},\ldots,p_{k}\geq 1\end{subarray}}\,\mprod_{l=1}^{k}\overline{Y_{l}^{p_{l}}}\bigg\|_{L_{\nu}}^{2}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\msum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}+\ldots+p_{k}=m\\
p_{1},\ldots,p_{k}\geq 1\end{subarray}}\msum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q_{1}+\ldots+q_{k}=m\\
q_{1},\ldots,q_{k}\geq 1\end{subarray}}\Big\|\mprod_{l=1}^{k}\overline{Y_{l}^{p_{l}}}\Big\|_{L_{\nu}}\Big\|\mprod_{l=1}^{k}\overline{Y_{l}^{q_{l}}}\Big\|_{L_{\nu}}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\msum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}+\ldots+p_{k}=m\\
p_{1},\ldots,p_{k}\geq 1\end{subarray}}\msum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q_{1}+\ldots+q_{k}=m\\
q_{1},\ldots,q_{k}\geq 1\end{subarray}}\mprod_{l=1}^{k}\Big\|\overline{Y_{1}^{p_{l}}}\Big\|_{L_{\nu}}\Big\|\overline{Y_{1}^{q_{l}}}\Big\|_{L_{\nu}}\;.

By noting that
\nu\leq 3
and using the moment bound in
Lemma

23
, we get that for some absolute constants
C^{\prime},C^{\prime\prime}>0
,

\displaystyle\Big\|\overline{Y_{1}^{p}}\Big\|_{L_{\nu}}^{\nu}\Big\|\overline{Y_{1}^{q}}\Big\|_{L_{\nu}}^{\nu}\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\big[\big|Y_{1}^{p}-\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}^{p}]\big|^{\nu}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[\big|Y_{1}^{q}-\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}^{q}]\big|^{\nu}\big]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime}\mathbb{E}[|Y_{1}|^{p\nu}]\mathbb{E}[|Y_{1}|^{q\nu}]\;\leq\;C^{\prime\prime}p^{p\nu/2}q^{q\nu/2}\;\leq\;C^{\prime\prime}(p+q)^{(p+q)\nu/2}\;.

This implies that

\displaystyle\big(\alpha_{\nu}^{*}(k)\big)^{2}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}+\ldots+p_{k}=m\\
p_{1},\ldots,p_{k}\geq 1\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q_{1}+\ldots+q_{k}=m\\
q_{1},\ldots,q_{k}\geq 1\end{subarray}}\mprod_{l=1}^{k}(C^{\prime\prime})^{1/\nu}(p_{l}+q_{l})^{(p_{l}+q_{l})/2}\;\leq\;(\tilde{c})^{2m}\big(\alpha_{2}^{*}(k)\big)^{2}

for some absolute constant
\tilde{c}>0
. (
21
) then becomes

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
\msum_{i=1}^n \big\| \partial_i \,q^*_{v;m}(\bW_{Y;i})^\top\bY_i \big\|_{L_\nu}^\nu}{ (\Var[q^*_{v;m}(\Xi_\bY)])^{\nu/2} }$}}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;(C_{3})^{m}\,n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2}}\,\Bigg(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
\sum_{k=1}^m
\binom{n}{k}
(\alpha_\nu^*(k))^2
}{
\sum_{k=1}^m
\binom{n}{k}
(\alpha_2^*(k))^2
}$}}\Bigg)^{\frac{\nu}{2}}\;\leq\;(\tilde{c}^{\nu}C_{3})^{\nu m}\,n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2}}\;.

Plugging in this bound and (
20
) into (
19
), we get that

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \sum_{i=1}^n \big\| \partial_i \,q_m^*(\bW_i)^\top\bX_i \big\|_{L_\nu}^\nu}{ \big(\Var[p_m^*(X)]\big)^{\nu/2}
}$}}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ C_1 n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2}}}{ c_2^{\nu/2} }$}}+4(\tilde{c}^{\nu}C_{3})^{\nu m}\,n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2}}\;\leq\;C^{m}n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2}}

for some absolute constant
C>0
. This finishes the proof.
∎

The next result allows
q^{*}_{m}(\Xi)+\mathbb{E}[p^{*}_{m}(X)]
to be approximated by
p^{*}_{m}(Z)
.

Lemma 25
.

Let
C
and
c
be the absolute constants in the upper and lower bounds in
Lemma

23
, and assume that
m\leq\delta(\log n)(2+\max\{\log c,0\}+\max\{\log C,0\})^{-1}
for some
\delta\in[0,1)
. Also assume that
Z_{Y}
and
\Xi_{\mathbf{Y}}
are coupled such that
Z_{Y;i}=\xi_{Y;i1}
almost surely. Then there exists some absolute constant
C^{\prime}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \| q^*_m(\Xi) + \mean[p^*_m(X)] - p^*_m(Z) \|_{L_2}^2 }{\Var[q^*_m(\Xi)]}$}}\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ (C')^m }{ n^{1-\delta}}$}}\;.

We first compute the quantities from (
7
) and (
8
) in the definition of
\delta
-regularity, which can be bounded together as

\displaystyle\max_{q_{1}+\ldots+q_{j}=m\,,\,q_{l}\in\mathbb{N}}

\displaystyle\max\bigg\{\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[n^{m/2}\mprod_{l=1}^{j}Y_{l}^{q_{l}}\Big]\Big)^{2}\,,\,\text{\rm Var}\Big[n^{m/2}\mprod_{l=1}^{j}Y_{l}^{q_{l}}\Big]\bigg\}

\displaystyle\hskip-40.00006pt\;\leq\;n^{m}\max_{q_{1}+\ldots+q_{j}=m\,,\,q_{l}\in\mathbb{N}}\Big\|\mprod_{l=1}^{j}Y_{l}^{q_{l}}\Big\|_{L_{2}}^{2}\;\;=\;\;n^{m}\max_{q_{1}+\ldots+q_{j}=m\,,\,q_{l}\in\mathbb{N}}\mprod_{l=1}^{j}\|Y_{1}^{q_{l}}\|_{L_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip-40.00006pt\;\leq\;n^{m}\,\max_{q_{1}+\ldots+q_{j}=m\,,\,q_{l}\in\mathbb{N}}\,\mprod_{l=1}^{j}\big(C(2q_{l})^{q_{l}}\big)\;\;\leq\;\;n^{m}\,(2C)^{m}\max_{q_{1}+\ldots+q_{j}=m\,,\,q_{l}\in\mathbb{N}}\,\mprod_{l=1}^{j}q_{l}^{q_{l}}

for some absolute constant
C>0
. Note that we have used independence followed by
Lemma

23
again, and that we only need to bound the above for
j\in[m-1]
. We shall now perform an induction to show that

\displaystyle Q(j,m)\;\coloneqq\;\max_{q_{1}+\ldots+q_{j}=m\,,\,q_{l}\in\mathbb{N}}\,\mprod_{l=1}^{j}q_{l}^{q_{l}}\;=\;(m-j+1)^{m-j+1}\;.

This holds trivially for
j=1<m
. To prove this for
j\geq 2
and
m>j
, suppose the statement holds for all
j^{\prime}<j
and all
m^{\prime}>j^{\prime}
. By applying the pigeonhole principle,
q_{1}
can only take values in
[m-j+1]
, and

\displaystyle Q(j,m)\;=

\displaystyle\;\max_{q_{1}\in[m-j+1]}q_{1}^{q_{1}}Q(j-1,m-q_{1})

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\max_{q_{1}\in[m-j+1]}q_{1}^{q_{1}}(m-q_{1}-j+2)^{m-q_{1}-j+2}\;=\;\max_{q_{1}\in[m-j+1]}\psi_{m-j+2}(q_{1})\;,

where we have denoted
\psi_{m^{\prime}}(x)=x^{x}(m^{\prime}-x)^{m^{\prime}-x}
, defined for
x\in[0,m^{\prime}]
. Since
\psi_{m^{\prime}}(x)
is strictly convex with a minimum at
x=m^{\prime}/2
, its maximum is obtained at the boundary, so

\displaystyle Q(j,m)\;=\;\psi_{m-j+2}(1)\;=\;(m-1-j+2)^{m-1-j+2}\;=\;(m-j+1)^{m-j+1}\;.

This finishes the induction. By additionally recalling that, by using the lower bound from
Lemma

23
(as proved in
20
), we have

\displaystyle\text{\rm Var}[v^{*}_{m}(Y)]\;=\;\text{\rm Var}\Big[\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{n}}$}}\msum_{i=1}^{n}Y_{i}\Big)^{m}\Big]\;=\;\text{\rm Var}[Y_{1}^{m}]\;\geq\;(1/c)^{m}m^{m}

for some absolute constant
c>0
. This implies that

\displaystyle\max_{q_{1}+\ldots+q_{j}=m\,,\,q_{l}\in\mathbb{N}}\max\bigg\{\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[n^{m/2}\mprod_{l=1}^{j}Y_{l}^{q_{l}}\Big]\Big)^{2}\,,\,\text{\rm Var}\Big[n^{m/2}\mprod_{l=1}^{j}Y_{l}^{q_{l}}\Big]\bigg\}

\displaystyle\;\leq\;n^{m}\,(2C)^{m}\,m^{m-j+1}

\displaystyle\;\leq\;n^{m}\,(2\max\{C,1\})^{m}\,m^{m-j+1}c^{m}m^{-m}\text{\rm Var}[v^{*}_{m}(Y)]

\displaystyle\;\leq\;(4\max\{c,1\}\max\{C,1\})^{m}n^{m}m^{-j}\text{\rm Var}[v^{*}_{m}(Y)]

\displaystyle\;=\;e^{m(\log 4+\max\{\log c,0\}+\max\{\log C,0\})}n^{m}m^{-j}\text{\rm Var}[v^{*}_{m}(Y)]

\displaystyle\;<\;e^{m(2+\max\{\log c,0\}+\max\{\log C,0\})}n^{m}m^{-j}\text{\rm Var}[v^{*}_{m}(Y)]

\displaystyle\;\leq\;n^{m+\delta}m^{-j}\text{\rm Var}[v^{*}_{m}(Y)]\;,

where we have used the assumption on
m
in the last line. This implies that
v^{*}_{m}
in (
6
) is
\delta
-regular with respect to
Y
. By
Lemma

7
, there is some absolute constant
C^{\prime}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \| q^*_{v;m}(\Xi_\bY) + \mean[v^*_m(Y)] - v^*_m(Z_Y) \|_{L_2}^2 }{\Var[q^*_{v;m}(\Xi_\bY)]}$}}\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ (C')^m }{ n^{1-\delta}}$}}\;.

Recall that
v^{*}_{m}(Z_{Y})-\mathbb{E}[v^{*}_{m}(Y)]=p^{*}_{m}(Z)-\mathbb{E}[p^{*}_{m}(X)]-v^{*}_{1}(Z_{V})
and, by the coupling assumption,
q^{*}_{v;m}(\Xi_{\mathbf{Y}})=q^{*}_{m}(\Xi)-v^{*}_{1}(Z_{V})
. Also, since
v^{*}_{m}(Y)=\big(n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Y_{i}\big)^{m}\overset{d}{=}Y_{1}^{m}
, by
Lemma

23
,
\text{\rm Var}[q^{*}_{v;m}(\Xi_{\mathbf{Y}})]=\text{\rm Var}[v^{*}_{m}(Y)]>c^{m}\,(2m)^{m}
for some absolute constant
c>0
. These imply the desired bound that

\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \| q^*_{v;m}(\Xi) - (p^*_m(Z) - \mean[p^*_m(X)]) \|_{L_2}^2 }{\Var[f^*(\Xi)]}$}}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \| q^*_{v;m}(\Xi_\bY) + \mean[v^*_m(Y)] - v^*_m(Z_Y) \|_{L_2}^2 }{\Var[v^*_1(Z_V)] + \Var[q^*_{v;m}(\Xi_\bY)]}$}}\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ (C')^m }{ n^{1-\delta}}$}}\;.\qed

Let
X^{\prime}
and
Y^{\prime}
be two real-valued random variables. We will make use of the following bound for controlling the influence of
Y^{\prime}
on
X^{\prime}+Y^{\prime}
.

Lemma 26
.

For any
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
and
\epsilon>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(a\leq X^{\prime}+Y^{\prime}\leq b)\;\leq\;\mathbb{P}(a-\epsilon\leq X^{\prime}\leq b+\epsilon)+\mathbb{P}(|Y^{\prime}|\geq\epsilon)\;,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(a\leq X^{\prime}+Y^{\prime}\leq b)\;\geq\;\mathbb{P}(a+\epsilon\leq X^{\prime}\leq b-\epsilon)-\mathbb{P}(|Y^{\prime}|\geq\epsilon)\;.

By conditioning on the size of
Y
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(a\leq X^{\prime}+Y^{\prime}\leq b)\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}(a\leq X^{\prime}+Y^{\prime}\leq b\,,\,|Y^{\prime}|\leq\epsilon)+\mathbb{P}(a\leq X^{\prime}\leq b\,,\,|Y^{\prime}|\geq\epsilon)

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}(a-\epsilon\leq X^{\prime}\leq b+\epsilon)+\mathbb{P}(|Y^{\prime}|\geq\epsilon)\;,

and by using the order of inclusion of events, we have the lower bound

\begin{aligned} \mathbb{P}(a\leq X^{\prime}+Y^{\prime}\leq b)\;\geq&\;\mathbb{P}(a+\epsilon\leq X^{\prime}\leq b-\epsilon\,,\,|Y^{\prime}|\leq\epsilon)\\
\;=&\;\mathbb{P}(a+\epsilon\leq X^{\prime}\leq b-\epsilon)-\mathbb{P}(|Y^{\prime}|\geq\epsilon)\;.\end{aligned}\qed

Combining the results in this section allows us to prove
Theorem

3
.

By a direct application of
Theorem

1
to
q^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{X})=p^{*}_{m}(X)-\mathbb{E}[p^{*}_{m}(X)]
followed by the moment bound computed in
Lemma

24
, we get that if
n\geq 2m^{2}
, there is an absolute constant
C^{\prime}>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup\nolimits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\big|\mathbb{P}(q^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{X})\leq t)-\mathbb{P}(q^{*}_{m}(\Xi)\leq t)\big|\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime}mn^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2\nu m+2}}\;.

(22)

Since
m=o(\log n)
, there is some absolute constant
N^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
such that the above holds for all
n\geq N^{\prime}
. Meanwhile since
m
is even, by
Lemma

22
, there are absolute constants
c^{\prime},\sigma_{0}>0
and
N^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
such that, for any
n\geq N^{\prime\prime}
,

\displaystyle\sup\nolimits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\big|\mathbb{P}(p^{*}_{m}(X)\leq t)-\mathbb{P}(p^{*}_{m}(Z)\leq t)\big|\;\geq\;c^{\prime}n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2\nu m}}\;.

(23)

We are left with applying
\delta
-regularity. WLOG assume that
Z_{Y;i}=\xi_{Y;i1}
almost surely. By
Lemma

25
, there are absolute constants
c_{*},C_{*},C_{**}>0
such that, if
m\leq\delta(\log n)(2+\max\{\log c_{*},0\}+\max\{\log C_{*},0\})^{-1}
for any
\delta\in[0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \| q^*_m(\Xi) + \mean[p^*_m(X)] - p^*_m(Z) \|_{L_2}^2 }{\Var[q^*_m(\Xi)]}$}}\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ (C_{**})^m }{ n^{1-\delta}}$}}\;.

(24)

Since
m=\log(n)
, there is some absolute constant
N^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
such that the above holds for all
n\geq N^{\prime\prime\prime}
and any fixed
\delta\in[0,1)
to be specified. Now by
Lemma

26
, for any
\epsilon>0
and
t\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(p^{*}_{m}(Z)\leq t\big)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\,\mathbb{P}\big(q^{*}_{m}(\Xi)+\mathbb{E}[p^{*}_{m}(X)]\leq t+\epsilon\big)+\mathbb{P}\big(|q^{*}_{m}(\Xi)+\mathbb{E}[p^{*}_{m}(X)]-p^{*}_{m}(Z)|\geq\epsilon)\;,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(p^{*}_{m}(Z)\leq t\big)\;\geq

\displaystyle\;\,\mathbb{P}\big(q^{*}_{m}(\Xi)+\mathbb{E}[p^{*}_{m}(X)]\leq t-\epsilon\big)-\mathbb{P}\big(|q^{*}_{m}(\Xi)+\mathbb{E}[p^{*}_{m}(X)]-p^{*}_{m}(Z)|\geq\epsilon)\;.

This implies that

\displaystyle\;\big|\mathbb{P}\big(q^{*}_{m}(\Xi)+\mathbb{E}[p^{*}_{m}(X)]\leq t\big)-\mathbb{P}\big(p^{*}_{m}(Z)\leq t\big)\big|

\displaystyle\;\;\leq\;\mathbb{P}\big(t-\epsilon\leq q^{*}_{m}(\Xi)+\mathbb{E}[p^{*}_{m}(X)]\leq t+\epsilon\big)+\mathbb{P}\big(|q^{*}_{m}(\Xi)+\mathbb{E}[p^{*}_{m}(X)]-p^{*}_{m}(Z)|\geq\epsilon)

\displaystyle\;\;\overset{(a)}{\leq}\;C_{1}m\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\epsilon}{\sqrt{\Var[q^*_m(\Xi)]}}$}}\Big)^{1/m}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\| q^*_m(\Xi) + \mean[p^*_m(X)] - p^*_m(Z) \|_{L_2}^2}{\epsilon^2}$}}

\displaystyle\;\;\overset{(b)}{\leq}\;C_{1}m\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\epsilon}{\sqrt{\Var[q^*_m(\Xi)]}}$}}\Big)^{1/m}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(C_{**})^m}{n^{1-\delta}}$}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{\Var[q^*_m(\Xi)]}}{\epsilon}$}}\Big)^{2}

for some absolute constant
C_{1}>0
. In
(a)
, we used the Carbery-Wright argument in (
18
) in the proof of
Theorem

1
for the first term, and Markov’s inequality for the second term. In
(b)
, we have applied (
24
). Choosing
\epsilon=(C_{**})^{m^{2}/(2m+1)}n^{-m(1-\delta)/(2m+1)}\sqrt{\text{\rm Var}[q^{*}_{m}(\Xi)]}
, we get that for some absolute constant
C_{***}>0
,

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{P}\big(q^{*}_{m}(\Xi)+\mathbb{E}[p^{*}_{m}(X)]\leq t\big)-\mathbb{P}\big(p^{*}_{m}(Z)\leq t\big)\big|\;\leq

\displaystyle\;(C_{1}m+1)(C_{**})^{\frac{m}{2m+1}}n^{-\frac{1-\delta}{2m+1}}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C_{***}mn^{-\frac{1-\delta}{2m+1}}\;.

(25)

Combining this with (
22
) via a triangle inequality, we get that for all
n\geq\max\{N^{\prime},N^{\prime\prime\prime}\}
,

\displaystyle\sup\nolimits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\big|\mathbb{P}(p^{*}_{m}(X)\leq t)-\mathbb{P}(p^{*}_{m}(Z)\leq t)\big|\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime\prime\prime}mn^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2\nu m+2}}+C_{***}mn^{-\frac{1-\delta}{2m+1}}\;,

whereas combining the bound with (
23
) via a reverse triangle inequality, we get that for all
n\geq\max\{N^{\prime},N^{\prime\prime}\}
,

\displaystyle\sup\nolimits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\big|\mathbb{P}\big(q^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{X})\leq t\big)-\mathbb{P}\big(q^{*}_{m}(\Xi)\leq t\big)\big|\;\geq

\displaystyle\;c^{\prime\prime}n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2\nu m}}-C_{***}mn^{-\frac{1-\delta}{2m+1}}\;.

Now choose
\delta=\frac{1}{2}
. Since
\nu\in(2,3]
and
m
is even, this implies

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1-\delta}{2m+1}$}}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{4m+2}$}}\;>\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{6 m}$}}\;\geq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\nu-2}{2\nu m}$}}\;\geq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\nu-2}{2 \nu m +2 }$}}\;,

and that the
mn^{-\frac{1-\delta}{2m+1}}=o\big((\log n)n^{-\frac{1-\delta}{2m+1}}\big)
term can be ignored. This finishes the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2

By
Lemma

26
, for any
\epsilon>0
and
t\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\sigma^{-1}f(X)>t\big)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}\big(\sigma^{-1}q_{m}(\mathbf{X})>t-\epsilon\big)+\mathbb{P}\big(\sigma^{-1}|f(X)-q_{m}(\mathbf{X})|\geq\epsilon)\;,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\sigma^{-1}f(X)>t\big)\;\geq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}\big(\sigma^{-1}q_{m}(\mathbf{X})>t+\epsilon\big)-\mathbb{P}\big(\sigma^{-1}|f(X)-q_{m}(\mathbf{X})|\geq\epsilon)\;.

By additionally expressing

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\sigma^{-1}q_{m}(\Xi)>t\big)\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}\big(\sigma^{-1}q_{m}(\Xi)>t+\epsilon\big)+\mathbb{P}\big(t\leq\sigma^{-1}q_{m}(\Xi)<t+\epsilon\big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}\big(\sigma^{-1}q_{m}(\Xi)>t-\epsilon\big)-\mathbb{P}\big(t-\epsilon<\sigma^{-1}q_{m}(\Xi)\leq t\big)\;,

we can bound

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{P}\big(\sigma^{-1}f(X)\leq t\big)-

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\sigma^{-1}q_{m}(\Xi)\leq t\big)\big|\;=\;\big|\mathbb{P}\big(\sigma^{-1}f(X)>t\big)-\mathbb{P}\big(\sigma^{-1}q_{m}(\Xi)>t\big)\big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}\big(\sigma^{-1}|f(X)-q_{m}(\mathbf{X})|\geq\epsilon)+\mathbb{P}\big(|\sigma^{-1}q_{m}(\Xi)-t|<\epsilon\big)

\displaystyle\;+\max\nolimits_{s\,\in\{+1,-1\}}\big|\mathbb{P}\big(\sigma^{-1}q_{m}(\mathbf{X})>t+s\epsilon\big)-\mathbb{P}\big(\sigma^{-1}q_{m}(\Xi)>t+s\epsilon\big)\big|

\displaystyle\;\eqqcolon

\displaystyle\;T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}\;.

By Markov’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle T_{1}\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\| f(X) - q_m(\bX) \|_{L_2}^2}{\sigma^2 \epsilon^2}$}}\;.

By the Carbery-Wright inequality from Lemma
18
and noting that
\mathbb{E}q_{m}(\mathbf{X})=0
and
\text{\rm Var}\,q_{m}(\mathbf{X})=\text{\rm Var}\,q_{m}(\Xi)
by
Theorem

1
, we have that for some absolute constant
C^{\prime}>0
,

\displaystyle T_{2}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime}m\,\epsilon^{1/m}(\mathbb{E}[(\sigma^{-1}q_{m}(\Xi)-t)^{2}])^{-1/2m}\;\leq\;C^{\prime}m\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\epsilon^{1/m}}{(1 + t^2 )^{1/2m}}$}}\;.

By
Theorem

1
, there exists some absolute constant
C^{\prime\prime}>0
such that

\displaystyle T_{3}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime\prime}m\,\max_{s\in\{+1,-1\}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \sum_{i=1}^n M_{\nu;i}^\nu}{ (1 + (t + s \epsilon)^2)^{\nu/2} \, \sigma^\nu}$}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{\nu m+1}}\;.

Combining the bounds and choosing
\epsilon=\epsilon_{t}=(\frac{\|f(X)-q_{m}(\mathbf{X})\|_{L_{2}}}{\sigma})^{2m/(2m+1)}(1+t^{2})^{1/2(2m+1)}
, we obtain the non-uniform bound: There exists some absolute constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{P}\big(\sigma^{-1}f(\mathbf{X})

\displaystyle\leq t\big)-\mathbb{P}\big(\sigma^{-1}q_{m}(\Xi)\leq t\big)\big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;Cm\Big(\,\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\| f(X) - q_m(\bX) \|_{L_2}}{\sigma(1 + t^2)^{1/2}}$}}\Big)^{\frac{2}{2m+1}}+\max_{s\in\{+1,-1\}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \sum_{i=1}^n M_{\nu;i}^\nu}{ (1 + (t + s\epsilon_t)^2)^{\nu/2} \, \sigma^\nu}$}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{\nu m+1}}\Big)\;.

Taking a supremum over
t
gives the desired bound. ∎

Moment computation for U-statistics

The proofs for
Section

2.3
and
Section

3
rely extensively on moment formula for U-statistics, which are collected next. We follow the notation of
Section

3.2
:
Y=(Y_{i})_{i\leq n}
are i.i.d. variables in a general measurable space
\mathcal{E}
, and
U_{j}^{\rm H}(Y)
and
u_{j}^{\rm H}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{j})
are defined as in Hoeffding’s decomposition (
11
) of
u_{m}
in (
10
). The next result states that Hoeffding decompositions of different orders are orthogonal in
L_{2}
.

Lemma 27

(Lemma 1.2.3 of
[
20
]
)
.

Fix some
j\neq j^{\prime}\in[m]
. Suppose
\|U_{j}^{\rm H}(Y)\|_{L_{2}}
and
\|U_{j^{\prime}}^{\rm H}(Y)\|_{L_{2}}
are bounded. Then
\mathbb{E}[U_{j}^{\rm H}(Y)U_{j^{\prime}}^{\rm H}(Y)]=0
.

Denote
\sigma^{2}_{r}\coloneqq\text{\rm Var}\,\mathbb{E}[u(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{m})\,|\,Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{r}]
, where
u
is the kernel of
u_{m}
in (
10
). The next two results compute the variances associated with
u^{\rm H}_{j}
,
U^{\rm H}_{j}
and
u_{m}
.

Lemma 28

(Lemma 1.2.4 of
[
20
]
)
.

For each
j\in[m]
, we have

\displaystyle\text{\rm Var}\big[u_{j}^{\rm H}(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{j})\big]\;=

\displaystyle\;\msum_{r=1}^{j}\,\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{j}{j-r}$}}\Bigr)\,(-1)^{j-r}\sigma_{r}^{2}\;.

Lemma 29

(Theorem 1.2.2 of
[
20
]
)
.

We have that

\displaystyle\text{\rm Var}\big[U_{j}^{\rm H}(Y)\big]\;=

\displaystyle\;\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{n}{j}$}}\Bigr)^{-1}\sigma_{j}^{2}

and

\displaystyle\text{\rm Var}[u_{m}(Y)]\;=

\displaystyle\;\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{n}{m}$}}\Bigr)^{-1}\msum_{r=1}^{m}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{m}{r}$}}\Bigr)\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{n-m}{m-r}$}}\Bigr)\sigma^{2}_{r}\;.

Proofs for Section
2.3

The proofs for
\delta
-regularity are mathematically straightforward but tedious, as it involves expanding out a degree-
m
V-statistic and comparing moment terms of different orders. Throughout, we express
\xi_{i}=(\xi_{i1},\ldots,\xi_{im})
, where each
\xi_{ij}
matches
\overline{X_{i}^{\otimes j}}=X_{i}^{\otimes j}-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{\otimes j}]
in mean and variance. We require two auxiliary results. The first one expresses a V-statistic as a sum of U-statistics:

Lemma 30

(Theorem 1, p.183,
[
40
]
)
.

Given a function
u:(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n^m}$}}\msum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{m}\in[n]}u(y_{i_{1}},\ldots,y_{i_{m}})\;=

\displaystyle\;n^{-m}\msum_{j=1}^{m}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{n}{j}$}}\Bigr)\tilde{u}_{j;m}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{n})\;,

where
\tilde{u}_{j;m}
is a degree-
j
U-statistic defined by

\displaystyle\tilde{u}_{j;m}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{n})\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n(n-1) \ldots(n-j+1)}$}}\msum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{j}\in[n]\text{ distinct}}u_{j;m}(y_{i_{1}},\ldots,y_{i_{j}})

\displaystyle u_{j;m}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{j})\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\msum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(p_{1},\ldots,p_{m})\in[j]^{m}\\
\text{with exactly $j$ distinct elements}\end{subarray}}u(y_{p_{1}},\ldots,y_{p_{m}})\;.

The next result controls the moment of a martingale, and is also used in
Section

10
to prove results for
Section

3
. The original result is due to Burkholder
[
10
]
.
[
62
]
and
[
21
]
provides a further upper bound to Burkholder’s result with an explicit constant
C_{\nu}
, and Lemma 26 of
[
35
]
provides a further lower bound to Burkholder’s result; these are summarised by the first statement of
Lemma

31
. We also include Burkholder’s original upper bound, which is used when a finer control is required. The boundedness of
C^{\prime}_{\nu}
over a bounded interval is because
[
10
]
’s constant arises from Marcinkiewicz interpolation theorem and the Khintchine inequality.

Lemma 31
.

Fix
\nu>1
. For a martingale difference sequence
Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{n}
taking values in
\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle c_{\nu}\,n^{\min\{0,\,\nu/2-1\}}\msum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[|Y_{i}|^{\nu}]\;\leq\;\mathbb{E}\big[\big|\msum_{i=1}^{n}Y_{i}\big|^{\nu}\big]\;\leq\;C_{\nu}\,n^{\max\{0,\,\nu/2-1\}}\msum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[|Y_{i}|^{\nu}]\;,

for
C_{\nu}\coloneqq\max\big\{2,(8(\nu-1)\max\{1,2^{\nu-3}\})^{\nu}\big\}
and a constant
c_{\nu}>0
depending only on
\nu
. Moreover, there exists some constant
C^{\prime}_{\nu}>0
depending only on
\nu
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big|\msum_{i=1}^{n}Y_{i}\big|^{\nu}\big]\;\leq\;C^{\prime}_{\nu}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|\msum_{i=1}^{n}Y_{i}^{2}\big|^{\nu/2}\Big]\;,

where
\sup\nolimits_{\nu\in I}C^{\prime}_{\nu}
is bounded whenever
I\subset(1,\infty)
is a fixed bounded interval.

Proof of Lemma
7

By
Lemma

30
, we have

\displaystyle v_{m}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\;=

\displaystyle\;n^{-m}\msum_{j=1}^{m}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{n}{j}$}}\Bigr)\tilde{u}_{j;m}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\;,

where

\displaystyle\tilde{u}_{j;m}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n(n-1) \ldots(n-j+1)}$}}\msum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{j}\in[n]\text{ distinct}}u_{j;m}(x_{i_{1}},\ldots,x_{i_{j}})

\displaystyle u_{j;m}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{j})\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\msum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(p_{1},\ldots,p_{m})\in[j]^{m}\\
\text{with exactly $j$ distinct elements}\end{subarray}}\langle S,\,x_{p_{1}}\otimes\ldots\otimes x_{p_{m}}\rangle\;.

By the symmetry of
S
, we can express

\displaystyle u_{j;m}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{j})\;=\;\msum_{q_{1}+\ldots+q_{j}=m\,,\,q_{l}\in\mathbb{N}}\,\langle S,\,x_{1}^{\otimes q_{1}}\otimes\ldots\otimes x_{j}^{\otimes q_{j}}\rangle

\displaystyle\;=\;\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q_{1}+\ldots+q_{j}=m\\
q_{l}\in\mathbb{N}\end{subarray}}\,\langle S,\,\big(x_{1}^{\otimes q_{1}}-\mathbb{E}[X_{1}^{\otimes q_{1}}]+\mathbb{E}[X_{1}^{\otimes q_{1}}]\big)\otimes\ldots\otimes\big(x_{j}^{\otimes q_{j}}-\mathbb{E}[X_{1}^{\otimes q_{j}}]+\mathbb{E}[X_{1}^{\otimes q_{j}}]\big)\rangle\;.

Analogously, we can express, for
y_{i}=(y_{i1},\ldots,y_{im})
with
y_{ij}\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{j}}
,

\displaystyle q^{v}_{m}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{n})+\mathbb{E}[v_{m}(X)]\;=

\displaystyle\;n^{-m}\msum_{j=1}^{m}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{n}{j}$}}\Bigr)\tilde{u}^{q}_{j;m}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{n})\;,

where

\displaystyle\tilde{u}^{q}_{j;m}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{n})\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n(n-1) \ldots(n-j+1)}$}}\msum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{j}\in[n]\text{ distinct}}u_{j;m}^{q}(y_{i_{1}},\ldots,y_{i_{j}})

\displaystyle u^{q}_{j;m}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{j})\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\msum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q_{1}+\ldots+q_{j}=m\\
q_{l}\in\mathbb{N}\end{subarray}}\,\big\langle S,\,\big(y_{1q_{1}}+\mathbb{E}[X_{1}^{\otimes q_{1}}]\big)\otimes\ldots\otimes\big(y_{jq_{j}}+\mathbb{E}[X_{1}^{\otimes q_{j}}]\big)\big\rangle\;.

This allows us to express the difference of interest as a sum of centred U-statistics:

\displaystyle\|q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)+\mathbb{E}[v_{m}(X)]-v_{m}(Z)\|_{L_{2}}^{2}\;=

\displaystyle\;\Big\|n^{-m}\msum_{j=1}^{m}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{n}{j}$}}\Bigr)\tilde{u}^{\Delta}_{j;m}(\Xi)\Big\|_{L_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\Big(n^{-m}\msum_{j=1}^{m}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{n}{j}$}}\Bigr)\,\big\|\tilde{u}^{\Delta}_{j;m}(\Xi)\big\|_{L_{2}}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;m\,n^{-2m}\msum_{j=1}^{m}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{n}{j}$}}\Bigr)^{2}\,\big\|\tilde{u}^{\Delta}_{j;m}(\Xi)\big\|_{L_{2}}^{2}\;,

where we have noted the coupling
\xi_{i1}=Z_{i}-\mathbb{E}Z_{i}
a.s. and defined the degree-
j
U-statistic

\displaystyle\tilde{u}^{\Delta}_{j;m}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{n})\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n(n-1) \ldots(n-j+1)}$}}\msum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{j}\in[n]\text{ distinct}}u_{j;m}^{\Delta}(y_{i_{1}},\ldots,y_{i_{j}})\;,

\displaystyle u_{j;m}^{\Delta}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{j})\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\msum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q_{1}+\ldots+q_{j}=m\\
q_{l}\in\mathbb{N}\end{subarray}}\Big(\big\langle S,\,\big(y_{1q_{1}}+\mathbb{E}[X_{1}^{\otimes q_{1}}]\big)\otimes\ldots\otimes\big(y_{jq_{j}}+\mathbb{E}[X_{1}^{\otimes q_{j}}]\big)\big\rangle

\displaystyle\;\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad-\big\langle S,\,y_{11}^{\otimes q_{1}}\otimes\ldots\otimes y_{j1}^{\otimes q_{j}}\big\rangle\Big)\;.

The task now is to control
\|\tilde{u}^{\Delta}_{j;m}(\Xi)\|_{L_{2}}
. By the variance formula in
Lemma

29
, we have that

\displaystyle\|\tilde{u}^{\Delta}_{j;m}

\displaystyle(\Xi)\|_{L_{2}}^{2}\;=\;\big(\mathbb{E}\big[\tilde{u}^{\Delta}_{j;m}(\Xi)\big]\big)^{2}+\text{\rm Var}\big[\tilde{u}^{\Delta}_{j;m}(\Xi)\big]

\displaystyle\;=\;\big(\mathbb{E}\big[u^{\Delta}_{j;m}(\Xi)\big]\big)^{2}+\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{n}{j}$}}\Bigr)^{-1}\msum_{r=1}^{j}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{j}{r}$}}\Bigr)\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{n-j}{j-r}$}}\Bigr)\,\text{\rm Var}\,\mathbb{E}\big[u_{j;m}^{\Delta}(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{j})\,\big|\,\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{r}\big]\;.

To compute the moments of
u^{\Delta}_{j;m}
, first note that we can express

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[u_{j;m}^{\Delta}(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{j})\,\big|

\displaystyle\,\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{r}\big]

(26)

\displaystyle\;=\;\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q_{1}+\ldots+q_{j}=m\\
q_{l}\in\mathbb{N}\end{subarray}}\bigg(\bigg\langle S\,,\,\bigotimes_{l=1}^{r}\big(\xi_{lq_{l}}+\mathbb{E}[X_{1}^{\otimes q_{l}}]\big)\otimes\bigotimes_{l^{\prime}=r+1}^{j}\mathbb{E}[X_{1}^{\otimes q_{l^{\prime}}}]\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle\;\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\bigg\langle S\,,\,\bigotimes_{l=1}^{r}\xi_{l1}^{\otimes q_{l}}\otimes\bigotimes_{l^{\prime}=r+1}^{j}\mathbb{E}\big[\xi_{11}^{\otimes q_{l^{\prime}}}\big]\bigg\rangle\bigg)\;.

Meanwhile, recall that
\xi_{i1}=Z_{1}
almost surely,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\xi_{i1}]\;=\;\mathbb{E}[X_{1}]\;=\;0\;,

and, since
\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{\otimes 2}]=\mathbb{E}[Z_{i}^{\otimes 2}]=\mathbb{E}[\xi_{i1}^{\otimes 2}]
and
\mathbb{E}[\xi_{i2}]=0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\xi_{i2}+\mathbb{E}\big[X_{1}^{\otimes 2}\big]\big]-\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\xi_{i1}+\mathbb{E}[X_{1}]\big)^{\otimes 2}\Big]\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\big[X_{1}^{\otimes 2}\big]-(Z_{1}-\mathbb{E}[Z_{1}])^{\otimes 2}-\mathbb{E}[X_{1}]^{\otimes 2}\Big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;0\;.

This implies that the summand in (
26
) vanishes if

(i)

q_{l^{\prime}}=1
for any
l=r+1,\ldots,j
, or

(ii)

q_{l}=1
for all
l=1,\ldots,r
and
q_{l^{\prime}}=2
for all
l=r+1,\ldots,j
.

For (
26
) to be non-zero, (i) and (ii) imply that
r+2(j-r)<m
by the pigeonhole principle. In other words,
r\geq 2j-m+1
, which allows us to rewrite

\displaystyle\text{\rm Var}\big[\tilde{u}^{\Delta}_{j;m}(\Xi)\big]\;=

\displaystyle\;\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{n}{j}$}}\Bigr)^{-1}\msum_{r=2j-m+1}^{j}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{j}{r}$}}\Bigr)\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{n-j}{j-r}$}}\Bigr)\,\text{\rm Var}\,\mathbb{E}\big[u_{j;m}^{\Delta}(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{j})\,\big|\,\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{r}\big]\;,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[u^{\Delta}_{j;m}(\Xi)\big]\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\big[u^{\Delta}_{j;m}(\Xi)\big]\;\mathbb{I}_{\{j\leq\lfloor\frac{m-1}{2}\rfloor\}}\;.

Therefore by a Jensen’s inequality combined with the above bound, we get that

\displaystyle\|q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}[v_{m}(X)]-v_{m}(Z)\|_{L_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{m}{n^{2m}}$}}\,\msum_{j=1}^{\lfloor\frac{m-1}{2}\rfloor}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{n}{j}$}}\Bigr)^{2}\,\big(\mathbb{E}\big[u^{\Delta}_{j;m}(\Xi)\big]\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{m}{n^{2m}}$}}\,\msum_{j=1}^{m}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{n}{j}$}}\Bigr)\msum_{r=2j-m+1}^{j}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{j}{r}$}}\Bigr)\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{n-j}{j-r}$}}\Bigr)\,\text{\rm Var}\,\mathbb{E}\big[u_{j;m}^{\Delta}(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{j})\,\big|\,\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{r}\big]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{m}{n^{2m}}$}}\,\msum_{j=1}^{\lfloor\frac{m-1}{2}\rfloor}\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{e^{2j} n^{2j}}{j^{2j}}$}}\,\big(\mathbb{E}\big[u^{\Delta}_{j;m}(\Xi)\big]\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{m}{n^{2m}}$}}\,\msum_{j=1}^{m-1}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{e^j n^j}{j^j}$}}\msum_{r=2j-m+1}^{j}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{j}{r}$}}\Bigr)n^{m-1-j}\,\text{\rm Var}\big[u_{j;m}^{\Delta}(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{j})\big]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{m}{n^{2m}}$}}\,\msum_{j=1}^{\lfloor\frac{m-1}{2}\rfloor}\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{e^{2j} n^{2j}}{j^{2j}}$}}\,\big(\mathbb{E}\big[u^{\Delta}_{j;m}(\Xi)\big]\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{m}{n^{m+1}}$}}\,\msum_{j=1}^{m-1}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(2e)^j}{j^j}$}}\,\text{\rm Var}\big[u_{j;m}^{\Delta}(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{j})\big]\;.

(27)

To get a further control on the moment terms, note that

\displaystyle\text{\rm Var}\big[u_{j;m}^{\Delta}(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{j})\big]\;=

\displaystyle\;\text{\rm Var}\big[u_{j;m}^{q}(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{j})-u_{j;m}(\xi_{11},\ldots,\xi_{j1})\big]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;2\,\text{\rm Var}\,\big[u_{j;m}^{q}(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{j})\big]+2\,\text{\rm Var}\,\big[u_{j;m}(\xi_{11},\ldots,\xi_{j1})\big]\;,

and similarly

\displaystyle\big(\mathbb{E}\big[u^{\Delta}_{j;m}(\Xi)\big]\big)^{2}\;\leq\;2\big(\mathbb{E}\big[u^{q}_{j;m}(\Xi)\big]\big)^{2}+2\big(\mathbb{E}\big[u_{j;m}(\Xi)\big]\big)^{2}\;.

Meanwhile, note that for any
j\in[m-1]
,

\displaystyle\text{\rm Var}\big[u^{q}_{j;m}(\xi_{11},\ldots,\xi_{j1})\big]\;=

\displaystyle\;\text{\rm Var}\bigg[\msum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q_{1}+\ldots+q_{j}=m\\
q_{l}\in\mathbb{N}\end{subarray}}\bigg\langle S\,,\,\bigotimes_{l=1}^{j}\big(\xi_{1q_{l}}+\mathbb{E}\big[X_{1}^{\otimes q_{l}}\big]\big)\bigg\rangle\bigg]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{m-1}{j-1}$}}\Bigr)^{2}\max_{q_{1}+\ldots+q_{j}=m\,,\,q_{l}\in\mathbb{N}}\text{\rm Var}\bigg[\bigg\langle S\,,\,\bigotimes_{l=1}^{j}\big(\xi_{lq_{l}}+\mathbb{E}\big[X_{1}^{\otimes q_{l}}\big]\big)\bigg\rangle\bigg]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;m^{2j}\max_{q_{1}+\ldots+q_{j}=m\,,\,q_{l}\in\mathbb{N}}\text{\rm Var}\bigg[\bigg\langle S\,,\,\bigotimes_{l=1}^{j}\big(\xi_{lq_{l}}+\mathbb{E}\big[X_{1}^{\otimes q_{l}}\big]\big)\bigg\rangle\bigg]

\displaystyle\;\overset{(a)}{=}

\displaystyle\;m^{2j}\max_{q_{1}+\ldots+q_{j}=m\,,\,q_{l}\in\mathbb{N}}\text{\rm Var}\bigg[\bigg\langle S\,,\,\bigotimes_{l=1}^{j}X_{l}^{\otimes q_{l}}\bigg\rangle\bigg]

\displaystyle\;\overset{(b)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\;m^{2j}\big(C^{\prime}\,n^{m+\delta}m^{-j}\,\text{\rm Var}[v_{m}(X)]\big)\;=\;C^{\prime}\,m^{j}\,n^{m+\delta}\,\text{\rm Var}[q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)]

for some absolute constant
C^{\prime}>0
