, where we have used
Lemma

17
in
(a)
and
\delta
-regularity in
(b)
. Similarly by
\delta
-regularity again,

\displaystyle\text{\rm Var}\big[u_{j;m}(\xi_{11},\ldots,\xi_{j1})\big]\;\leq

\displaystyle\;m^{2j}\;\max_{q_{1}+\ldots+q_{j}=m\,,\,q_{l}\in\mathbb{N}}\text{\rm Var}\bigg[\bigg\langle S\,,\,\bigotimes_{l=1}^{j}Z_{l}^{\otimes q_{l}}\bigg\rangle\bigg]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime}\,m^{j}\,n^{m+\delta}\,\text{\rm Var}[q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)]\;,

whereas

\displaystyle\;\max\big\{\big(\mathbb{E}\big[u^{q}_{j;m}(\Xi)\big]\big)^{2}\,,\,\big(\mathbb{E}\big[u_{j;m}(\Xi)\big]\big)^{2}\big\}

\displaystyle\;\leq\;m^{2j}\,\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}q_{1}+\ldots+q_{j}=m\\
q_{l}\in\mathbb{N}\end{subarray}}\max\bigg\{\bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg\langle S\,,\,\bigotimes_{l=1}^{j}\big(\xi_{lq_{l}}+\mathbb{E}\big[X_{1}^{\otimes q_{l}}\big]\big)\bigg\rangle\bigg]\bigg)^{2}\,,\,\bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg\langle S\,,\,\bigotimes_{l=1}^{j}Z_{l}^{\otimes q_{l}}\bigg\rangle\bigg]\bigg)^{2}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\;=\;m^{2j}\,\max_{q_{1}+\ldots+q_{j}=m\,,\,q_{l}\in\mathbb{N}}\max\bigg\{\bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg\langle S\,,\,\bigotimes_{l=1}^{j}X_{1}^{\otimes q_{l}}\bigg\rangle\bigg]\bigg)^{2}\,,\,\bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg\langle S\,,\,\bigotimes_{l=1}^{j}Z_{l}^{\otimes q_{l}}\bigg\rangle\bigg]\bigg)^{2}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\;\leq\;C^{\prime}\,m^{2j}\,n^{m+\delta}\,\text{\rm Var}[q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)]\;.

Substituting these bounds into (
27
), we get that

\displaystyle\|q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)+

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[v_{m}(X)]-v_{m}(Z)\|_{L_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;4C^{\prime}\,m\,n^{-2m}\,\msum_{j=1}^{\lfloor\frac{m-1}{2}\rfloor}\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{e^{2j} n^{2j + m + \delta} m^{2j}}{j^{2j}}$}}\,\text{\rm Var}[q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)]

\displaystyle\;+4C^{\prime}\,m\,n^{-m-1}\,\msum_{j=1}^{m-1}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ (2e)^j m^j n^{m+\delta}}{j^j}$}}\,\text{\rm Var}[q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;4C^{\prime}\,m\,n^{-(1-\delta)}\,\text{\rm Var}[q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)]\Big(\msum_{j=1}^{\lfloor\frac{m-1}{2}\rfloor}\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{e^{2j} m^{2j}}{j^{2j}}$}}+\msum_{j=1}^{m-1}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ (2e)^j m^j}{j^j}$}}\Big)

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;4C^{\prime}\,m\,n^{-(1-\delta)}\,\text{\rm Var}[q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)]\Big(\msum_{j=0}^{m}e^{2j}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{m}{j}$}}\Bigr)^{2}+\msum_{j=0}^{m}(2e)^{j}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{m}{j}$}}\Bigr)\Big)

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;4C^{\prime}\,m\,n^{-(1-\delta)}\,\text{\rm Var}[q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)]\Big(\Big(\msum_{j=0}^{m}e^{j}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{m}{j}$}}\Bigr)\Big)^{2}+\msum_{j=0}^{m}(2e)^{j}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{m}{j}$}}\Bigr)\Big)

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{m}\,n^{-(1-\delta)}\,\text{\rm Var}[q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)]

for some absolute constant
C>0
. This proves the first bound. To show the second bound, we use a triangle inequality to get that

\displaystyle\text{\rm Var}[

\displaystyle v_{m}(Z)]\;=\;\|q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)+\mathbb{E}[v_{m}(X)]-v_{m}(Z)+\mathbb{E}[v_{m}(Z)]-q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)-\mathbb{E}[v_{m}(X)]\|_{L_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\big(\|q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)+\mathbb{E}[v_{m}(X)]-v_{m}(Z)\|_{L_{2}}+\|q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)\|_{L_{2}}+|\mathbb{E}[v_{m}(Z)]-\mathbb{E}[v_{m}(X)]|\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\Big(\|q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)+\mathbb{E}[v_{m}(X)]-v_{m}(Z)\|_{L_{2}}+\sqrt{\text{\rm Var}[q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)]}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\big|\mathbb{E}\big[v_{m}(Z)-q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)-\mathbb{E}[v_{m}(X)]\big]\big|\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\Big(\,2\|q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)+\mathbb{E}[v_{m}(X)]-v_{m}(Z)\|_{L_{2}}+\sqrt{\text{\rm Var}[q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)]}\;\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\text{\rm Var}[q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)]\big(1+(C_{*})^{m}n^{-(1-\delta)/2}\big)^{2}\;=\;\text{\rm Var}[v_{m}(X)]\big(1+(C_{*})^{m}n^{-(1-\delta)/2}\big)^{2}\;,

where we have written
(C_{*})^{m}=2C^{m/2}
. Similarly by a reverse triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\hskip 60.00009pt\text{\rm Var}[v_{m}(Z)]\;\geq

\displaystyle\;\big(\sqrt{\text{\rm Var}[q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)]}-2\big\|q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)+\mathbb{E}[v_{m}(X)]-v_{m}(Z)\big\|_{L_{2}}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\;\geq

\displaystyle\;\text{\rm Var}[v_{m}(X)]\big(1-(C_{*})^{m}n^{-(1-\delta)/2}\big)^{2}\;.\hskip 80.00012pt\qed

Proof of Lemma
8

The first result holds directly by
Theorem

1
: There exists an absolute constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup\nolimits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\big|\mathbb{P}(\sigma^{-1}q^{v}_{m}(\mathbf{X})\leq t)-\mathbb{P}(\sigma^{-1}q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)\leq t)\big|\;\leq

\displaystyle\;Cm\,\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \sum_{i=1}^n M_{\nu;i}^\nu}{ \sigma^\nu}$}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{\nu m+1}}\;.

(28)

We now show that, if
n\geq 2m^{2}
, there exists some absolute constant
C^{\prime}>0
such that

\displaystyle\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \sum_{i=1}^n M_{\nu;i}^\nu}{ \sigma^\nu}$}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{\nu m+1}}\;\leq\;C^{\prime}\,n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2\nu m+2}}\,\Bigg(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
\sum_{k=1}^m
\binom{n}{k}
(\alpha_\nu(S, k))^2
}{
\sum_{k=1}^m
\binom{n}{k}
(\alpha_2(S,k))^2
}$}}\Bigg)^{\frac{\nu}{2\nu m+2}}\;.

(29)

We proceed by induction. For
i\leq n
and
\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{1}\subseteq[n]
with
|\mathcal{I}_{1}|\leq m
and
\mathcal{I}_{0}\cap\mathcal{I}_{1}=\emptyset
, define

\displaystyle T(i,\mathcal{I}_{0},

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1})\;\coloneqq\;

\displaystyle\msum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}+\ldots+p_{n}=m\\
0\leq p_{l}\leq m\;\;\forall\,l\in[n]\\
p_{l^{\prime}}\geq 1\;\;\forall\,l^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{1}\\
p_{l^{\prime}}=0\;\;\forall\,l^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{0}\end{subarray}}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{m}{p_1 \, \ldots\, p_n}$}}\Bigr)\Big\langle S\,,\,\overline{X_{1}^{\otimes p_{1}}}\otimes\ldots\otimes\overline{X_{i}^{\otimes p_{i}}}\otimes\xi_{(i+1)p_{i+1}}\otimes\ldots\otimes\xi_{np_{n}}\Big\rangle\;,

where
\binom{m}{p_{1}\,\ldots\,p_{n}}\coloneqq\frac{m!}{p_{1}!\ldots p_{n}!}
is the multinomial coefficient. The quantities of interest become

\displaystyle\sigma\;=\;\sqrt{\text{\rm Var}[v_{m}(X)]}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \big\| T(n, \emptyset, \emptyset) \big\|_{L_2}}{n^m}$}}\;,\;\;\;M_{\nu;i}\;=\;\big\|\partial_{i}q^{v}_{m}(\mathbf{W}_{i})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}\big\|_{L_{\nu}}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\big\| T(i , \{i\}, \emptyset) \big\|_{L_\nu}}{n^m}$}}\;.

Write
\alpha_{\nu}(k)=\alpha_{\nu}(S,k)
for short. We first show by induction on
|\mathcal{I}_{1}|
that for
\nu\in[2,3]

\displaystyle\big\|T(i,\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{1})\big\|_{L_{\nu}}^{2}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;(C_{*})^{m}\msum_{k=0}^{m-|\mathcal{I}_{1}|}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(C'')^{k+1}}{k!}$}}(n-|\mathcal{I}_{0}|-|\mathcal{I}_{1}|+1)^{k}(\alpha_{\nu}(|\mathcal{I}_{1}|+k))^{2}\;,

(30)

\displaystyle\big\|T(i,\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{1})\big\|_{L_{2}}^{2}\;\geq

\displaystyle\;\msum_{k=0}^{m-|\mathcal{I}_{1}|}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(c'')^{k+1}}{k!}$}}(\alpha_{2}(|\mathcal{I}_{1}|+k))^{2}

(31)

\displaystyle\quad\;\;\,\times\max\big\{(n-|\mathcal{I}_{0}|-|\mathcal{I}_{1}|+1)^{k}-k^{2}(n-|\mathcal{I}_{0}|-|\mathcal{I}_{1}|+1)^{k-1},0\big\}\Big)\;.

C_{*}\geq 1
is the supremum of the constant in the upper bound of
Lemma

17
over
\nu\in[2,3]
,
C^{\prime\prime}
is the supremum of the constant in the first upper bound of
Lemma

31
over
\nu\in[2,3]
, and
c^{\prime\prime}
is the constant in the lower bound of
Lemma

31
when
\nu=2
.

For the base case, if
|\mathcal{I}_{1}|=n-|\mathcal{I}_{0}|
, i.e.
\mathcal{I}_{0}=[n]\setminus\mathcal{I}_{1}
, by using the independence of
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
and applying
Lemma

17
to replace each
\xi_{i}
by
\big(\overline{X_{i}^{\otimes 1}},\ldots,\overline{X_{i}^{\otimes m}}\big)
, we get that

\displaystyle\|T(i,\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{1})\|_{L_{2}}^{2}\;=

\displaystyle\;\bigg\|\msum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sum_{l\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}p_{l}=m\\
p_{l}\geq 1\;\;\forall\,l\in\mathcal{I}_{1}\end{subarray}}\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{m!}{\prod_{l \in\cI_1} (p_l !)}$}}\,\Big\langle S\,,\,\bigotimes_{l\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}\overline{X_{l}^{\otimes p_{l}}}\,\Big\rangle\bigg\|_{L_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\;\geq

\displaystyle\;\big(\alpha_{2}(|\mathcal{I}_{1}|)\big)^{2}\;.

By applying
Lemma

17
again with
\nu\in[2,3]
, we get that

\displaystyle\|T(i,\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{1})\|_{L_{\nu}}^{2}\leq(C_{*})^{|\mathcal{I}_{1}|}\bigg\|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sum_{l\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}p_{l}=m\\
p_{l}\geq 1\;\;\forall\,l\in\mathcal{I}_{1}\end{subarray}}\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{m!}{\prod_{l \in\cI_1} (p_l !)}$}}\,\Big\langle S\,,\,\bigotimes_{l\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}\overline{X_{l}^{\otimes p_{l}}}\,\Big\rangle\bigg\|_{L_{\nu}}^{2}\leq(C_{*})^{m}\big(\alpha_{\nu}(|\mathcal{I}_{1}|)\big)^{2}.

Meanwhile, if
|\mathcal{I}_{1}|=m
, we automatically have
|\mathcal{I}_{1}|=n-|\mathcal{I}_{0}|
, so the above also holds.

For the inductive step, fix
\mathcal{I}_{1}\subseteq[n]
with
|\mathcal{I}_{1}|<\min\{n-|\mathcal{I}_{0}|,m\}
. Suppose (
30
) and (
31
) hold for all disjoint
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{0},\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{1}\subseteq[n]
with
|\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{1}|>|\mathcal{I}_{1}|
. For convenience, write
\mathbf{V}^{(i)}_{j}\coloneqq\mathbf{X}_{j}
for
j\leq i
and
\mathbf{V}^{(i)}_{j}\coloneqq\xi_{j}
for
j>i
. We also denote
V^{(i)}_{jp_{j}}=\overline{X_{j}^{\otimes p_{j}}}
for
j\leq i
and
V^{(i)}_{jp_{j}}=\xi_{jp_{j}}
for
j>i
.

Notice that by definition,
T(i,\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{1})
does not depend on
\mathbf{V}^{(i)}_{j}
for
j\in\mathcal{I}_{0}
. We enumerate the elements of
[n]\setminus(\mathcal{I}_{0}\cup\mathcal{I}_{1})
as
i_{1}<\ldots<i_{N}
for
N\coloneqq n-|\mathcal{I}_{0}|-|\mathcal{I}_{1}|
. Also denote
\mathbf{V}^{(i)}_{\mathcal{I}}\coloneqq\big\{\mathbf{V}^{(i)}_{j}\,\big|\,j\in\mathcal{I}\big\}
. Since
\mathbb{E}[T(i,\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{1})]=0
, we can define a martingale difference sequence by

\displaystyle D_{0}(i,\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{1})\;\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\;\mathbb{E}\Big[T(i,\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{1})\,\Big|\,\mathbf{V}^{(i)}_{\mathcal{I}_{1}}\Big]\;

and, for
1\leq j\leq N
,

\displaystyle D_{j}(i,\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{1})\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\Big[T(i,\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{1})\,\Big|\,\mathbf{V}^{(i)}_{\mathcal{I}_{1}},\mathbf{V}^{(i)}_{i_{1}},\ldots,\mathbf{V}^{(i)}_{i_{j}}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad-\mathbb{E}\Big[T(i,\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{1})\,\Big|\,\mathbf{V}^{(i)}_{\mathcal{I}_{1}},\mathbf{V}^{(i)}_{i_{1}},\ldots,\mathbf{V}^{(i)}_{i_{j-1}}\Big].

Then almost surely,

\displaystyle\bar{T}(i,\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{1})\;=\;\msum_{j=0}^{N}\bar{D}_{j}(i,\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{1})\;.

By applying
Lemma

31
with
\nu\in[2,3]
followed by a triangle inequality, we get that

\displaystyle\big\|T(i,\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{1})\big\|_{L_{\nu}}^{2}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime\prime}_{*}\,\Big\|\msum_{j=0}^{N}D_{j}(i,\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{1})^{2}\Big\|_{L_{\nu/2}}\;\leq\;C^{\prime\prime}\,\msum_{j=0}^{N}\|D_{j}(i,\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{1})\|_{L_{\nu}}^{2}\;,

(32)

\displaystyle\big\|T(i,\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{1})\big\|_{L_{2}}^{2}\;\geq

\displaystyle\;c^{\prime\prime}\,\msum_{j=0}^{N}\|D_{j}(i,\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{1})\|_{L_{2}}^{2}\;.

(33)

To control the martingale difference terms, recall that
\mathbb{E}\big[V^{(i)}_{jp_{j}}\big]=0
for all
i,j\in[n]
. Therefore

\displaystyle D_{0}(i,\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{1})\;=

\displaystyle\;\msum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}+\ldots+p_{n}=m\\
0\leq p_{l}\leq m\;\;\forall\,l\in[n]\\
p_{l^{\prime}}\geq 1\;\;\forall\,l^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{1}\\
p_{l^{\prime}}=0\;\;\forall\,l^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{0}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\langle S\,,\,V^{(i)}_{1p_{1}}\otimes\ldots\otimes V^{(i)}_{np_{n}}\big\rangle\,\Big|\,\mathbf{V}^{(i)}_{\mathcal{I}_{1}}\Big]\;=\;T(i,\,[n]\setminus\mathcal{I}_{1},\,\mathcal{I}_{1})\;,

i.e. there is no dependence on
\mathbf{V}^{(i)}_{j}
for any
j\not\in\mathcal{I}_{1}
. Similarly for
1\leq j\leq N
,

\displaystyle D_{j}(i,\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{1})\;=

\displaystyle\;\msum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}+\ldots+p_{n}=m\\
0\leq p_{l}\leq m\;\;\forall\,l\in[n]\\
p_{l^{\prime}}\geq 1\;\;\forall\,l^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{1}\\
p_{l^{\prime}}=0\;\;\forall\,l^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{0}\end{subarray}}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\langle S\,,\,V^{(i)}_{1p_{1}}\otimes\ldots\otimes V^{(i)}_{np_{n}}\big\rangle\,\Big|\,\mathbf{V}^{(i)}_{\mathcal{I}_{1}},\mathbf{V}^{(i)}_{i_{1}},\ldots,\mathbf{V}^{(i)}_{i_{j}}\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\;-\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\langle S\,,\,V^{(i)}_{1p_{1}}\otimes\ldots\otimes V^{(i)}_{np_{n}}\big\rangle\,\Big|\,\mathbf{V}^{(i)}_{\mathcal{I}_{1}},\mathbf{V}^{(i)}_{i_{1}},\ldots,\mathbf{V}^{(i)}_{i_{j-1}}\Big]\Big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;T(i,\mathcal{I}_{0}\cup\{i_{j+1},\ldots,i_{N}\},\mathcal{I}_{1}\cup\{i_{j}\})\;.

By noting that
N=n-|\mathcal{I}_{0}|-|\mathcal{I}_{1}|
and applying the inductive statement , we get that

\displaystyle\;\msum_{j=0}^{N}\|D_{j}(i,\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{1})\|_{L_{\nu}}^{2}

\displaystyle\;=\;\big\|T(i,[n]\setminus\mathcal{I}_{1},\mathcal{I}_{1})\big\|_{L_{\nu}}^{2}+\msum_{j=1}^{N}\big\|T(i,\mathcal{I}_{0}\cup\{i_{j+1},\ldots,i_{N}\},\mathcal{I}_{1}\cup\{i_{j}\})\big\|_{L_{\nu}}^{2}

\displaystyle\;\leq\;(C_{*})^{m}(\alpha_{\nu}(|\mathcal{I}_{1}|))^{2}+(C_{*})^{m}\msum_{j=1}^{N}\msum_{k=0}^{m-(|\mathcal{I}_{1}|+1)}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(C'')^{k+1}}{k!}$}}\times

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad(n-(|\mathcal{I}_{0}|+N-j)-(|\mathcal{I}_{1}|+1)+1)^{k}(\alpha_{\nu}(|\mathcal{I}_{1}|+1+k))^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\;=\;(C_{*})^{m}(\alpha_{\nu}(|\mathcal{I}_{1}|))^{2}+(C_{*})^{m}\msum_{j=1}^{N}\msum_{k=1}^{m-|\mathcal{I}_{1}|}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(C'')^k}{(k-1)!}$}}j^{k-1}(\alpha_{\nu}(|\mathcal{I}_{1}|+k))^{2}

\displaystyle\;=\;(C_{*})^{m}(\alpha_{\nu}(|\mathcal{I}_{1}|))^{2}+(C_{*})^{m}\msum_{k=1}^{m-|\mathcal{I}_{1}|}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(C'')^k}{(k-1)!}$}}\big(\msum_{j=1}^{N}j^{k-1}\big)(\alpha_{\nu}(|\mathcal{I}_{1}|+k))^{2}\;.

Since
k-1\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\msum_{j=1}^{N}j^{k-1}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\msum_{j=1}^{N}\mint_{j}^{j+1}x^{k-1}dx\;=\;\msum_{j=1}^{N}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(j+1)^k - j^k }{k}$}}\;<\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(N+1)^k}{k}$}}\;.

Substituting these into (
32
), we get that

\displaystyle\big\|T(i,\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{1})\big\|_{L_{\nu}}^{2}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime\prime}\msum_{j=0}^{N}\|D_{j}(i,\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{1})\|_{L_{\nu}}^{2}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime\prime}(C_{*})^{m}\msum_{k=0}^{m-|\mathcal{I}_{1}|}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(C'')^k}{k!}$}}(N+1)^{k}(\alpha_{\nu}(|\mathcal{I}_{1}|+k))^{2}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;(C_{*})^{m}\msum_{k=0}^{m-|\mathcal{I}_{1}|}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(C'')^{k+1}}{k!}$}}(n-|\mathcal{I}_{0}|-|\mathcal{I}_{1}|+1)^{k}(\alpha_{\nu}(|\mathcal{I}_{1}|+k))^{2}\;,

which finishes the induction for (
30
). Similarly for (
31
), using the inductive statement gives

\displaystyle\;\msum_{j=0}^{N}\|D_{j}(i,\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{1})\|_{L_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\;=\;\big\|T(i,[n]\setminus\mathcal{I}_{1},\mathcal{I}_{1})\big\|_{L_{2}}^{2}+\msum_{j=1}^{N}\big\|T(i,\mathcal{I}_{0}\cup\{i_{j+1},\ldots,i_{N}\},\mathcal{I}_{1}\cup\{i_{j}\})\big\|_{L_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\;\geq\;(\alpha_{2}(|\mathcal{I}_{1}|))^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\sum_{k=0}^{m-(|\mathcal{I}_{1}|+1)}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{( c'' )^{k+1}}{k!}$}}(\alpha_{2}(|\mathcal{I}_{1}|+1+k))^{2}\max\{j^{k}-k^{2}j^{k-1}\,,\,0\}\Big)

\displaystyle\;=\;(\alpha_{2}(|\mathcal{I}_{1}|))^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{m-|\mathcal{I}_{1}|}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(c'' )^k}{(k-1)!}$}}\Bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{N}\max\big\{j^{k-1}-(k-1)^{2}j^{k-2}\,,\,0\big\}\Bigg)(\alpha_{2}(|\mathcal{I}_{1}|+k))^{2}\;.

Since
k-1\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\msum_{j=1}^{N}\max\big\{j^{k-1}-(k-1)^{2}j^{k-2}\,,\,0\big\}

\displaystyle\;\geq\;\max\big\{\msum_{j=1}^{N}\big(j^{k-1}-(k-1)^{2}j^{k-2}\big)\,,\,0\big\}

\displaystyle\;=\;\max\Big\{\msum_{j=1}^{N+1}j^{k-1}-(N+1)^{k-1}-\msum_{j=1}^{N}(k-1)^{2}j^{k-2}\,,\,0\Big\}

\displaystyle\;\geq\;\max\Big\{\msum_{j=1}^{N+1}\mint_{j-1}^{j}x^{k-1}dx-(N+1)^{k-1}-\msum_{j=1}^{N}(k-1)^{2}\mint^{j+1}_{j}x^{k-2}dx\,,\,0\Big\}

\displaystyle\;=\;\max\bigg\{\sum_{j=1}^{N+1}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{j^k - (j-1)^k}{k}$}}-(N+1)^{k-1}-\sum_{j=1}^{N}(k-1)((j+1)^{k-1}-j^{k-1})\,,\,0\bigg\}

\displaystyle\;>\;\max\Big\{\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(N+1)^k}{k}$}}-(N+1)^{k-1}-(k-1)(N+1)^{k-1}\,,\,0\Big\}

\displaystyle\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{k}$}}\max\big\{(N+1)^{k}-k^{2}(N+1)^{k-1}\,,\,0\big\}\;,

Substituting these into (
33
), we get that

\displaystyle\big\|T(i,\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{1})

\displaystyle\big\|_{L_{2}}^{2}\;\geq\;c^{\prime\prime}\,\msum_{j=0}^{N}\|D_{j}(i,\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{1})\|_{L_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\;\geq

\displaystyle\;c^{\prime\prime}\msum_{k=0}^{m-|\mathcal{I}_{1}|}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(c'')^k}{k!}$}}\max\{(N+1)^{k}-k^{2}(N+1)^{k-1},0\}(\alpha_{2}(|\mathcal{I}_{1}|+k))^{2}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\msum_{k=0}^{m-|\mathcal{I}_{1}|}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(c'')^{k+1}}{k!}$}}(\alpha_{2}(|\mathcal{I}_{1}|+k))^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\max\big\{(n-|\mathcal{I}_{0}|-|\mathcal{I}_{1}|+1)^{k}-k^{2}(n-|\mathcal{I}_{0}|-|\mathcal{I}_{1}|+1)^{k-1},0\big\}\Big)\;.

This finishes the induction for (
31
). In particular, we can now obtain

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sum_{i=1}^n M_{\nu;i}^\nu}{\sigma^\nu}$}}\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sum_{i=1}^n \| T(i, \{i\}, \emptyset)\|_{L_\nu}^\nu}{ \| T(n,\emptyset,\emptyset) \|_{L_2}^\nu}$}}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
n
\Big(
(C_*)^m
\sum_{k=0}^{m - 1}
\frac{(C'')^{k+1} (\alpha_\nu( k+1))^2}{k!}
n^k
\Big)^{\frac{\nu}{2}}
}{
\Big(
\sum_{k=0}^m
\frac{(c'')^{k+1} (\alpha_2(k))^2}{k!}
\max\big\{ (n+1)^k - k^2 (n+1)^{k-1} , 0\big\}
\Big)^{\frac{\nu}{2}}
}$}}\;.

Since
n\geq 2m^{2}
by assumption, we have

\displaystyle(n+1)^{k}-k^{2}(n+1)^{k-1}\;\geq

\displaystyle\;(n+1)^{k-1}(n+1-m^{2})\;\geq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}(n+1)^{k}\;.

By further noting that
\alpha_{2}(0)=0
, we can simplify the ratio as

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sum_{i=1}^n M_{\nu;i}^\nu}{\sigma^\nu}$}}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
2^\nu(C_*)^{\frac{m\nu}{2}}
n
\Big(
\msum_{k=0}^{m - 1}
\mfrac{(C'')^{k+1}}{k!}
n^k
(\alpha_\nu( k+1))^2
\Big)^{\nu/2}
}{
\Big(
\msum_{k=0}^m
\mfrac{(c'')^{k+1}}{k!}
(n+1)^k
(\alpha_2(k))^2
\Big)^{\nu/2}
}$}}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
2^\nu(C_*)^{\frac{m\nu}{2}}
n
\Big(
\msum_{k=1}^m
\mfrac{(C'')^{k}}{(k-1)!}
n^{k-1}
(\alpha_\nu( k))^2
\Big)^{\nu/2}
}{
\Big(
\msum_{k=1}^m
\mfrac{(c'')^{k+1}}{k!}
(n+1)^k
(\alpha_2(k))^2
\Big)^{\nu/2}
}$}}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2^\nu(C_*)^{\frac{m\nu}{2}}}{n^{\frac{\nu-2}{2}}}$}}\,\Bigg(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
\sum_{k=1}^m
\frac{(C'')^{k}}{(k-1)!}
n^k
(\alpha_\nu(k))^2
}{
\sum_{k=1}^m
\frac{(c'')^{k+1}}{k!}
(n+1)^k
(\alpha_2(k))^2
}$}}\Bigg)^{\frac{\nu}{2}}\;.

Meanwhile by Stirling’s approximation,
\frac{n^{k}}{(k-1)!}\leq A^{m}\binom{n}{k}
and
\frac{(n+1)^{k}}{k!}\geq\frac{n^{k}}{k!}\geq B^{m}\binom{n}{k}
for some absolute constants
A,B>0
. Since
\nu<3
, we get that for some absolute constant
C^{\prime}>0
,

\displaystyle\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \sum_{i=1}^n M_{\nu;i}^\nu}{ \sigma^\nu}$}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{\nu m+1}}\;\leq\;C^{\prime}\,n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2\nu m+2}}\,\Bigg(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
\sum_{k=1}^m
\binom{n}{k}
(\alpha_\nu(S, k))^2
}{
\sum_{k=1}^m
\binom{n}{k}
(\alpha_2(S,k))^2
}$}}\Bigg)^{\frac{\nu}{2\nu m+2}}\;=\;C^{\prime}\,n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2\nu m+2}}\,\beta_{m,\nu}^{\frac{\nu}{2\nu m+2}}\;.

By applying (
30
) and (
31
) to
\sigma^{2}=\text{\rm Var}[v_{m}(X)]
and using the same argument as above,
we also get that if
n\geq 2m^{2}
, there exist some absolute constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
such that

\displaystyle\hskip 15.00002pt\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(C_1)^m}{n^{2m}}$}}\msum_{k=1}^{m}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{n}{k}$}}\Bigr)(\alpha_{2}(S,k))^{2}\;\leq\;\text{\rm Var}[v_{m}(X)]\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(C_2)^m}{n^{2m}}$}}\msum_{k=1}^{m}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{n}{k}$}}\Bigr)(\alpha_{2}(S,k))^{2}\;.\hskip 10.00002pt\qed

Proof of Proposition
6

Recall that
q^{v}_{m}
is the multilinear representation of
v_{m}
, and WLOG assume the coupling between
\xi_{i}
and
Z_{i}
considered in
Lemma

7
. By the exact same argument as the proof of
Theorem

3
leading up to (
25
) (
\delta
-regularity,
Lemma

26
, a Markov’s inequality and choosing
\epsilon
appropriately), we get that for some absolute constant
C_{1}>0
,

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{P}\big(\sigma^{-1}(q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)+\mathbb{E}[v_{m}(X)])\leq t\big)-\mathbb{P}\big(\sigma^{-1}v_{m}(Z)\leq t\big)\big|\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C_{1}mn^{-\frac{1-\delta}{2m+1}}\;.

(34)

By applying
Lemma

8
to replace
q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)
by
q^{v}_{m}(\mathbf{X})
, noting that
v_{m}(X)=q^{v}_{m}(\mathbf{X})+\mathbb{E}[v_{m}(X)]
and using a triangle inequality to combine the bounds, we get that for some absolute constant
C^{\prime}>0
,

\displaystyle\sup\nolimits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\big|\mathbb{P}(\sigma^{-1}v_{m}(X)\leq t)-\mathbb{P}(\sigma^{-1}v_{m}(Z)\leq t)\big|\;\leq\;C_{1}mn^{-\frac{1-\delta}{2m+1}}+C^{\prime}m\Delta_{\delta}\;.

To prove the final bound, we first note that by
Lemma

8
, (
34
) and the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\sup\nolimits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\big|\mathbb{P}(\sigma^{-1}(v_{m}(X)-\mathbb{E}[v_{m}(X)])\leq t)-\mathbb{P}(\sigma^{-1}(v_{m}(Z)-\mathbb{E}[v_{m}(Z)])\leq t)\big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime}m\Delta_{\delta}+\sup\nolimits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\big|\mathbb{P}(\sigma^{-1}q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)\leq t)-\mathbb{P}(\sigma^{-1}(v_{m}(Z)-\mathbb{E}[v_{m}(Z)])\leq t)\big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime}m\Delta_{\delta}+C_{1}mn^{-\frac{1-\delta}{2m+1}}

\displaystyle\;\qquad+\sup\nolimits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\big|\mathbb{P}(\sigma^{-1}q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)\leq t)-\mathbb{P}(\sigma^{-1}(q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)+\mathbb{E}[v_{m}(X)]-\mathbb{E}[v_{m}(Z)])\leq t)\big|\;.

By the same Carbery-Wright inequality argument used in the proof of
Theorem

1
in (
18
), the last term can be bounded by

\displaystyle\;\sup\nolimits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{P}\big(|\sigma^{-1}q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)-t|\leq\sigma^{-1}|\mathbb{E}[v_{m}(X)-v_{m}(Z)]|\big)

\displaystyle\;\leq\;C_{2}m\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{| \sigma^{-1} \mean[v_m(X)] - \sigma^{-1} \mean[v_m(Z)]|^{1/m}}{\mean[(\sigma^{-1} q^v_m(\Xi) - t )^2]^{1/2m}}$}}\;=\;C_{2}m\sigma^{-1/m}\big|\mathbb{E}[v_{m}(X)]-\mathbb{E}[v_{m}(Z)]\big|^{1/m}

\displaystyle\;=\;C_{2}m\sigma^{-1/m}\big|\mathbb{E}\big[q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)+\mathbb{E}[v_{m}(X)]-v_{m}(Z)\big]\big|^{1/m}

\displaystyle\;\leq\;C_{2}m\bigg(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\big\| q^v_m(\Xi) + \mean[ v_m(X) ] - v_m(Z) \big\|_{L_2}^2}{\Var[q^v_m(\Xi)]}$}}\bigg)^{1/(2m)}\;\leq\;C_{3}mn^{-\frac{1-\delta}{2m}}\;\leq\;C_{3}mn^{-\frac{1-\delta}{2m+1}}

for some absolute constants
C_{2},C_{3}>0
; in the last line, we noted that
\sigma^{2}=\text{\rm Var}[v_{m}(X)]=\text{\rm Var}[q^{v}_{m}(\Xi)]
and applied
Lemma

7
again. By taking
C=C_{1}+C_{3}>0
, we get that

\displaystyle\sup\nolimits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\big|\mathbb{P}(\sigma^{-1}(v_{m}(X)-\mathbb{E}[v_{m}(X)])\leq t)-\mathbb{P}(\sigma^{-1}(v_{m}(Z)-

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[v_{m}(Z)])\leq t)\big|

\displaystyle\hskip-10.00002pt\;\leq\;Cmn^{-\frac{1-\delta}{2m+1}}+C^{\prime}m\Delta_{\delta}\;.\qed

Proofs for Section
3

Proof of Proposition
9

Since
g
is
(m+1)
-times continuously differentiable, by an
m
-th order Taylor expansion around
\mathbb{E}X_{1}
, we have that almost surely

\displaystyle\big|\hat{g}(X)

\displaystyle-\msum_{l=0}^{m}\mu_{l}-\big(\hat{g}^{(\mathbb{E}X_{1})}_{m}(X)-\mu_{m}\big)\big|

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\Big|\mu_{0}+\msum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{g}_{j}^{(\mathbb{E}X_{1})}(X)\,+\,(m+1)\mathbb{E}\Big[(1-\Theta)^{m+1}\,\hat{g}_{m+1}^{\big(\mathbb{E}X_{1}+\Theta n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\bar{X}_{i}\big)}(X)\,\Big|\,X\Big]

\displaystyle\quad-\msum_{l=0}^{m}\mu_{l}-\big(\hat{g}^{(\mathbb{E}X_{1})}_{m}(X)-\mu_{m}\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\Big|\msum_{l=1}^{m-1}\big(\hat{g}^{(\mathbb{E}X_{1})}_{l}(X)-\mu_{l}\big)\,+\,(m+1)\mathbb{E}\Big[(1-\Theta)^{m+1}\,\hat{g}_{m+1}^{\big(\mathbb{E}X_{1}+\Theta n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\bar{X}_{i}\big)}(X)\,\Big|\,X\Big]\Big|

\displaystyle\;\eqqcolon

\displaystyle\;R\;.

Now let
\mathbf{X}_{i}=(\bar{X}_{i},\ldots,\overline{X}_{i}^{\otimes m})
and
\xi_{i}
be defined as in
Theorem

2
,
f_{1}
be the multilinear representation of
\hat{g}^{(\mathbb{E}X_{1})}_{m}
and denote

\sigma_{m}\;\coloneqq\;\text{\rm Var}\big[\hat{g}^{(\mathbb{E}X_{1})}_{m}(X)\big]\;=\;\text{\rm Var}\big[f_{1}(\mathbf{X})\big]\;.

Then by
Theorem

2
, there is some absolute constant
C^{\prime}>0
,

\displaystyle\sup\nolimits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\big|\mathbb{P}\big(\hat{g}(X)-\msum_{l=0}^{m}\mu_{l}\leq t\big)-\mathbb{P}(f_{1}(\Xi)\leq t)\big|

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}m\Bigg(\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\| R \|_{L_2}^2}{\sigma_m^2}$}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2m+1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+\Bigg(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \sum_{i=1}^n
\big\| \partial_i f_1( \bX_1, \ldots, \bX_{i-1}, \mean[\bX_1], \xi_{i+1}, \ldots, \xi_n) (\bX_i - \mean[\bX_1]) \big\|_{L_\nu}^\nu}{ \sigma_m^\nu}$}}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{\nu m+1}}\Bigg)\;.

The first term can be controlled by applying the triangle inequality twice, using a Jensen’s inequality and noting the definition of
\epsilon_{m}
:

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\| R \|_{L_2}^2}{\sigma_m^2}$}}

\displaystyle\,\;\leq\;\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
\Big( \Big\|
\sum_{l=1}^{m-1} \big( \hat g^{(\mean X_1)}_{l}(X) - \mu_l\big)
\Big\|_{L_2}
+
(m+1)
\Big\|
\mean\Big[
(1-\Theta)^{m+1}
\,
\hat g_{m+1}^{\big(\mean X_1 + \Theta n^{-1} \sum_{i \leq n} \bar X_i \big)}(X)
\,\Big|\, X \Big]
\Big\|_{L_2}\Big)^2}{\sigma_m^2}$}}

\displaystyle\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
2
\Var\Big[
\sum_{l=1}^{m-1} \hat g^{(\mean X_1)}_{l}(X)
\Big]
}{\sigma_m^2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
2
(m+1)^2
\Big\|
\mean\Big[
(1-\Theta)^{m+1}
\,
\hat g_{m+1}^{\big(\mean X_1 + \Theta n^{-1} \sum_{i \leq n} \bar X_i \big)}(X)
\Big| X \Big]
\Big\|_{L_2}^2
}{\sigma_m^2}$}}

\displaystyle\;=\;2\epsilon_{m}\;.

By noting that
Lemma

8
applies to
\hat{g}_{m}^{(\mathbb{E}X_{1})}(X)
, the second term can be bounded by

\displaystyle C^{\prime\prime}\,n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2\nu m+2}}\,\Bigg(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
\sum_{k=1}^m
\binom{n}{k}
(\alpha_{m,\nu}(S))^2
}{
\sum_{k=1}^m
\binom{n}{k}
(\alpha_{m,2}(k))^2
}$}}\Bigg)^{\frac{\nu}{2\nu m+2}}\;=\;C^{\prime\prime}n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2\nu m+2}}\beta_{m,\nu}^{\frac{\nu}{2\nu m+2}}

for some absolute constant
C^{\prime\prime}>0
. Moreover by the
\delta
-regularity assumption, the first bound of
Proposition

6
implies that for some absolute constant
C^{\prime\prime\prime}>0
,

\displaystyle\sup\nolimits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\big|\,\mathbb{P}(f_{1}(\Xi)\leq t)-\mathbb{P}\big(\hat{g}_{m}^{(\mathbb{E}X_{1})}(Z)-\mathbb{E}[\hat{g}_{m}^{(\mathbb{E}X_{1})}(Z)]\leq t\big)\,\big|\;\leq\;C^{\prime\prime\prime}mn^{-\frac{1-\delta}{2m+1}}\;.

Combining all three bounds gives that, for some absolute constant
C>0
,

\displaystyle\sup\nolimits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\big|\mathbb{P}\big(\hat{g}(X)-\msum_{l=0}^{m}\mu_{l}\leq t\big)-\mathbb{P}\big(

\displaystyle\hat{g}_{m}^{(\mathbb{E}X_{1})}(Z)-\mathbb{E}[\hat{g}_{m}^{(\mathbb{E}X_{1})}(Z)]\leq t\big)\big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;Cm\big(\epsilon_{m}^{\frac{1}{2m+1}}+n^{-\frac{1-\delta}{2m+1}}+n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2\nu m+2}}\,\beta_{m,\nu}^{\frac{\nu}{2\nu m+2}}\big)\;.

To prove the fourth moment bound, applying
Proposition

4
and
Remark

4
imply that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\Big|\mathbb{P}\big(\hat{g}_{m}^{(\mathbb{E}X_{1})}(Z)-\mathbb{E}[\hat{g}_{m}^{(\mathbb{E}X_{1})}(Z)]\leq t\big)-\Phi(\sigma_{Z}^{-1}t)\Big|\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4m-4}{3m}$}}\,\big|\text{\rm Kurt}\big[\hat{g}_{m}^{(\mathbb{E}X_{1})}(Z)\big]\big|\Big)^{1/2}\;,

where
\sigma_{Z}^{2}\coloneqq\text{\rm Var}\big[\hat{g}_{m}^{(\mathbb{E}X_{1})}(Z)\big]
. Write
\sigma_{X}^{2}\coloneqq\text{\rm Var}\big[\hat{g}_{m}^{(\mathbb{E}X_{1})}(X)\big]
. Note that if
t=0
,

\displaystyle\big|\Phi(\sigma_{Z}^{-1}t)-\Phi(\sigma_{X}^{-1}t)\big|\;=\;0\;.

Suppose
t\neq 0
and write
\eta\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. By
Lemma

18
, we have that

\displaystyle\big|\Phi(\sigma_{Z}^{-1}t)-\Phi(\sigma_{X}^{-1}t)\big|\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime}\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\big| \sigma_Z^{-1} t - \sigma_X^{-1} t \big| \, / 2 }{ \big( \mean\big[ \big(\eta- (\sigma_Z^{-1} t + \sigma_X^{-1}t) / 2 \big)^2 \big] \, \big)^{1/2} }$}}\;\leq\;C^{\prime}\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\big| \sigma_Z^{-1} t - \sigma_X^{-1} t \big| }{\big| \sigma_Z^{-1} t + \sigma_X^{-1}t \big| }$}}

for some absolute constant
C^{\prime}>0
. Rearranging and applying the second bound of
Lemma

7
,

\displaystyle\big|\Phi(\sigma_{Z}^{-1}t)-\Phi(\sigma_{X}^{-1}t)\big|\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime}\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\big| 1 - \sigma_X^{-1} \sigma_Z \big| }{ 1 + \sigma_X^{-1} \sigma_Z }$}}\;\leq\;C^{\prime}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(C'')^m \, n^{-(1-\delta)/2}}{ 2 - (C'')^m \, n^{-(1-\delta)/2}}$}}

for some absolute constant
C^{\prime\prime}>0
. Applying a triangle inequality and replacing
t
with
\sigma_{X}t
, we obtain the final bound that

\displaystyle\sup\nolimits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\Big|\mathbb{P}\Big(\sigma_{X}^{-1}

\displaystyle\big(\hat{g}(X)-\msum_{l=0}^{m}\mu_{l}\big)\leq t\Big)-\Phi(t)\Big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\Delta_{\hat{g},m}+C^{\prime}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(C'')^m \, n^{-(1-\delta)/2}}{ 2 - (C'')^m \, n^{-(1-\delta)/2}}$}}+\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4m-4}{3m}$}}\,\big|\text{\rm Kurt}\big[\hat{g}_{m}^{(\mathbb{E}X_{1})}(Z)\big]\big|\Big)^{1/2}.\qquad\qed

Proof of Proposition
10

Fix
K\in\mathbb{N}
. Denote

\displaystyle\,\mu_{k}\;\coloneqq\;\mathbb{E}[\phi^{(K)}_{k}(Y_{1})]\in\mathbb{R}\;,\quad\bar{u}\;\coloneqq\;\mathbb{E}[u(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{m})]\quad\text{ and }\quad V^{K}\;\coloneqq\;\{V^{K}_{1},\ldots,V^{K}_{n}\}\;,

\displaystyle\hskip 120.00018pt\text{ where }\quad V^{K}_{i}\;\coloneqq\;(\phi^{(K)}_{1}(Y_{i})-\mu_{1},\ldots,\phi^{(K)}_{K}(Y_{i})-\mu_{K})\;.

By Hoeffding’s decomposition (
11
) and writing

j\neq M
in short for
j\in[m]\setminus\{M\}
, we have

\displaystyle u_{m}(Y)-\bar{u}\;=\;\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{m}{M}$}}\Bigr)\,U^{(K)}_{M}(V^{K})+\msum_{j\neq M}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{m}{j}$}}\Bigr)\,U_{j}^{\rm H}(Y)+\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{m}{M}$}}\Bigr)\,\Big(U_{M}^{\rm H}(Y)-U^{(K)}_{M}(V^{K})\Big)\;.

By
Theorem

2
, we get that for some absolute constant
C^{\prime}>0
and for every
t\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{P}\big(

\displaystyle u_{m}(Y)-\bar{u}\leq t\big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{m}{M}$}}\Bigr)\,U_{M}^{(K)}(\Xi^{(K)})\leq t\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime}m\Bigg(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
\sum_{i=1}^n
\big\|
\partial_i U_M^{(K)}
\Big(
V^K_1, \ldots, V^K_{i-1}, \bzero, \xi^{(K)}_{i+1}, \ldots, \xi^{(K)}_n
\Big)
V^K_i
\big\|_{L_\nu}^\nu}{
\Big\| U_M^{(K)}(V^K) \Big\|_{L_2}^\nu}$}}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{\nu M+1}}

\displaystyle\hskip-5.0pt+C^{\prime}m\Bigg(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
\Big\|
\sum_{j \neq M} \mbinom{m}{j} \, U_j^{\rm H}(Y)
+
\mbinom{m}{M} \,
\Big( U_M^{\rm H}(Y) - U^{(K)}_M(V^K) \Big)
\Big\|_{L_2}^2
}{
\Big\| \mbinom{m}{M} \, U_M^{(K)}(V^K) \Big\|_{L_2}^2
}$}}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2M+1}}\hskip-5.0pt\eqqcolon C^{\prime}m(R_{1}+R_{2})\;.

Before proceeding, first note that
U_{M}^{(K)}(V^{K})
is a U-statistic with the kernel

\displaystyle u^{(K)}_{M}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{j})\;=

\displaystyle\;\msum_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{m}=1}^{K}\lambda^{(K)}_{k_{1}\ldots k_{m}}v_{1k_{1}}\ldots v_{Mk_{M}}\;=\;\langle T_{M},v_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes v_{M}\rangle

for some deterministic tensor
T_{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{K^{M}}
, and where
V^{K}_{i}
’s are zero-mean. By
Lemma

29
,

\displaystyle\|U_{M}^{(K)}(V^{K})\|_{L_{2}}^{2}\;\geq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n^M}$}}\big\|u_{m}^{(K)}(V_{1}^{K},\ldots,V_{M}^{K})\big\|_{L_{2}}^{2}\;.

We defer to
Lemma

32
to show that for some absolute constant
C_{*}>0
,

\displaystyle\Big\|\partial_{i}U_{M}^{(K)}\Big(V^{K}_{1},\ldots,V^{K}_{i-1},\mathbf{0},\xi^{(K)}_{i+1},\ldots,\xi^{(K)}_{n}\Big)V^{K}_{i}\Big\|_{L_{\nu}}^{\nu}\leq\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ C_*^m \big\| u_m^{(K)}(V_1^K, \ldots, V_M^K)
\big\|_{L_\nu}^\nu}{n^{(M+1)\nu/2}}$}}\;.

(35)

Let
Y^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,Y^{\prime}_{m}
be i.i.d. copies of
Y_{1}
. Now by the definition of
V^{K}_{i}
, the binomial theorem, the triangle inequality and Jensen’s inequality, the truncation error satisfies

\displaystyle\Big\|

\displaystyle U_{M}^{\rm H}(Y)-U_{M}^{(K)}(V^{K})\Big\|_{L_{\nu}}\;\leq\;\Big\|u_{M}^{\rm H}(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{M})-u_{M}^{(K)}(V^{K}_{1},\ldots,V^{K}_{M})\Big\|_{L_{\nu}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big\|\msum_{r=0}^{M}(-1)^{M-r}\msum_{1\leq l_{1}<\ldots<l_{r}\leq M}\mathbb{E}\big[u(Y_{l_{1}},\ldots,Y_{l_{r}},Y^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,Y^{\prime}_{m-r})\big|Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{M}\big]

\displaystyle\;-\msum_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{m}=1}^{K}\,\lambda^{(K)}_{k_{1}\ldots k_{m}}(\phi^{(K)}_{k_{1}}(Y_{i})-\mu_{k_{1}})\ldots(\phi^{(K)}_{k_{M}}(Y_{M})-\mu_{k_{M}})\mu_{k_{M+1}}\ldots\mu_{k_{m}}\Big\|_{L_{\nu}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big\|\msum_{r=0}^{M}(-1)^{M-r}\msum_{1\leq l_{1}<\ldots<l_{r}\leq M}\mathbb{E}\big[u(Y_{l_{1}},\ldots,Y_{l_{r}},Y^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,Y^{\prime}_{m-r})\big|Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{M}\big]

\displaystyle\;-\msum_{r=0}^{M}(-1)^{M-r}\msum_{1\leq l_{1}<\ldots<l_{r}\leq M}

\displaystyle\hskip 80.00012pt\Big(\msum_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{m}=1}^{K}\,\lambda^{(K)}_{k_{1}\ldots k_{m}}\phi_{k_{l_{1}}}(Y_{l_{1}})\ldots\phi_{k_{l_{r}}}(Y_{l_{r}})\,\mprod_{\begin{subarray}{c}k^{\prime}\in[M]\\
k^{\prime}\neq l_{s}\,\forall s\end{subarray}}\mu_{k^{\prime}}\Big)\Big\|_{L_{\nu}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\msum_{r=0}^{M}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{M}{r}$}}\Bigr)\Big\|u(Y_{l_{1}},\ldots,Y_{l_{r}},Y^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,Y^{\prime}_{m-r})

\displaystyle\hskip 60.00009pt-\msum_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{m}=1}^{K}\Big(\,\lambda^{(K)}_{k_{1}\ldots k_{m}}\phi_{k_{l_{1}}}(Y_{l_{1}})\ldots\phi_{k_{l_{r}}}(Y_{l_{r}})\mprod_{\begin{subarray}{c}k^{\prime}\in[M]\\
k^{\prime}\neq l_{s}\,\forall s\end{subarray}}\phi_{k^{\prime}}(Y^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}})\Big)\Big\|_{L_{\nu}}

\displaystyle\;\overset{(a)}{=}

\displaystyle\;2^{M}\epsilon_{K;\nu}\;.

In
(a)
above, we have noted that for any permutation
\pi
on
\{1,\ldots,m\}
, the
L_{\nu}
approximation error satisfies

\displaystyle\epsilon_{K;\nu}\;=

\displaystyle\;\big\|\msum_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{m}=1}^{K}\lambda^{(K)}_{k_{1}\ldots k_{m}}\phi^{(K)}_{k_{1}}(Y_{1})\times\ldots\times\phi^{(K)}_{k_{m}}(Y_{m})-u(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{m})\big\|_{L_{\nu}}

\displaystyle\;\overset{(b)}{=}

\displaystyle\;\big\|\msum_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{m}=1}^{K}\lambda^{(K)}_{k_{1}\ldots k_{m}}\phi^{(K)}_{k_{1}}(Y_{\pi(1)})\times\ldots\times\phi^{(K)}_{k_{m}}(Y_{\pi(m)})-u(Y_{\pi(1)},\ldots,Y_{\pi(m)})\big\|_{L_{\nu}}

\displaystyle\;\overset{(c)}{=}

\displaystyle\;\big\|\msum_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{m}=1}^{K}\lambda^{(K)}_{k_{1}\ldots k_{m}}\phi^{(K)}_{k_{\pi^{-1}(1)}}(Y_{1})\times\ldots\times\phi^{(K)}_{k_{\pi^{-1}(m)}}(Y_{m})-u(Y_{\pi(1)},\ldots,Y_{\pi(m)})\big\|_{L_{\nu}}

\displaystyle\;\overset{(d)}{=}

\displaystyle\;\big\|\msum_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{m}=1}^{K}\lambda^{(K)}_{k_{1}\ldots k_{m}}\phi^{(K)}_{k_{\pi^{-1}(1)}}(Y_{1})\times\ldots\times\phi^{(K)}_{k_{\pi^{-1}(m)}}(Y_{m})-u(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{m})\big\|_{L_{\nu}}\;,

where we have used that
Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{m}
are i.i.d. in
(b)
, the commutativity of scalar product in
(c)
and that
u
is symmetric in
(d)
. Combining the bounds above, we get that there is some absolute constant
C^{\prime\prime}>0
such that

\displaystyle R_{1}

\displaystyle\;\leq\;C^{\prime\prime}n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2(\nu M+1)}}\Bigg(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
\Big\|
u_M^{(K)} \big( V^K_1,\ldots, V^K_M\big)
\Big\|_{L_\nu} }{
\Big\|
u_M^{(K)} \big( V^K_1,\ldots, V^K_M \big)
\Big\|_{L_2}
}$}}\Bigg)^{\frac{\nu}{\nu M+1}}

\displaystyle\;\leq\;C^{\prime\prime}n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2(\nu M+1)}}\Bigg(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
\Big\|
u_M^{\rm H} \big( Y_1,\ldots, Y_M \big)
\Big\|_{L_\nu} + (2^M ) \, \epsilon_{K;\nu}
}{
\Big\|
u_M^{\rm H} \big( Y_1,\ldots, Y_M \big)
\Big\|_{L_2}
- (2^M ) \, \epsilon_{K;\nu}
}$}}\Bigg)^{\frac{\nu}{\nu M+1}}\;.

Denote
\sigma_{j}^{2}\coloneqq\text{\rm Var}\,\mathbb{E}[u(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{m})\,|\,Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{j}]
. By the truncation error bound again, we have

\displaystyle R_{2}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\Bigg(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
2
\Big\|
\sum_{j \neq M} \mbinom{m}{j} \, U_j^{\rm H}(Y)
\Big\|_{L_2}^2
+
\mbinom{m}{M}^2
2^{M+1} \epsilon_{K;\nu}^2
}{
\Big\| \mbinom{m}{M} \, U_M^{\rm H}(Y) \Big\|_{L_2}^2
-
\mbinom{m}{M}^2
2^{M+1} \epsilon_{K;\nu}^2
}$}}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2M+1}}

\displaystyle\;\overset{(a)}{=}

\displaystyle\;\Bigg(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
2
\sum_{j \neq M} \mbinom{m}{j}^2
\big\|
\, U_j^{\rm H}(Y)
\big\|_{L_2}^2
+
\mbinom{m}{M}^2
2^{M+1} \epsilon_{K;\nu}^2
}{
\mbinom{m}{M}^2 \big\| \, U^{\rm H}_M(Y) \big\|_{L_2}^2
-
\mbinom{m}{M}^2
2^{M+1} \epsilon_{K;\nu}^2
}$}}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2M+1}}

\displaystyle\;\overset{(b)}{=}

\displaystyle\;\Bigg(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
2
\sum_{j \neq M} \mbinom{m}{j}^2
\mbinom{n}{j}^{-1} \sigma^2_j
+
\mbinom{m}{M}^2
2^{M+1} \epsilon_{K;\nu}^2
}{
\mbinom{m}{M}^2 \mbinom{n}{M}^{-1} \sigma^2_M
-
\mbinom{m}{M}^2
2^{M+1} \epsilon_{K;\nu}^2
}$}}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2M+1}}

\displaystyle\;\overset{(c)}{=}

\displaystyle\;\Bigg(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
2
\sum_{j \neq M} \sigma^2_{m,n;j}
+
\mbinom{m}{M}^2
2^{M+1} \epsilon_{K;\nu}^2
}{
\sigma^2_{m,n;M}
-
\mbinom{m}{M}^2
2^{M+1} \epsilon_{K;\nu}^2
}$}}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2M+1}}\;.

In
(a)
, we have used the orthogonality of the degenerate U-statistics of different degrees by
Lemma

27
; in
(b)
, we have used the variance formula of
U_{j}^{\rm H}(Y)
in
Lemma

29
; in
(c)
, we have plugged in the definition of
\sigma^{2}_{m,n;j}
. Combining the bounds and taking
K\rightarrow\infty
, we get

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{P}\big(u_{m}(Y)-

\displaystyle\bar{u}\leq t\big)-\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big(\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{m}{M}$}}\Bigr)\,\tilde{U}_{M}^{(K)}(\Xi^{(K)})\leq t\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;Cmn^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2(\nu M+1)}}\Bigg(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
\Big\|
u_M^{\rm H} \big( Y_1,\ldots, Y_M \big)
\Big\|_{L_\nu}
}{
\Big\|
u_M^{\rm H} \big( Y_1,\ldots, Y_M \big)
\Big\|_{L_2}
}$}}\Bigg)^{\frac{\nu}{\nu M+1}}+Cm\Bigg(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
\sum_{j \neq M} \sigma^2_{m,n;j}
}{
\sigma^2_{m,n;M}
}$}}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2M+1}}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;Cm\Big(n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2(\nu M+1)}}\tilde{\beta}_{M,\nu}^{\frac{\nu}{\nu M+1}}+\rho_{m,n;M}^{\frac{1}{2M+1}}\Big)

for some absolute constant
C>0
. This proves the first bound. The Gaussian approximation bound is obtained by combining the above with
Proposition

4
. ∎

Lemma 32
.

(
35
) holds.

Write
W_{j}=V^{K}_{j}
for
j<i
,
W_{i}=0
and
W_{j}=\xi^{(K)}_{j}
for
j>i
. Then we can express

\displaystyle\big\|\partial_{i}\,U_{M}^{(K)}(

\displaystyle V^{K}_{1},\ldots,V^{K}_{i-1},\mathbf{0},\xi^{(K)}_{i+1},\ldots,\xi^{(K)}_{n})X_{i}\big\|_{L_{\nu}}^{\nu}\;=\;\big\|\partial_{i}\,U^{(K)}_{M}(W_{1},\ldots,W_{n})V^{K}_{i}\big\|_{L_{\nu}}^{\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(n-m)!}{n!}$}}\Big)^{\nu}m\,\bigg\|\msum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1},\ldots,j_{m-1}\\
\text{\rm distinct and in }[n]\setminus\{i\}\end{subarray}}\,\langle T_{m},V^{K}_{i}\otimes W_{j_{1}}\otimes\ldots\otimes\ldots\otimes W_{j_{m-1}}\rangle\bigg\|_{L_{\nu}}^{\nu}\;,

where we used the symmetry of
T_{m}
in the last equality. Now by the independence of
W_{j}
’s and
V^{K}_{i}
and noting that
\mathbb{E}\,W_{i}=0
and
\nu\in(2,3]
, we can use
Lemma

17
repeatedly to get

\displaystyle\big\|\partial_{i}\,U_{M}^{(K)}(

\displaystyle V^{K}_{1},\ldots,V^{K}_{i-1},\mathbf{0},\xi^{(K)}_{i+1},\ldots,\xi^{(K)}_{n})X_{i}\big\|_{L_{\nu}}^{\nu}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;(C^{\prime})^{m}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(n-m)!}{n!}$}}\Big)^{\nu}m\,\bigg\|\msum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1},\ldots,j_{m-1}\\
\text{\rm distinct and in }[n]\setminus\{i\}\end{subarray}}\,\langle T_{m},V^{K}_{i}\otimes V^{K}_{j_{1}}\otimes\ldots\otimes V^{K}_{j_{m-1}}\rangle\bigg\|_{L_{\nu}}^{\nu}

for some absolute constant
C^{\prime}>0
.
Denote
\|{\,\vbox{\hbox{$\bullet$}}\,}\|_{L_{\nu}|i}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}[{\,\vbox{\hbox{$\bullet$}}\,}|V^{K}_{i}]
,
V^{(-i)}_{j}\coloneqq V^{K}_{j}
for
j<i
,
V^{(-i)}_{j}\coloneqq V^{K}_{j+1}
for
j\geq i
and
V^{(-i)}=(V^{(-i)}_{j})_{j\leq n-1}
. We can further express the above as

\displaystyle\;(C^{\prime})^{m}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{m}{n^\nu}$}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big\|\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(n-m)!}{(n-1)!}$}}\msum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1},\ldots,j_{m-1}\\
\text{\rm distinct and in }[n]\setminus\{i\}\end{subarray}}\,\langle T_{m},V^{K}_{i}\otimes V^{K}_{j_{1}}\otimes\ldots\otimes V^{K}_{j_{m-1}}\rangle\Big\|_{L_{\nu}|i}^{\nu}\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad\;\eqqcolon\;(C^{\prime})^{m}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{m}{n^\nu}$}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big\|u^{(i)}_{m-1}\big(V^{(-i)}\big)\Big\|_{L_{\nu}|i}^{\nu}\bigg]\;.

Conditioning on
V^{K}_{i}
,
u^{(i)}_{m-1}(V^{(-i)})
is now a degree
m-1
degenerate U-statistic of
n-1
i.i.d. zero-mean random vectors,
V^{(-i)}_{1},\ldots,V^{(-i)}_{n-1}
. By a standard moment bound on degenerate U-statistics (Theorem 1.1 of
[
23
]
), there is some absolute constant
C^{\prime\prime}>0
such that

\displaystyle\Big\|u^{(i)}_{m-1}\big(V^{(-i)}\big)\Big\|_{L_{\nu}|i}

\displaystyle\leq(C^{\prime\prime})^{m-1}\,\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{n}{m-1}$}}\Bigr)^{-1}n^{\frac{m-1}{2}}\Big(\mprod_{l=1}^{m-1}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2}{(l-1) \nu+ 2}$}}\Big)\big\|\big\langle T_{m},V^{K}_{i}\otimes V^{(-i)}_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes V^{(-i)}_{m-1}\big\rangle\big\|_{L_{\nu}|i}

\displaystyle\leq(C^{\prime\prime})^{m-1}\,n^{\frac{m-1}{2}}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(m-1)^{m-1}}{n^{m-1}}$}}\Big(\mprod_{l=1}^{m-1}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\nu}{(l-1) \nu+ \nu}$}}\Big)\big\|\big\langle T_{m},V^{K}_{i}\otimes V^{(-i)}_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes V^{(-i)}_{m-1}\big\rangle\big\|_{L_{\nu}|i}

\displaystyle=(C^{\prime\prime})^{m-1}\,n^{-\frac{m-1}{2}}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(m-1)^{m-1}}{(m-1)!}$}}\big\|\big\langle T_{m},V^{K}_{i}\otimes V^{(-i)}_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes V^{(-i)}_{m-1}\big\rangle\big\|_{L_{\nu}|i}

\displaystyle\leq(C^{\prime\prime})^{m-1}\,n^{-\frac{m-1}{2}}e^{m-1}\big\|\big\langle T_{m},V^{K}_{i}\otimes V^{(-i)}_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes V^{(-i)}_{m-1}\big\rangle\big\|_{L_{\nu}|i}\qquad\qquad\text{ almost surely }\,,

where we have used Stirling’s approximation of the factorial in the last line. Combining the two bounds finishes the proof.
∎

Proofs for Section
3.3

The results on vertex-level fluctuations concern a U-statistic of i.i.d. variables, which has already been studied in
Proposition

10
.

The first bound follows directly from the proof of
Proposition

10
in
Section

10.2
by replacing
u_{m}(Y)
by
\kappa_{1}(Y)
.
The variance computation follows from substituting
u_{1}
into the definition of
\sigma^{2}_{m,n;j}
in
Proposition

10
, and computing

\displaystyle\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{m}{r}$}}\Bigr)^{2}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{n}{m}$}}\Bigr)^{2}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{n}{r}$}}\Bigr)^{-1}\;=

\displaystyle\;\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{m}{r}$}}\Bigr)\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{n}{m}$}}\Bigr)\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(m!) (n!) (r!) (n-r)!}{(r!) ((m-r)!) (m!) ((n-m)!) (n!)}$}}\;=\;\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{m}{r}$}}\Bigr)\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{n}{m}$}}\Bigr)\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{n-r}{m-r}$}}\Bigr)\;.

∎

By the variance formula in
Corollary

11
and the fact that
w
is bounded,
\sigma^{2}_{m,n;r}=O(n^{2m-r})
for all
r\in[m]
, which implies (i)
\Leftrightarrow
(ii). We also have (ii)
\Leftrightarrow
(iii) in view of the formula in
Corollary

11
. To prove (iii)
\Leftrightarrow
(iv), recall that
|\textrm{Aut}(H)|
is the number of automorphisms of
H
, and let
P_{m}
be the set of permutations of
\{1,\ldots,m\}
. Also denote
P_{m}^{i\rightarrow 1}\subseteq P_{m}
as the set of permutations that sends
\{i\}
to
\{1\}
. Then we can write

\displaystyle\;\msum_{H^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{G}_{H}(\{1,\ldots,m\})}\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\mprod_{(i_{s},i_{t})\in E(H^{\prime})}w(U_{i_{s}},U_{i_{t}})\,\Big|\,U_{1}=x\Big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{|\textrm{Aut}(H)|}$}}\;\msum_{\sigma\in P_{m}}\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\mprod_{(i_{s},i_{t})\in E(H)}w\big(U_{\sigma(i_{s})},U_{\sigma(i_{t})}\big)\,\Big|\,U_{1}=x\Big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{|\textrm{Aut}(H)|}$}}\;\msum_{\sigma\in P_{m}}\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\mprod_{(i_{s},i_{t})\in E(H)}w\big(U_{i_{s}},U_{i_{t}}\big)\,\Big|\,U_{\sigma^{-1}(1)}=x\Big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{|\textrm{Aut}(H)|}$}}\;\msum_{i=1}^{m}\msum_{\sigma\in P_{m}^{i\rightarrow 1}}\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\mprod_{(i_{s},i_{t})\in E(H)}w\big(U_{i_{s}},U_{i_{t}}\big)\,\Big|\,U_{i}=x\Big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(m-1)!}{|\textrm{Aut}(H)|}$}}\;\msum_{i=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\Big[\mprod_{(i_{s},i_{t})\in E(H)}w\big(U_{i_{s}},U_{i_{t}}\big)\,\Big|\,U_{i}=x\Big]\;.

(iii) says the above is constant for almost every
x\in[0,1]
, whereas (iv) is equivalent to requiring that

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{m}$}}\msum_{i=1}^{m}\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\mprod_{(i_{s},i_{t})\in E(H)}w(U_{i_{s}},U_{i_{t}})\,\Big|\,U_{i}=x\Big]

is constant for almost every
x\in[0,1]
. This proves that (iii)
\Leftrightarrow
(iv).
∎

To prove the results on the edge-level fluctuations, we first present a stronger lemma, which will imply
Lemma

14
and greatly simplify the proof of
Proposition

13
. For
i\in[n_{*}]
, write
W^{(i)}_{j}\coloneqq\bar{Y}_{j}
for
j\leq i
and
W^{(i)}_{j}\coloneqq Z_{j}
for
j>i
. Denote
e_{j}
as the edge in
K_{n}
indexed by
j\in[n_{*}]
. Let
I
and
E
be edge sets. Recall that
\delta_{H}(I)
is the indicator of whether the graph formed by
I
is isomorphic to
H
. Also define
n_{H}(k,I,E)
as the number of subgraphs of
K_{n}
that are isomorphic to
H
and can be formed with
k
edges from
I
and all edges from
E
.

Given
i\in[n_{*}]
,
0\leq k^{\prime}\leq k
, the edge subsets
I\subseteq[n_{*}]
with
|I|\geq k^{\prime}
and
E_{k-k^{\prime}}\subseteq E(K_{n_{*}})
with
|E_{k-k^{\prime}}|=k-k^{\prime}
, as well as some random variable
W
that are either zero-mean or constant almost surely and are independent of
\{W^{(i)}_{j}\,|\,j\in I\}
, we define

\displaystyle S^{(i)}(k^{\prime},I,E_{k-k^{\prime}},W)\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\msum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1},\ldots,j_{k^{\prime}}\,\in\,I\\
j_{1}<\ldots<\,j_{k^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}\;\delta_{H}(\{e_{j_{l}}\}_{l\in[k^{\prime}]}\cup E_{k-k^{\prime}})\,W\mprod_{l\in[k^{\prime}]}W^{(i)}_{j_{l}}\;\;\;\text{ for }k^{\prime}\geq 1\;,

\displaystyle S^{(i)}(0,I,E_{k},W)\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\,\delta_{H}(E_{k})\,W\;.

Notice that
\kappa_{2}(\bar{Y})=S^{(n_{*})}(k,[n_{*}],\emptyset,1)
. The next lemma controls the conditional moments of these quantities given
U
, by exploiting that
\bar{Y}_{j}
’s and
Z_{j}
’s are conditionally independent and zero-mean given
U
:

Lemma 33
.

There are some absolute constants
c,C>0
and
\nu\in(2,3]
such that almost surely

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big|S^{(i)}(k^{\prime},I,E_{k-k^{\prime}},W)\big|^{\nu}\,\big|\,U\big]\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{k^{\prime}}\,\big(n_{H}(k^{\prime},I,E_{k-k^{\prime}})\big)^{\nu/2}\;\mathbb{E}[|W|^{\nu}\,|\,U\,]\,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\max\nolimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},\ldots,i_{k^{\prime}}\in I\\
\text{ all distinct}\end{subarray}}\;\mprod_{l=1}^{k^{\prime}}\,\mathbb{E}\big[|X_{i_{l}}|^{\nu}\,\big|\,U\,\big]\;,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big|S^{(n_{*})}(k^{\prime},I,E_{k-k^{\prime}},W)\big|^{2}\,\big|\,U\big]\;\geq

\displaystyle\;c^{k^{\prime}}\,n_{H}(k^{\prime},I,E_{k-k^{\prime}})\;\mathbb{E}[|W|^{2}\,|\,U\,]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\max\nolimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},\ldots,i_{k^{\prime}}\in I\\
\textrm{all distinct}\end{subarray}}\;\mprod_{l=1}^{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\big[|X_{i_{l}}|^{2}\,\big|\,U\big]\;.

The proof proceeds by induction on
k\geq 0
. We claim that the constant in the upper bound is specified by
C=C_{1}C_{2}
, where
C_{1}
is the maximum of the absolute constant
C^{\prime}_{\nu}
in the second upper bound of
Lemma

31
over
\nu\in[2,3]
, and
C_{2}
is the absolute constant in
Lemma

17
. The constant
c
in the lower bound is the absolute constant in the lower bound of
Lemma

31
with
\nu=2
.

The base case
k^{\prime}=0
is straightforward by noting that
\delta_{H}(E_{k})=n_{H}(0,I,E_{k})
for all
I\subseteq[n_{*}]
. Suppose that the inductive statement holds for
k^{\prime}-1
. To prove the statement for
k^{\prime}
, we enumerate the elements of
I
as
i_{1}<\ldots<i_{n^{\prime}}
and consider a martingale difference sequence
(S_{l^{\prime}})_{l^{\prime}=1}^{n^{\prime}}
conditioning on
U
: For
l^{\prime}\in[n^{\prime}]
, define

\displaystyle S^{(i)}_{l^{\prime}}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\big[S^{(i)}(k^{\prime},I,E_{k-k^{\prime}},W)\,\big|\,U,W,W^{(i)}_{i_{1}},\ldots,W^{(i)}_{i_{l^{\prime}}}\big]

\displaystyle\;-\mathbb{E}\big[S^{(i)}(k^{\prime},I,E_{k-k^{\prime}},W)\,\big|\,U,W,W^{(i)}_{i_{1}},\ldots,W^{(i)}_{i_{l^{\prime}-1}}\big]\;.

Since
(W^{(i)}_{i_{j}})_{j\leq l^{\prime}}
are zero-mean and
(W^{(i)}_{i_{j}})_{j\leq l^{\prime}}\cup\{W\}
are independent conditioning on
U
, for
k^{\prime}\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[S^{(i)}(k^{\prime},I,E_{k-k^{\prime}},W)\,\big|\,U,W\big]\;=\;0\qquad\text{ almost surely }.

This allows us to express, almost surely,

\displaystyle S^{(i)}(k^{\prime},I,E_{k-k^{\prime}},W)\;=\;\msum_{l^{\prime}=1}^{n^{\prime}}S^{(i)}_{l^{\prime}}\;.

Now notice that for
l^{\prime}<k^{\prime}
,
S^{(i)}_{l^{\prime}}=0
almost surely. Since the non-zero terms in each difference
S^{(i)}_{l^{\prime}}
must involve
W^{(i)}_{i_{l^{\prime}}}
, for
l^{\prime}\geq k^{\prime}>1
, we have that almost surely

\displaystyle S^{(i)}_{l^{\prime}}\;=

\displaystyle\;\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1},\ldots,j_{k^{\prime}-1}\,\in\,\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l^{\prime}-1}\}\\
j_{1}<\ldots<\,j_{k^{\prime}-1}\end{subarray}}\;\delta_{H}(\{e_{j_{l}}\}_{l\in[k^{\prime}-1]}\cup E_{k-k^{\prime}}\cup\{e_{i_{l^{\prime}}}\})\,W\,W^{(i)}_{i_{l^{\prime}}}\mprod_{l\in[k^{\prime}-1]}W^{(i)}_{j_{l}}\;,

and for
l^{\prime}\geq k^{\prime}=1
, we have that almost surely

\displaystyle S^{(i)}_{l^{\prime}}\;=

\displaystyle\;\delta_{H}(E_{k-k^{\prime}}\cup\{e_{i_{l^{\prime}}}\})\,W\,W^{(i)}_{i_{l^{\prime}}}\;.

This in particular implies that for
l^{\prime}\geq k^{\prime}
, almost surely

\displaystyle S^{(i)}_{l^{\prime}}\;=

\displaystyle\;S^{(i)}\big(k^{\prime}-1\,,\,\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l^{\prime}-1}\}\,,\,E_{k-k^{\prime}}\cup\{e_{i_{l^{\prime}}}\}\,,\,W\,W^{(i)}_{i_{l^{\prime}}}\big)\;,

(36)

Now by the martingale moment bound in
Lemma

31
with
\nu\in[2,3]
, we get the almost sure bound

\displaystyle c\,\msum_{l^{\prime}=k^{\prime}}^{n^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}[|S^{(i)}_{l^{\prime}}|^{\nu}\,|\,U\,]\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\big[\big|S^{(i)}(k^{\prime},I,E_{k-k^{\prime}},W)\big|^{\nu}\,\big|\,U\,\big]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C_{1}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\msum_{l^{\prime}=k^{\prime}}^{n^{\prime}}|S^{(i)}_{l^{\prime}}|^{2}\big)^{\nu/2}\,\Big|\,U\Big]\;,

(37)

and since
W\times W^{(i)}_{i_{l^{\prime}}}
is independent of
W^{(i)}_{i_{1}},\ldots,W^{(i)}_{i_{l^{\prime}-1}}
, we may apply the inductive statement to control the individual
S^{(i)}_{l^{\prime}}
term. Now notice that the moment bound on each
S^{(i)}_{l^{\prime}}
will introduce a constant
w_{l^{\prime}}\coloneqq n_{H}(k^{\prime}-1,\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l^{\prime}-1}\},E_{k-k^{\prime}}\cup\{e_{i_{l}^{\prime}}\})
. Denote their sum as
w_{H}\coloneqq\sum_{l^{\prime}=k^{\prime}}^{n^{\prime}}w_{l^{\prime}}
, which satisfies

\displaystyle w_{H}\;=\;n_{H}(k^{\prime},I,E_{k-k^{\prime}})\;.

(38)

By reweighting the sum in the upper bound in (
37
) and a Jensen’s inequality, almost surely

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big|S^{(i)}(k^{\prime},I,E_{k-k^{\prime}},W)\big|^{\nu}\,\big|\,U\,\big]\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C_{1}\,(w_{H})^{\nu/2}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\msum_{l^{\prime}=k^{\prime}}^{n^{\prime}}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{w_{l'}}{w_H}$}}\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{w_{l'}}$}}|S^{(i)}_{l^{\prime}}|^{2}\Big)^{\nu/2}\,\Big|\,U\,\Big]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C_{1}\,(w_{H})^{\nu/2}\,\msum_{l^{\prime}=k^{\prime}}^{n^{\prime}}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{w_{l'}}{w_H}$}}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{w_{l'}}$}}|S^{(i)}_{l^{\prime}}|^{2}\Big)^{\nu/2}\,\Big|\,U\Big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;C_{1}\,(w_{H})^{\nu/2}\,\msum_{l^{\prime}=k^{\prime}}^{n^{\prime}}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{w_{l'}}{w_H}$}}\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\mean\big[ \big| S^{(i)}_{l'} \big|^\nu\,\big|\, U \big]}{w_{l'}^{\nu/2}}$}}\;.

By applying the upper bound in the inductive statement to (
36
), we have that almost surely

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big|S^{(i)}_{l^{\prime}}\big|^{\nu}\,\big|\,U\big]

\displaystyle\leq\;C^{k^{\prime}-1}\,w_{l^{\prime}}^{\nu/2}\,\mathbb{E}\big[\big|W\times W^{(i)}_{i_{l^{\prime}}}\big|^{\nu}\,\big|\,U\big]\,

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt\times\max\nolimits_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{k^{\prime}-1}\in\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l^{\prime}-1}\}\text{ distinct}}\,\mprod_{l=1}^{k^{\prime}-1}\mathbb{E}\big[|X_{j_{l}}|^{\nu}\,\big|\,U\,\big]

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\;C_{2}C^{k^{\prime}-1}\,w_{l^{\prime}}^{\nu/2}\,\mathbb{E}[|W|^{\nu}|U]\;\mathbb{E}[|X_{i_{l^{\prime}}}|^{\nu}|U]\,

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt\times\max_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{k^{\prime}-1}\in\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l^{\prime}-1}\}\text{ distinct}}\,\mprod_{l=1}^{k^{\prime}-1}\mathbb{E}\big[|X_{j_{l}}|^{\nu}\,\big|\,U\big]

\displaystyle\leq\;C_{2}C^{k^{\prime}-1}\,w_{l^{\prime}}^{\nu/2}\,\mathbb{E}[|W|^{\nu}\,|\,U]\,\max\nolimits_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k^{\prime}}\in I\text{ distinct}}\,\mprod_{l=1}^{k^{\prime}}\,\mathbb{E}\big[|X_{i_{l}}|^{\nu}\,|\,U\big]\;,

where we have noted that
W
is independent of
W^{(i)}_{i_{l^{\prime}}}
and used
Lemma

17
in
(a)
to replace
W^{(i)}_{i_{l^{\prime}}}
by
X_{i_{l^{\prime}}}
. By noting that
C_{*}=C_{1}C_{2}
and using (
38
), we get that almost surely

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big|S^{(i)}(k^{\prime},I,

\displaystyle E_{k-k^{\prime}},W)\big|^{\nu}\,\big|\,U\big]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{k^{\prime}}\,(w_{H})^{\nu/2}\,\msum_{l^{\prime}=k^{\prime}}^{n^{\prime}}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{w_{l'}}{w_H}$}}\,\mathbb{E}[|W|^{\nu}\,|\,U]\,\max_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k^{\prime}}\in I\text{ distinct}}\,\mprod_{l=1}^{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}[|X_{i_{l}}|^{\nu}\,|\,U]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;C^{k^{\prime}}\,(n_{H}(k^{\prime},I,E_{k-k^{\prime}}))^{\nu/2}\,\mathbb{E}[|W|^{\nu}\,|\,U]\,\max\nolimits_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k^{\prime}}\in I\text{ distinct}}\,\mprod_{l=1}^{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}[|X_{i_{l}}|^{\nu}]\;,

which proves the upper bound. Similarly by the lower bound in the inductive statement and noting that we do not need to use
Lemma

17
, we get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|S^{(n_{*})}(k^{\prime},I,

\displaystyle E_{k-k^{\prime}},W)|^{2}\,|\,U]

\displaystyle\;\geq

\displaystyle\;c^{k^{\prime}}\,n_{H}(k^{\prime},I,E_{k-k^{\prime}})\,\mathbb{E}[|W|^{2}\,|\,U]\,\min\nolimits_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k^{\prime}}\in I\text{ distinct}}\,\mprod_{l=1}^{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}[|X_{i_{l}}|^{2}\,|\,U]\;.

which proves the lower bound.
∎

Since
\mathbb{E}[\kappa_{2}(\bar{Y})|U]=0
almost surely, by the law of total variance,

\displaystyle\text{\rm Var}[\kappa_{2}(\bar{Y})]\;=\;\mathbb{E}\,\text{\rm Var}[\kappa_{2}(\bar{Y})|U]\;=\;\mathbb{E}\,\Big\|\msum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}\in[n_{*}]\\
j_{1}<\ldots<\,j_{k}\end{subarray}}\;\delta_{H}\big(\{e_{j_{l}}\}_{l\in[k]}\big)\,\mprod_{l\in[k]}\bar{Y}_{l}\Big\|_{L_{2}|U}^{2}\;,

where we have denoted
\|{\,\vbox{\hbox{$\bullet$}}\,}\|_{L_{2}|U}^{2}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}[|{\,\vbox{\hbox{$\bullet$}}\,}|^{2}|\,U]
. Since
(\bar{Y}_{ij})_{(i,j)\in E(K_{n})}
is a collection of
n_{*}
random variables that are conditionally independent and zero-mean given
U
, the quantity inside the conditional norm can be identified with
S^{(n_{*})}(k,[n_{*}],\emptyset,1)
. Applying
Lemma

33
conditionally on
U
, we get that for some absolute constants
c,C>0
, almost surely,

\displaystyle\text{\rm Var}[\kappa_{2}(\bar{Y})|U]\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{k}\,n_{H}(k,[n_{*}],\emptyset)\,\max\nolimits_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\in[n_{*}]\text{ distinct}}\,\mprod_{l=1}^{k}\text{\rm Var}[\bar{Y}_{i_{l}}\,|\,U]\;,

\displaystyle\text{\rm Var}[\kappa_{2}(\bar{Y})|U]\;\geq

\displaystyle\;c^{k}\,n_{H}(k,[n_{*}],\emptyset)\,\min\nolimits_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\in[n_{*}]\text{ distinct}}\,\mprod_{l=1}^{k}\ \text{\rm Var}[\bar{Y}_{i_{l}}\,|\,U]\;.

Noting that
n(k,[n_{*}],\emptyset)=|\mathcal{G}_{H}([n])|
and taking an expectation yield the desired bounds.
∎

Observe that
\kappa_{2}(\bar{Y})
is multilinear in
(\bar{Y}_{i})_{i\in[n_{*}]}
and a degree-
k
polynomial. Moreover, as
\bar{Y}_{ij}
’s are conditionally centred given
U
,
\mathbb{E}[\kappa_{2}(\bar{Y})\,|\,U]=0
. Since
\text{\rm Var}[\kappa_{2}(Z)|U]>0
almost surely by assumption, by applying
Theorem

1
and noting that the bounding constant is absolute, we get that for some absolute constant
C^{\prime}>0
, almost surely

\displaystyle\sup\nolimits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\Big|\mathbb{P}\big(\kappa_{2}(\bar{Y})

\displaystyle\leq\;t\,\big|\,U\big)-\mathbb{P}\big(\kappa_{2}(Z)\leq t\,\big|\,U\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime}m\Bigg(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
\sum_{i=1}^{n_*}
\mean\big[
\partial_i \kappa_2(\bar Y_1, \ldots, \bar Y_{i-1}, 0, Z_{i+1}, \ldots, Z_{n_*})
\, \bar Y_i
\big|^\nu\,\big|\, U \,\big]
}{
\Var[ \kappa_2(\bar Y) \,|\, U \,]^{\nu/2}
}$}}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{\nu k+1}}\;.

(39)

Lemma

33
implies that, for some absolute constants
C_{*},c_{*}>0
, we have that almost surely

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\partial_{i}

\displaystyle\kappa_{2}(\bar{Y}_{1},\ldots,\bar{Y}_{i-1},0,Z_{i+1},\ldots,Z_{n_{*}})\,\bar{Y}_{i}\big|^{\nu}\,\big|\,U\,\big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\msum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1},\ldots,j_{k-1}\in[n_{*}]\setminus\{i\}\\
j_{1}<\ldots<\,j_{k-1}\end{subarray}}\;\delta_{H}\big(\{e_{j_{l}}\}_{l\in[k-1]}\cup\{e_{i}\}\big)\,W^{(i)}_{i}\mprod_{l\in[k-1]}W^{(i)}_{j_{l}}\Big|^{\nu}\,\Big|\,U\,\Big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}[\,|S^{(i)}(k-1,[n_{*}]\setminus\{i\},\{e_{i}\},\bar{Y}_{i})|^{\nu}\,|\,U]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;(C_{*})^{k-1}\,\big(n_{H}(k-1,[n_{*}]\setminus\{i\},\{e_{i}\})\big)^{\nu/2}\,\mathbb{E}\big[\,\big|\bar{Y}_{i}\big|^{\nu}\,\big|\,U\big]\,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\max\nolimits_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k-1}\in[n_{*}]\setminus\{i\}\text{ distinct}}\,\mprod_{l=1}^{k-1}\mathbb{E}\big[\big|\bar{Y}_{i_{l}}\big|^{\nu}\,\big|\,U\big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;(C_{*})^{k-1}\,\big(n_{H}(k-1,[n_{*}]\setminus\{i\},\{e_{i}\})\big)^{\nu/2}\,\max\nolimits_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\in[n_{*}]\text{ distinct}}\,\mprod_{l=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}\big[\big|\bar{Y}_{i_{l}}\big|^{\nu}\,\big|\,U\big]\;,

and

\displaystyle\text{\rm Var}[\kappa_{2}(\bar{Y})\,|\,U]\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\msum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}\in[n_{*}]\\
j_{1}<\ldots<\,j_{k}\end{subarray}}\;\delta_{H}\big(\{e_{j_{l}}\}_{l\in[k]}\big)\,\mprod_{l\in[k]}W^{(n_{*})}_{j_{l}}\Big|^{2}\,\Big|\,U\,\Big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\big[\big|S^{(n_{*})}(k,[n_{*}],\emptyset,1)\big|^{2}\big|\,U\,\big]

\displaystyle\;\geq

\displaystyle\;(c_{*})^{k}\,n_{H}(k,[n_{*}],\emptyset)\,\min\nolimits_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k^{\prime}}\in[n_{*}]\text{ distinct}}\,\mprod_{l=1}^{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}[|\bar{Y}_{i_{l}}|^{2}\,|\,U]\;.

Now recall that
[n_{*}]
indexes all edges of an complete graph
K_{n}
, whereas
n_{H}(k-1,[n_{*}]\setminus\{i\},\{e_{i}\})
counts the number of subgraphs in
K_{n}
that contains
e_{i}
and is isomorphic to
H
. By symmetry,
n_{H}(k-1,[n_{*}]\setminus\{i\},\{e_{i}\})
is the same for all
i\in[n_{*}]
, and

\displaystyle\msum_{i=1}^{n_{*}}n_{H}(k-1,[n_{*}]\setminus\{i\},\{e_{i}\})\;=\;k\,|\mathcal{G}_{H}([n])|\;=\;k\,\big(n_{H}(k,[n_{*}],\emptyset)\big)\;,

since each subgraph of
K_{n}
that is isomorphic to
H
has been counted exactly
k=|H|
times. Thus

\displaystyle\msum_{i=1}^{n_{*}}\big(n_{H}(k-1,[n_{*}]\setminus\{i\},\{e_{i}\})\big)^{\nu/2}\;=

\displaystyle\;\msum_{i=1}^{n_{*}}\big(n_{H}(k-1,[n_{*}]\setminus\{1\},\{e_{1}\})\big)^{\nu/2}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;n_{*}\big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{k}{n_*}$}}\,|\mathcal{G}_{H}([n])|)^{\nu/2}\;.

Combining the above, we get that for some absolute constants
C^{\prime\prime},C>0
, almost surely

\displaystyle\sup\nolimits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{P}\big(\kappa_{2}(\bar{Y})\leq t\big)-\mathbb{P}\big(\kappa_{2}(Z)\leq t\,\big|\,U\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime\prime}m\Bigg(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
\sum_{i=1}^{n_*}
\big( n_H(k-1, [n_*] \setminus\{i\}, \{e_i\} ) \big)^{\nu/2}
}{
| \cG_H([n]) |^{\nu/2 } \,
}$}}\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\mmax_{i_1, \ldots, i_k \in[n_*] \text{ distinct}} \, \mprod_{l=1}^{k}
\mean[ | \bar Y_{i_l} |^\nu\,|\, U ]}{\mmin_{i_1, \ldots, i_{k'} \in[n_*] \text{ distinct}} \, \mprod_{l=1}^{k'}
\mean[ | \bar Y_{i_l} |^2 \,|\, U] }$}}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{\nu k+1}}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime\prime}m\bigg(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
\big( \sum_{i=1}^{n_*}
n_H(k-1, [n_*] \setminus\{i\}, \{e_i\} ) \big)^{\nu/2}
}{
| \cG_H([n]) |^{\nu/2 } \,
}$}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{\nu k+1}}\rho_{\bar{Y}}(U)^{\frac{1}{\nu k+1}}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime\prime}m\,k^{\frac{\nu}{2\nu k+2}}\,n_{*}^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2\nu k+2}}\,\rho_{\bar{Y}}(U)^{\frac{1}{\nu k+1}}\;\leq\;Cm\,n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{\nu k+1}}\,\rho_{\bar{Y}}(U)^{\frac{1}{\nu k+1}}\;.

In the last line, we have noted that
n_{*}=\binom{n}{2}\leq n^{2}
. This proves the first bound. The second bound follows by applying
Proposition

4
while conditioning on
U
.
∎

The appendices contain additional examples, a discussion on
1
and additional details on Senatov’s results
[
54
]
used in our proof of
Theorem

3
.

Additional results

A toy degree-three V-statistic

Given a feature map
\phi:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
and i.i.d.
\mathbb{R}^{d}
-valued random vectors
Y_{i}
’s, consider a V-statistic

\displaystyle v(Y)\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n^3}$}}\msum_{i,j,k\leq n}\phi(Y_{i})\phi(Y_{j})\phi(Y_{k})\;.

Write
\mu_{r}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}[X_{1}^{r}]
. Since
v(Y)
is a degree-three polynomial in the random variables
X_{i}\coloneqq\phi(Y_{i})
,
Theorem

1
approximates
p(X)\coloneqq v(Y)
by replacing the
\mathbb{R}^{3}
block tensors

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}_{i}\;=\;\Big(\bar{X}_{i}\,,\,\overline{X_{i}^{2}}\,,\,\overline{X_{i}^{3}}\,\Big)\;=\;\big(X_{i}-\mu_{1}\,,\,X_{i}^{2}-\mu_{2}\,,\,X_{i}^{3}-\mu_{3}\big)

by Gaussian surrogates
\xi_{i}
with the same mean and variance as
\mathbf{X}_{i}
. To understand the structure of the multilinear representation
q(\mathbf{X})
defined in (
3
), we consider the decomposition

\displaystyle v(Y)

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\,v(Y)\;=\;p(X)-\mathbb{E}\,p(X)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n^3}$}}\msum_{i,j,k\leq n}X_{i}X_{j}X_{k}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(n-1)(n-2)}{n^2}$}}\mu_{1}^{3}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3(n-1)}{n^2}$}}\mu_{1}\mu_{2}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n^2}$}}\mu_{3}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n^3}$}}\msum_{i,j,k\text{ distinct }}X_{i}X_{j}X_{k}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(n-1)(n-2)}{n^2}$}}\mu_{1}^{3}\Big)+\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3}{n^3}$}}\msum_{i\neq j}X_{i}^{2}X_{j}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3(n-1)}{n^2}$}}\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\;+\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n^3}$}}\msum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}^{3}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n^2}$}}\mu_{3}\Big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3(n-1)}{n^2}$}}\msum_{i\leq n}\mu_{1}^{2}\bar{X}_{j}\;+\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3}{n^2}$}}\msum_{i,j\text{ distinct }}\mu_{1}\bar{X}_{i}\bar{X}_{j}\;+\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n^3}$}}\msum_{i,j,k\text{ distinct }}\bar{X}_{i}\bar{X}_{j}\bar{X}_{k}

\displaystyle\;+\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3}{n^2}$}}\msum_{i=1}^{n}\overline{X_{i}^{2}}\mu_{1}\;+\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3}{n^2}$}}\msum_{i=1}^{n}\mu_{2}\bar{X}_{i}\;+\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3}{n^3}$}}\msum_{i\neq j}\overline{X_{i}^{2}}\bar{X}_{j}\;+\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n^3}$}}\msum_{i=1}^{n}\overline{X_{i}^{3}}

\displaystyle\;\eqqcolon

\displaystyle\;q_{1}(\mathbf{X})+q_{2}(\mathbf{X})+q_{3}(\mathbf{X})+q_{4}(\mathbf{X})+q_{5}(\mathbf{X})+q_{6}(\mathbf{X})+q_{7}(\mathbf{X})\;\eqqcolon\;q(\mathbf{X})\;.

Note that
q_{1}(\mathbf{X})
,
q_{2}(\mathbf{X})
and
q_{3}(\mathbf{X})
correspond to the Hoeffding’s decomposition of the U-statistic associated with
p(X)=v(Y)
(see (
11
) in
Section

3.2
).

Theorem

1
gives the error of approximating
q(\mathbf{X})
by
q(\Xi)
. Variance domination (
Theorem

2
) says when we can make a further approximation by some
q_{l}(\Xi)
, depending on how the variances of
q_{1}(\mathbf{X}),\ldots,q_{7}(\mathbf{X})
compare: They can be computed respectively as

\displaystyle 3n^{-3}(n-1)^{2}\mu_{1}^{4}\,\text{\rm Var}[X_{1}]\;\eqqcolon

\displaystyle\;n^{-1}\sigma_{1}^{2}\;,

\displaystyle 3n^{-3}(n-1)\mu_{1}^{2}\,\text{\rm Var}[X_{1}]^{2}\;\eqqcolon

\displaystyle\;n^{-2}\sigma_{2}^{2}\;,

\displaystyle n^{-5}(n-1)(n-2)\,\text{\rm Var}[X_{1}]^{3}\;\eqqcolon

\displaystyle\;n^{-3}\sigma_{3}^{2}

\displaystyle 3n^{-3}\mu_{1}^{2}\,\text{\rm Var}[X_{1}^{2}]\;\eqqcolon

\displaystyle\;n^{-3}\sigma_{4}^{2}\;,

\displaystyle 3n^{-3}\mu_{2}^{2}\,\text{\rm Var}[X_{1}]\;\eqqcolon

\displaystyle\;n^{-3}\sigma_{5}^{2}\;,

\displaystyle 3n^{-5}(n-1)\,\text{\rm Var}[X_{1}^{2}]\text{\rm Var}[X_{1}]\;\eqqcolon

\displaystyle\;n^{-4}\sigma_{6}^{2}\;,

\displaystyle n^{-5}\text{\rm Var}[X_{1}^{3}]\;\eqqcolon

\displaystyle\;n^{-5}\sigma_{7}^{2}\;.

Theorem

2
then provides the error
of approximation by each
q_{l}(\Xi)
: For example, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\Big|\mathbb{P}\Big(

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{n^{1/2}}{\sigma_1}$}}\big(v(Y)-\mathbb{E}[v(Y)]\big)\leq t\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{n^{1/2}}{\sigma_1}$}}q_{1}(\Xi)\leq t\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C\Big(\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ n^{3/2} \sigma_2 + n (\sigma_3 + \sigma_4 + \sigma_5) + n^{1/2} \sigma_6 + \sigma_7 }{ n^2 \sigma_1 }$}}\Big)^{\frac{2}{3}}+n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2\nu+2}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \| \bar X_1 \|_{L_\nu} }{ \| \bar X_1 \|_{L_2}
}$}}\Big)^{\frac{\nu}{\nu+1}}\Big)\;,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\Big|\mathbb{P}\Big(

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{n}{\sigma_2}$}}\big(v(Y)-\mathbb{E}[v(Y)]\big)\leq t\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{n}{\sigma_2}$}}q_{2}(\Xi)\leq t\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C\Big(\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ n^2 \sigma_1 + n (\sigma_3 + \sigma_4 + \sigma_5) + n^{1/2} \sigma_6 + \sigma_7 }{ n^{3/2} \sigma_2 }$}}\Big)^{\frac{2}{5}}+n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{4\nu+2}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \| \bar X_1 \|_{L_\nu} }{ \| \bar X_1\|_{L_2} }$}}\Big)^{\frac{2\nu}{2\nu+1}}\Big)\;.

We may also characterise the different limits.
q_{1}(\Xi)
is a Gaussian. Writing
\bar{\xi}_{1}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i1}
, where
\xi_{i1}
is the first coordinate of
\xi_{i}
(corresponding to
\bar{X}_{i}
), we have

\displaystyle q_{2}(\Xi)\;=\;\big(\bar{\xi}_{1}\,\big)^{2}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}\msum_{i\leq n}\xi_{i1}^{2}\Big)\;\approx\;\big(\bar{\xi}_{1}\,\big)^{2}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}\mathbb{E}\big[\big(\bar{\xi}_{1}\,\big)^{2}\big]\;,

(40)

i.e.
q_{2}(\Xi)
asymptotically behaves like a centred and rescaled chi-square. An analogous argument shows that
q_{3}(\Xi)
behaves like a centred and rescaled cubic power of a Gaussian,
q_{6}(\Xi)
behaves like a centred and rescaled chi-square, and
q_{4}(\Xi)
,
q_{5}(\Xi)
and
q_{7}(\Xi)
are Gaussian.

Classically with
d
fixed and all
\sigma_{l}^{2}
bounded, the limit of
v(Y)
can be read off from variance domination:
q_{1}(\Xi)
always dominates when
\sigma_{1}\neq 0
,
q_{2}(\Xi)
dominates when
\sigma_{1}=0
and
\sigma_{2}\neq 0
, and so on. Meanwhile, provided that
X_{1}
is not constant almost surely, the only way to set some
\sigma_{l}
to zero is by requiring
\mu_{1}
to be zero. The only possible limits are therefore

(i)

the Gaussian limit given by
q_{1}(\Xi)
when
\mu_{1}\neq 0
;

(ii)

a mixture limit corresponding to
q_{3}(\Xi)+q_{5}(\Xi)
when
\mu_{1}=0
and
\mu_{2}\neq 0
.

Notably since
d
is fixed,
\delta
-regularity holds directly for the V-statistics associated with each
q_{l}(\mathbf{X})
, and
Lemma

7
allows these two limits to be expressed equivalently by replacing each
\xi_{i}
by
(Z_{i}-\mathbb{E}X_{i},Z_{i}^{2}-\mathbb{E}X_{i}^{2},Z_{i}^{3}-\mathbb{E}Z_{i}^{3})
. This agrees with classical results on asymptotics of non-degenerate (
\mu_{1}\neq 0
) and degenerate (
\mu_{1}=0
) V-statistics. We also remark that the presence of
q_{5}(\Xi)
in (ii) shows that the limit of a degenerate V-statistic differs from that of its associated degenerate U-statistic.

In the high-dimensional setting, however,
\sigma_{l}^{2}
are large relative to
n
through their dependence on
d\equiv d_{n}
. Since
d
is only an implicit parameter affecting the variances,
d
is allowed to be much larger than
n
(e.g. with exponential growth). In particular, we may have the mixture limit from
q_{3}(\Xi)+q_{5}(\Xi)
even if
\mu_{1}\neq 0
: It suffices to ask
\mu_{1}
to be asymptotically negligible, and the threshold for comparison is exactly given by variance domination (
Theorem

2
).

Meanwhile, if
q_{7}(\mathbf{X})
dominates, the implied limit from
q_{7}(\Xi)
(Gaussian universality with the augmented variables) is different from the limit given by replacing
X_{i}
’s with
Z_{i}
’s (Gaussian universality the original variables):

\displaystyle n^{5/2}q_{7}(\Xi)\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{n}}$}}\msum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i3}\;\sim\;\mathcal{N}(0,\text{\rm Var}[X_{1}^{3}])\;,

but

\displaystyle\text{\rm Var}\Big[\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{n}}$}}\msum_{i=1}^{n}(Z_{i}^{3}-\mathbb{E}X_{i}^{3})\Big]\;=

\displaystyle\;\text{\rm Var}[Z_{i}^{3}]\;=\;\mathbb{E}[X_{1}]^{3}+3\mathbb{E}[X_{1}]\text{\rm Var}[X_{1}]\;.

This is a case where
\delta
-regularity is violated, and the limits obtained from the two notions of Gaussian universality disagree. In view of the continuous mapping theorem applied to
v(Y)=\big(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}\big)^{3}
, we believe that
q_{7}(\mathbf{X})
never dominates in
v(Y)
for reasonable distributions of
X_{1}
. Notably if
X_{1}
is sufficiently light-tailed such that
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}
is asymptotically normal,
\text{\rm Var}[v(Y)]
is either
\Theta(n^{-1})
or
\Theta(n^{-3})
, i.e. larger than
\text{\rm Var}[q_{7}(\mathbf{X})]=\Theta(n^{-5})
.

Assumption
1
in
L_{2}

Denote
L_{2}(\mathcal{E},\mu)
as the
L_{2}
space of
\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
functions under
\mu
. We first show that
1
is very mild and holds almost for free when
\nu=2
.

Lemma 34
.

Fix
n,m,d\in\mathbb{N}
,
\nu\geq 1
and
(\mathcal{E},\mu)
a separable measure space. Assume that
\|u(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{m})\|_{L_{2}}<\infty
. Then there exists a sequence of orthonormal
L_{2}(\mathcal{E},\mu)
functions,
\{\phi_{k}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
, and an array of real values
\{\lambda_{k_{1}\ldots k_{m}}\}_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{m}=1}^{\infty}
, such that

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{K;2}\;=\;\Big\|\msum_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{m}=1}^{K}\lambda_{k_{1}\ldots k_{m}}\phi_{k_{1}}(Y_{1})\ldots\phi_{k_{m}}(Y_{m})-u(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{m})\Big\|_{L_{2}}\;\xrightarrow{K\rightarrow\infty}\;0\;.

Separability of
(\mathcal{E},\mu)
implies that
L_{2}(\mathcal{E},\mu)
is a separable Hilbert space (see e.g. Exercise 10(b), Chapter 1,
[
57
]
), which implies the existence of a countable orthonormal basis
\{\phi_{k}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
. Consider the
m
-fold product space
(\mathcal{E}^{m},\mu^{m})
. Then the collection of pointwise products
\{\phi_{k_{1}}\times\ldots\times\phi_{k_{m}}\}_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{m}=1}^{\infty}
forms an orthonormal basis in
L_{2}(\mathcal{E}^{m},\mu^{m})
. The stated assumption implies
u\in L_{2}(\mathcal{E}^{m},\mu^{m})
, which implies the desired result.
∎

Remark 20
.

We emphasise that the moment boundedness assumption is only required for every
fixed

n
,
m
and
d
. Therefore, this does not contradict our overall analysis, which considers how the moments can be large relative to
n
(through e.g. dependence on
d
and
m
).

In the next lemma, we give another choice of approximating functions provided that
u
is well-approximated by its Taylor expansion in
L_{\nu}
. For each
k\in K
, let
M_{k}\in\mathbb{N}
be the largest number such that

\displaystyle\msum_{M=1}^{M_{k}-1}d^{M}\;<\;k\;\leq\;\msum_{M=1}^{M_{k}}d^{M}\;.

For
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, let
(y^{\otimes M})_{t}
be the
t
-th coordinate of the tensor
y^{\otimes M}
according to a fixed total order on
\mathbb{N}^{m}
, and define

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{k}(y)\;\coloneqq\;\big(y^{\otimes M_{k}}\big)_{k-\sum_{M=1}^{M_{k}-1}d^{M}}\;.

For example,
\tilde{\phi}_{1}(y),\ldots,\tilde{\phi}_{d}(y)
are the
d
coordinates of
y
,
\tilde{\phi}_{d+1}(y),\ldots,\tilde{\phi}_{d+d^{2}}(y)
are the
d^{2}
coordinates of
y^{\otimes 2}
and so on.

Lemma 35
.

Fix
\nu\in(2,3]
. Assume that
u
is infinitely differentiable and
