\displaystyle\big\|u(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{m})-\msum_{l=0}^{L}\partial^{l}u(\mathbf{0})(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{m})^{\otimes l}\big\|_{L_{\nu}}\;\xrightarrow{L\rightarrow\infty}\;0\;.

Then
1
holds with
\{\tilde{\phi}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
defined above and some
\{\tilde{\lambda}_{k_{1}\ldots k_{m}}\}_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{m}\in\mathbb{N}}
.

Each coordinate of
(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{m})^{\otimes l}
can be written as a product of
\tilde{\phi}_{k_{1}}(Y_{1}),\ldots,\tilde{\phi}_{k_{m}}(Y_{m})
for some
k_{1},\ldots,k_{m}\leq\sum_{M=1}^{l}d^{M}
. By identifying
\tilde{\lambda}_{k_{1}\ldots k_{m}}
’s as the corresponding coordinate in
\partial^{l}u(\mathbf{0})
, we see that the Taylor approximation error above is exactly
\epsilon_{K;\nu}
, which converges to zero by assumption.
∎

Properties of univariate distributions in Theorem
3

Proof of Gaussian moment bound in Lemma
23

Since
\mathbb{E}|Z|^{\nu}=\pi^{-1/2}2^{\nu/2}\Gamma(\frac{\nu+1}{2})
, the proof boils down to approximating the Gamma function:
[
1
]
proves that for all
x\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\pi}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{x}{e}$}}\Big)^{x}\Big(8x^{3}+4x^{2}+x+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{100}$}}\Big)^{1/6}<\Gamma(x+1)<\sqrt{\pi}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{x}{e}$}}\Big)^{x}\Big(8x^{3}+4x^{2}+x+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{30}$}}\Big)^{1/6}\;.

Since
\nu\geq 1
,
\frac{\nu+1}{2}\geq 1
. As
8x^{3}+4x^{2}+x+\frac{1}{30}\leq 14(1+x)^{3}
for
x\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\Gamma\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\nu+1}{2}$}}\Big)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;14^{1/6}\sqrt{\pi}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\nu-1}{2e}$}}\Big)^{(\nu-1)/2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\nu+1}{2}$}}\Big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;14^{1/6}\sqrt{\pi}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\nu-1}{2}$}}\Big)^{\frac{\nu}{2}-1}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\nu^2 - 1}{4}$}}\Big)^{1/2}\;\leq\;14^{1/6}\sqrt{\pi}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\nu}{2}$}}\Big)^{\frac{\nu}{2}}\;,

which implies the desired bound that, for some absolute constant
C>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|Z|^{\nu}\;\leq\;C\nu^{\nu/2}\;.

For the second bound, we note that if
m_{1}
and
m_{2}
have different parities,

\displaystyle\text{\rm Cov}[Z^{m_{1}},Z^{m_{2}}]\;=\;\mathbb{E}[Z^{m_{1}+m_{2}}]-\mathbb{E}[Z^{m_{1}}]\mathbb{E}[Z^{m_{2}}]\;=\;0

since odd moments of
Z
vanish. Now focus on the case when
m_{1}
and
m_{2}
have the same parity, and recall that
m_{1}\geq m_{2}
by assumption. For
x\geq 0
and
\alpha\in[0,1]
, we define the function

R(x;\alpha)\coloneqq 2^{x+\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{x}{e}$}}\Big)^{x}(8x^{3}+4x^{2}+x+\alpha)^{1/6}
, which implies that

\displaystyle R\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\nu-1}{2}$}};\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{100}$}}\Big)\;<\;\mathbb{E}|Z|^{\nu}\;<\;R\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\nu-1}{2}$}};\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{30}$}}\Big)\;.

Then we can bound

\displaystyle\text{\rm Cov}[

\displaystyle Z^{m_{1}},Z^{m_{2}}]\;\geq\;\mathbb{E}[Z^{m_{1}+m_{2}}]-\mathbb{E}[|Z|^{m_{1}}]\mathbb{E}[|Z|^{m_{2}}]

\displaystyle\;\geq

\displaystyle\;R\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{m_1+m_2-1}{2}$}};\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{100}$}}\Big)-R\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{m_1-1}{2}$}};\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{30}$}}\Big)R\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{m_2-1}{2}$}};\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{30}$}}\Big)\;.

First suppose
m_{2}\geq 2
, and denote
\alpha=1/100
and
\beta=1/30
. Note that for
x\geq y\geq 3/4
,

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R(x+y; \alpha)}{R(x - 1/4;\beta)R(y - 1/4;\beta)}$}}

\displaystyle\;=\;e^{-1/2}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ (x+y)^{x+y} }{
(x-1/4)^{x-1/4}
(y-1/4)^{y-1/4}
}$}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
\big(
8(x+y)^3
+
4(x+y)^2
+
(x+y)
+
\alpha\big)^{1/6}
}{
\big(
8(x-1/4)^3
+
4(x-1/4)^2
+
(x-1/4)
+
\beta\big)^{1/6}
\big(
8(y-1/4)^3
+
4(y-1/4)^2
+
(y-1/4)
+
\beta\big)^{\frac{1}{6}}
}$}}

\displaystyle\;\geq\;e^{-1/2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{x+y}{y-1/4}$}}\Big)^{y-1/4}

\displaystyle\quad\times\bigg(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
8(x+y)^6
+
4(x+y)^5
+
(x+y)^4
+
\alpha(x+y)^3
}{
\big(
8(x-1/4)^3
+
4(x-1/4)^2
+
(x-1/4)
+
\beta\big)
\big(
8(y-1/4)^3
+
4(y-1/4)^2
+
(y-1/4)
+
\beta\big)
}$}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{6}}

\displaystyle\;\eqqcolon\;a(x,y)\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{b(x,y)}{c(x,y)}$}}\Big)^{1/6}\;.

Note that since
x\geq y\geq 3/4
, we have

\displaystyle a(x,y)\;=\;e^{-1/2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{x+y}{y-1/4}$}}\Big)^{y-1/4}\;\geq\;e^{-1/2}2^{y-1/4}\;\geq\;e^{-1/2}2^{2}\;>\;2\;.

On the other hand, a lengthy computation gives

\displaystyle c(x,y)\;<

\displaystyle\;64x^{3}y^{3}+32x^{3}y^{2}+8x^{3}y+8\beta x^{3}+32x^{2}y^{3}+16x^{2}y^{2}+4x^{2}y+4\beta x^{2}+8xy^{3}

\displaystyle\;+4xy^{2}+xy+\beta x+8\beta y^{3}+4\beta y^{2}+\beta y+\beta^{2}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;b(x,y)-\big(96x^{3}y^{3}-4x^{3}y-(8\beta-\alpha)x^{3}-10x^{2}y^{2}-x^{2}y-4\beta x^{2}-4xy^{3}-4xy^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad-xy-\beta x-8\beta y^{3}-4\beta y^{2}-\beta y-\beta^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;b(x,y)-46x^{3}y^{3}\;<\;b(x,y)\;,

where we have used that
x,y\geq 3/4
,
\alpha=1/100
and
\beta=1/30
in the last line. Therefore
b(x,y)/c(x,y)>1
and, for
x\geq y\geq 3/4
,

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{R(x+y; \alpha)}{R(x - 1/4;\beta)R(y - 1/4;\beta)}$}}\;>\;2\;.

By identifying
x=\frac{m_{1}}{2}-\frac{1}{4}
and
y=\frac{m_{2}}{2}-\frac{1}{4}
, we get that for
m_{1}\geq m_{2}\geq 2
such that
m_{1}
and
m_{2}
have the same parity,

\displaystyle\text{\rm Cov}[Z^{m_{1}},Z^{m_{2}}]\;\geq

\displaystyle\;R\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{m_1+m_2-1}{2}$}};\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{100}$}}\Big)-R\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{m_1-1}{2}$}};\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{30}$}}\Big)R\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{m_2-1}{2}$}};\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{30}$}}\Big)

\displaystyle\;>

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}R\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{m_1+m_2-1}{2}$}};\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{100}$}}\Big)\;.

Meanwhile for
m_{2}=1
and
m_{1}
odd, since
\mathbb{E}[Z]=0
, we obtain directly that

\displaystyle\text{\rm Cov}[Z^{m_{1}},Z^{m_{2}}]\;=\;\mathbb{E}[Z^{m_{1}+m_{2}-1}]\;\geq\;R\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{m_1+m_2-1}{2}$}};\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{100}$}}\Big)\;>\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}R\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{m_1+m_2-1}{2}$}};\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{100}$}}\Big)\;.

Therefore for any
m_{1}
and
m_{2}
with the same parity, we get the desired bound that

\displaystyle\text{\rm Cov}[Z^{m_{1}},

\displaystyle Z^{m_{2}}]\;>\;2^{\frac{m_{1}+m_{2}}{2}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{m_1+m_2-1}{2e}$}}\Big)^{\frac{m_{1}+m_{2}-1}{2}}\;=\;2^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{m_1+m_2-1}{e}$}}\Big)^{\frac{m_{1}+m_{2}-1}{2}}

\displaystyle\;\geq

\displaystyle\;c_{1}^{m_{1}+m_{2}}(m_{1}+m_{2}-1)^{\frac{m_{1}+m_{2}}{2}}\;\geq\;c_{1}^{m_{1}+m_{2}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{m_1 + m_2 -1}{m_1+m_2}$}}\Big)^{\frac{m_{1}+m_{2}}{2}}(m_{1}+m_{2})^{\frac{m_{1}+m_{2}}{2}}

\displaystyle\;\geq

\displaystyle\;c^{m_{1}+m_{2}}(m_{1}+m_{2})^{\frac{m_{1}+m_{2}}{2}}

for some sufficiently small absolute constants
c_{1},c>0
. ∎

Proofs for properties of the heavy-tailed distribution in Section
5

The first two moments of
V_{1}
can be obtained directly from construction. To control the
\omega
-th absolute moment for
\omega\geq 1
, first note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|U_{1}|^{\omega}\;=

\displaystyle\;2p|x_{0}|^{\omega}+p|2x_{0}|^{\omega}\;=\;(2+2^{\omega})px_{0}^{\omega}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(2 + 2^\omega) \sigma^\omega}{6^{\omega/2} p^{\omega/2-1}}$}}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(2 + 2^\omega)}{6^{\omega/2} \sigma^{2(\omega-\nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}\;.

(41)

Moreover, by Jensen’s inequality, we have
|a+b|^{\omega}\leq 2^{\omega-1}(|a|^{\omega}+|b|^{\omega})
for
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
. Combining this with the upper bound on
\mathbb{E}|\sigma^{-1}Z_{1}|^{\omega}
from
Lemma

23
, we get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|V_{1}|^{\omega}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2^{-\omega/2} \, 2^{\omega-1}(2+2^\omega)}{6^{\omega/2} \sigma^{2(\omega-\nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}+2^{-\omega/2}2^{\omega-1}\sigma^{\omega}\mathbb{E}|\sigma^{-1}Z|^{\omega}\;\leq\;c_{1}^{\omega}\sigma^{-\frac{2(\omega-\nu)}{\nu-2}}+c_{2}^{\omega}\sigma^{\omega}\omega^{\omega/2}

for some absolute constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
as desired.
∎

Lemma

20
approximates an empirical average of
V_{i}
’s by a Gaussian
Z^{\prime}_{1}
, and gives a finer control by considering an additional remainder term. The key idea is to perform a fourth-order Taylor expansion in the characteristic functions of both
n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{i}
and
Z^{\prime}_{1}
, before turning back to the distribution functions. Note that the smoothing by
Z_{1}
in the construction of
V_{1}
makes the distribution of
V_{1}
continuous, which enables this approach.

Write
S_{n}=n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{i}
,
F_{n}(x)=\mathbb{P}(S_{n}<x)
and
F_{Z}(x)=\mathbb{P}(Z^{\prime}_{1}<x)
. By the inversion formula for continuous random variables (Theorem 2.3.1.,
[
19
]
),

\displaystyle F_{n}(x)\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2\pi}$}}\mint^{\infty}_{-\infty}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ - e^{-i tx}}{it}$}}\chi_{n}(t)\,dt

and

\displaystyle F_{Z}(x)\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2\pi}$}}\mint^{\infty}_{-\infty}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ - e^{-i tx}}{it}$}}\chi_{Z}(t)\,dt\;

(42)

where
\chi_{n}
and
\chi_{Z}
are the characteristic functions of
S_{n}
and
Z
. We first compare the characteristic functions. Denoting
\theta\coloneqq 2^{-1/2}
for simplicity, the characteristic function of
V
satisfies

\displaystyle\chi_{V}(t)\;=\;\mathbb{E}[e^{it(\theta U+\theta\sigma Z)}]\;=\;e^{-\theta^{2}\sigma^{2}t^{2}/2}\big(2pe^{-i\theta x_{0}t}+(1-3p)+pe^{2i\theta x_{0}t}\big)

\displaystyle=\;e^{-\theta^{2}\sigma^{2}t^{2}/2}\big(1-3p+2p\cos(\theta x_{0}t)+p\cos(2\theta x_{0}t)+i(-2p\sin(\theta x_{0}t)+p\sin(2\theta x_{0}t))\big)

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}\;e^{-\frac{\theta^{2}\sigma^{2}t^{2}}{2}}\Big(1-3p+2p\big(1-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta^2 x_0^2 t^2 }{2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \theta^4 x_0^4 t^4 \cos(t \theta x'_1)}{24}$}}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 60.00009pt+p\big(1-2t^{2}\theta^{2}x_{0}^{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 \theta^4 x_0^4 t^4 \cos(2 t \theta x'_1) }{3}$}}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 60.00009pt-i(2p)\big(\theta x_{0}t-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \theta^3 x_0^3 t^3}{6}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \theta^4 x_0^4 t^4\sin( t \theta x'_2 ) }{24}$}}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 60.00009pt+ip\big(2\theta x_{0}t-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4 \theta^3 x_0^3 t^3 }{3}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 \theta^4 x_0^4 t^4 \sin( 2 t \theta x'_2 ) }{3}$}}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=\;e^{-\theta^{2}\sigma^{2}t^{2}/2}\Big(1-3p\theta^{2}x_{0}^{2}t^{2}-ip\theta^{3}x_{0}^{3}t^{3}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{p \theta^4 x_0^4 t^4}{12}$}}\cos(t\theta x^{\prime}_{1})+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2p \theta^4 x_0^4 t^4}{3}$}}\cos(2t\theta x^{\prime}_{1})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\;\;-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ip \theta^4 x_0^4 t^4 }{12}$}}\sin(t\theta x^{\prime}_{2})+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 i p \theta^4 x_0^4 t^4 }{3}$}}\sin(2t\theta x^{\prime}_{2})\Big)\;.

In
(a)
, we have performed Taylor expansions on the real and imaginary parts with some
x^{\prime}_{1},x^{\prime}_{2}\in[0,x_{0}]
. Since
x_{0}=\sigma/\sqrt{6p}
and
p=\sigma^{2\nu/(\nu-2)}
, we have that

\displaystyle px_{0}^{2}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma^2}{6}$}}\;,\qquad px_{0}^{3}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{6^{3/2}\sigma^{(6 - 2 \nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}\;,\qquad px_{0}^{4}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{6^2 \sigma^{(8- 2\nu)/(\nu-2)} }$}}\;.

Then the characteristic function of
S_{n}
can be expressed as

\displaystyle\chi_{n}

\displaystyle(t)\;=\;(\chi_{V}(n^{-1/2}\,t))^{n}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;e^{-\theta^{2}\sigma^{2}t^{2}/2}\Big(1-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3p \theta^2 x_0^2 t^2}{n}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ip\theta^3 x_0^3 t^3 }{n^{3/2}}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{p \theta^4 x_0^4 t^4}{12 n^2}$}}\cos(t\theta x^{\prime}_{1})+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2p \theta^4 x_0^4 t^4}{3 n^2 }$}}\cos(2t\theta x^{\prime}_{1})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\;\;-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ip \theta^4 x_0^4 t^4 }{12 n^2 }$}}\sin(t\theta x^{\prime}_{2})+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 i p \theta^4 x_0^4 t^4}{3 n^2}$}}\sin(2t\theta x^{\prime}_{2})\Big)^{n}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;e^{-\theta^{2}\sigma^{2}t^{2}/2}\Big(1-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \theta^2 \sigma^2 t^2 }{2 n}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{i \theta^3 t^3 }{6^{3/2} n^{3/2} \sigma^{(6 - 2 \nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta^4 t^4 \cos(t \theta x'_1) }{432 n^2 \sigma^{(8-2\nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\;\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta^4 t^4 \cos(2 t \theta x'_1) }{54 n^2 \sigma^{(8-2\nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{i \theta^4 t^4 \sin( t \theta x'_2 ) }{432 n^2 \sigma^{(8-2\nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ i \theta^4 t^4 \sin( 2 t \theta x'_2 ) }{54 n^2 \sigma^{(8-2\nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}\Big)^{n}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \theta^2 \sigma^2 t^2}{2}$}}+n\log\Big(1-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \theta^2 \sigma^2 t^2 }{2n}$}}-i\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{Q_n(t)}{n}$}}+R_{1}(t)+iR_{2}(t)\Big)\,\Big)\;,

where we have defined

\displaystyle Q_{n}(t)\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta^3 t^3}{6^{3/2} n^{1/2} \sigma^{(6 - 2 \nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}\;,

\displaystyle R_{1}(t)\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \theta^4 t^4\cos(t \theta x'_1) }{432 n^2 \sigma^{(8-2\nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \theta^4 t^4 \cos(2 t \theta x'_1) }{54 n^2 \sigma^{(8-2\nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}\;,

\displaystyle R_{2}(t)\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta^4 t^4 \sin( t \theta x'_2 ) }{432 n^2 \sigma^{(8-2\nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \theta^4 t^4 \sin( 2 t \theta x'_2 ) }{54 n^2 \sigma^{(8-2\nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}\;.

Now define

\displaystyle R_{n}(t)\;\coloneqq\;n\log\Big(1-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \theta^2 \sigma^2 t^2 }{2n}$}}-i\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{Q_n(t)}{n}$}}+R_{1}(t)+iR_{2}(t)\Big)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta^2 \sigma^2t^2}{2}$}}+iQ_{n}(t)\;.

By multiplying and dividing
e^{-\sigma^{2}t^{2}/4}
and recalling that
\theta=2^{-1/2}
, we get that

\displaystyle\chi_{n}(t)\;=

\displaystyle\;e^{-\frac{\sigma^{2}t^{2}}{2}-iQ_{n}(t)}e^{R_{n}(t)}\;=\;\chi_{Z}(t)\,e^{-iQ_{n}(t)}e^{R_{n}(t)}\;.

Now define
q(t)\coloneqq-iQ_{n}(t)\chi_{Z}(t)
. Then

\displaystyle|\chi_{n}(t)-\chi_{Z}(t)-q(t)|\;=

\displaystyle\;\Big|\chi_{Z}(t)\Big(e^{-iQ_{n}(t)}e^{R_{n}(t)}-1+iQ_{n}(t)\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;|\chi_{Z}(t)e^{-iQ_{n}(t)}(e^{R_{n}(t)}-1)|+|\chi_{Z}(t)(e^{-iQ_{n}(t)}-1+iQ_{n}(t))|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;e^{-\sigma^{2}t^{2}/2}\big(|e^{R_{n}(t)}-1|+|e^{-iQ_{n}(t)}-1+iQ_{n}(t)|\big)

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;e^{-\sigma^{2}t^{2}/2}\big(|e^{R_{n}(t)}-1|+|Q_{n}(t)|^{2}\big)\;.

(43)

We seek to control
R_{n}(t)
by a Taylor expansion of the complex logarithm around
1
, which is only permitted outside the branch cut
\mathbb{R}^{-}\cup\{0\}
. Set
T_{n}\coloneqq n^{1/2}\sigma^{(4-\nu)/(2\nu-4)}
. For
|t|\leq T_{n}
,

\displaystyle\Big|-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \theta^2 \sigma^2 t^2 }{2n}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{i Q_n(t)}{n}$}}+

\displaystyle R_{1}(t)+iR_{2}(t)\Big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta^2 \sigma^2 t^2 }{2n}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta^3 t^3 }{6^{3/2} n^{3/2} \sigma^{(6 - 2 \nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta^4 t^4 }{24 n^2 \sigma^{(8-2\nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1 }{6^{3/2}}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1 }{24}$}}\;\leq\;1\;.

In this case, the quantity in the complex logarithm in
R_{n}(t)
is outside the branch cut, so by a Taylor expansion,

\displaystyle R_{n}(t)\;=

\displaystyle\;nR_{1}(t)+inR_{2}(t)+nR_{3}(t)

where
R_{3}(t)
is a remainder term that satisfies, for
|t|\leq T_{n}
,

\displaystyle|R_{3}(t)|\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\;\Big|-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \theta^2 \sigma^2 t^2 }{2n}$}}-i\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{Q_n(t)}{n}$}}+R_{1}(t)+iR_{2}(t)\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\;\overset{(a)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\;\;3\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{t^4 \theta^4 \sigma^4}{4n^2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{t^6 \theta^6 }{6^3 n^3 \sigma^{(12 - 4 \nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{t^8 \theta^8 }{24^2 n^4 \sigma^{(16-4\nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}\Big)

\displaystyle\;\overset{(b)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\;\;3\theta^{4}t^{4}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \sigma^4 }{4n^2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1 }{6^3 n^2 \sigma^{(8-3\nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1 }{24^2 n^2 \sigma^{(8-2\nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}\Big)

\displaystyle\;\overset{(c)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\;\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{t^4}{5 n^2 \sigma^{(8-2\nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}\;,

In
(a)
, we have noted that
(A+B+C)^{2}\leq 3(A^{2}+B^{2}+C^{2})
; in
(b)
, we have used
|t|\leq T_{n}
and
\theta\leq 1
; in
(c)
, we have compared the powers of
\sigma\in(0,1]
by noting that
\frac{2\nu-8}{\nu-2}\leq\frac{3\nu-8}{\nu-2}
and
\frac{2\nu-8}{\nu-2}\leq 4
, and combined the constants while noting that
\theta^{4}=1/4
. By a further Taylor expansion of the complex exponential, we obtain that for
|t|\leq T_{n}
,

\displaystyle\big|e^{R_{n}(t)}-1\big|\;\leq\;|R_{n}(t)|\;\leq

\displaystyle\;n|R_{1}(t)|+n|R_{2}(t)|+n|R_{3}(t)|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta^4 t^4}{24 n \sigma^{(8-2\nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ t^4}{5 n \sigma^{(8-2\nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{0.22 t^4}{n \sigma^{(8-2\nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}\;.

By plugging this and
Q_{n}(t)=\frac{\theta^{3}t^{3}}{6^{3/2}n^{1/2}\sigma^{(6-2\nu)/(\nu-2)}}
into (
43
), we get that for
|t|\leq T_{n}
,

\displaystyle|\chi_{n}(t)-\chi_{Z}(t)-q(t)|\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}e^{-\sigma^{2}t^{2}/2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{0.22 t^4}{ \sigma^{(8-2\nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{0.03 t^6 }{\sigma^{(12 - 4 \nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}\Big)\;.

(44)

Now note that
F_{q}
is a function analogous to (
42
) for
q
:

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2\pi}$}}\mint^{\infty}_{-\infty}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ - e^{-i tx}}{it }$}}q(t)\,dt\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2\pi}$}}\mint^{\infty}_{-\infty}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ e^{-i tx}}{t}$}}Q_{n}(t)\chi_{Z}(t)\,dt

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2\pi}$}}\mint^{\infty}_{-\infty}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ e^{-i tx}}{t}$}}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta^3 t^3}{6^{3/2} n^{1/2} \sigma^{(6 - 2 \nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}e^{-\sigma^{2}t^{2}/2}\,dt

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta^3}{(2^{5/2}3^{3/2}\pi) \, n^{1/2} \sigma^{(6-2\nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}\mint^{\infty}_{-\infty}t^{2}e^{-\sigma^{2}t^{2}/2-itx}dt

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta^3}{(2^2 3^{3/2}\pi^{1/2}) \, n^{1/2} \sigma^{\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}\Big(1-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{x^2}{\sigma^2}$}}\Big)e^{-x^{2}/(2\sigma^{2})}\;=\;F_{q}(x)\;,

where we have set the constant
A
in the definition of
F_{q}
as

A\coloneqq\frac{\theta^{3}}{2^{2}3^{3/2}\pi^{1/2}}\in[0.07,0.08]\;.
since
\theta=2^{-1/2}
. Therefore by (
42
), a bound on the distribution functions can be given as

\displaystyle|F_{n}(x

\displaystyle)-F_{Z}(x)-F_{q}(x)|\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2\pi}$}}\mint^{\infty}_{-\infty}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{| \chi_n(t) - \chi_Z(t) - q(t) |}{|t|}$}}\,dt

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2\pi}$}}\mint_{|t|\leq T_{n}}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{| \chi_n(t) - \chi_Z(t) - q(t) |}{|t|}$}}\,dt+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2\pi}$}}\mint_{|t|>T_{n}}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{| \chi_n(t) - \chi_Z(t) - q(t) |}{|t|}$}}\,dt\;\eqqcolon\;I_{1}+I_{2}\;.

(
44
) allows the first integral to be controlled as

\displaystyle I_{1}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2 \pi n}$}}\mint_{|t|\leq T_{n}}e^{-\sigma^{2}t^{2}/2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{0.22 |t|^3}{ \sigma^{(8-2\nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{0.03 |t|^5 }{\sigma^{(12 - 4 \nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}\Big)\,dt

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2\pi n}$}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{0.44\big( 2 - (\sigma^2 T_n^2 + 2) e^{-\sigma^2 T_n^2/2}\big)}{\sigma^{2\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{0.06\big(8 - (\sigma^4 T_n^4 + 4 \sigma^2 T_n^2 + 8) e^{-\sigma^2 T_n^2/2} \big)}{\sigma^{2\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}\Big)

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{0.22 }{n \sigma^{2\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}\;.

To deal with the case
|t|>T_{n}
, we first let
\{(U_{i},V_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n}
be i.i.d. copies of
(U,V)
while applying independence and Jensen’s inequality to obtain

\displaystyle|\chi_{n}(t)|\;=\;\big|\mathbb{E}\big[e^{i\theta^{2}tn^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[e^{i\theta^{2}tn^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{i}}\big]\big|\;\leq\;\big|\mathbb{E}\big[e^{i\theta^{2}tn^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{i}}\big]\big|\;=\;e^{-\frac{\theta^{2}\sigma^{2}t^{2}}{2}}\;.

By noting that
\chi_{Z}(t)=e^{-\sigma^{2}t^{2}/2}
and
|q(t)|=|Q_{n}(t)|e^{-\sigma^{2}t^{2}/2}\leq\frac{\theta^{3}t^{3}}{6^{3/2}n^{1/2}\sigma^{(6-2\nu)/(\nu-2)}}e^{-\sigma^{2}t^{2}/2}
, we can bound
I_{2}
via a triangle inequality:

\displaystyle I_{2}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2\pi}$}}\mint_{|t|>T_{n}}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{|\chi_n(t)|}{|t|}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{|\chi_Z(t)|}{|t|}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{|q(t)|}{|t|}$}}dt

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2\pi}$}}\mint_{|t|>T_{n}}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{e^{- \theta^2 \sigma^ 2 t^2/2}}{|t|}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{e^{- \sigma^ 2 t^2/2}}{|t|}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \theta^3 t^2 e^{- \sigma^ 2 t^2/2}}{6^{3/2} n^{1/2} \sigma^{(6-2\nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}dt

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\pi\, T_n^2}$}}\Big(\mint_{t>T_{n}}te^{-\theta^{2}\sigma^{2}t^{2}/2}dt\Big)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\pi\, T_n^2}$}}\Big(\mint_{t>T_{n}}te^{-\sigma^{2}t^{2}/2}dt\Big)

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 }{ \pi(12)^{3/2} n^{1/2} \sigma^{(6-2\nu)/(\nu-2)} T_n}$}}\Big(\mint_{0}^{\infty}t^{3}e^{-\sigma^{2}t^{2}/2}dt\Big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 e^{- \sigma^2 T_n^2/4}}{\pi\,T_n^2 \sigma^2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{e^{- \sigma^2 T_n^2/2}}{\pi\,T_n^2 \sigma^2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4 }{\pi(12)^{3/2} n^{1/2} \sigma^{(6-2\nu)/(\nu-2) + 4} \, T_n}$}}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3 }{\pi}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4}{\pi\, (12)^{3/2}}$}}\Big)\max\Big\{\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1 }{n \sigma^{\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}\,,\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n \sigma^{ 3\nu/(2\nu-4) }}$}}\Big\}\,\;\leq\,\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n \sigma^{2\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}\;.

In the last line, we have recalled that
T_{n}=n^{1/2}\sigma^{(4-\nu)/(2\nu-4)}
and noted that
\sigma\in(0,1]
. Combining the bounds for
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
, we get that

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{n}}$}}\msum_{i=1}^{n}V_{i}<x\Big)-\mathbb{P}(Z<x)-F_{q}(x)\Big|\;=\;|F_{n}(x)-F_{Z}(x)-F_{q}(x)|\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2}{n \sigma^{2\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}

as desired.
∎

Lemma

21
provides a normal approximation error with tighter
x
-dependence than a typical non-uniform Berry-Esséen bound. The proof is tedious, but the key ideas are the following:

(i)

In the interval
|x|\leq\frac{2\sigma}{\sqrt{3}\,M}
, i.e.
x
is within some multiples of the standard deviation of the Gaussian from the mean, Berry-Esséen bound is sufficiently tight so we can apply it directly. The interesting case is when
x>\frac{2\sigma}{\sqrt{3}\,M}
(
x<\frac{2\sigma}{\sqrt{3}\,M}
can be handled by symmetry).

(ii)

To give a tighter control in the region
x>\frac{2\sigma}{\sqrt{3}\,M}
, the first part of the proof expands a telescoping sum of
n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{i}
and successively replacing the non-Gaussian part of each data point, i.e.
U_{i}
in
V_{i}=2^{-1/2}U_{i}+2^{-1/2}Z_{i}
, by a Gaussian.

(iii)

Since the support of
U_{i}
has size
\Theta(\sigma^{-\frac{2}{\nu-2}})
, which is made to grow slower than
n^{1/2}
by assumption of the lemma, the support of
n^{-1/2}U_{i}
shrinks. Therefore on most part of the real line, the Gaussian approximation error is determined solely by the mass of the Gaussian counterpart of
n^{-1/2}U_{i}
. By carefully choosing a region moderately far from the origin, we can make use of the exponential tail of
n^{-1/2}U_{i}
. This is the argument leading up to (
45
), and gives rise to the first exponential term in
Lemma

21
.

(iv)

At places where we cannot exploit the tail of
n^{-1/2}U_{i}
, we make use of the approximate Gaussianity of the remaining sum
n^{-1/2}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}V_{i^{\prime}}-n^{-1/2}U_{i}
, which is the proof starting from (
46
). The idea is that when
n^{-1/2}U_{i}
is close to the origin, the remaining sum
n^{-1/2}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}V_{i^{\prime}}-n^{-1/2}U_{i}
is allowed to be far from the origin, and we can obtain a sharper tail. This is achieved by symmetrising the sum
n^{-1/2}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}V_{i^{\prime}}-n^{-1/2}U_{i}
in (
47
) and then exploiting the improved
n^{-3/2}
rate of normal approximation of a symmetric sum via Lindeberg’s technique in (
48
). This yields the second
n^{-3/2}x^{-4}
term in
Lemma

21
; as suggested by the
x^{-4}
term, this error is only well-controlled at regions far from the origin.

The proof below is an elaborate version of the arguments in Example 9.1.3 of Senatov
[
54
]
and with their final step replaced by Lindeberg’s technique.

Recall that
V\coloneqq 2^{-1/2}U+2^{-1/2}Z
. Let
\{U_{i},Z_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be i.i.d. copies of
(U,Z)
and let
\{Z_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be an independent copy of
\{Z_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
. Write

\displaystyle W_{i}\;\coloneqq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{2 n}}$}}\big(\msum_{j=1}^{n}Z_{j}+\msum_{j=1}^{i-1}U_{i}+\msum_{j=i+1}^{n}Z^{\prime}_{i}\big)

and denote its probability measure by
\mu_{W_{i}}
. By expressing the quantity to be bounded as a telescoping sum and noting the independence of
(W_{i},U_{i},V_{i})
across different
i
’s, we have

\displaystyle|F_{n}

\displaystyle(x)-F_{Z}(x)|\;=\;\big|\mathbb{P}\big(W_{n}+(2n)^{-1/2}U_{n}<x\big)-\mathbb{P}\big(W_{1}+(2n)^{-1/2}Z_{1}<x\big)\big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\msum_{i=1}^{n}\big|\mathbb{P}\big(W_{i}+(2n)^{-1/2}U_{i}<x\big)-\mathbb{P}\big(W_{i}+(2n)^{-1/2}Z_{i}<x\big)\big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\msum_{i=1}^{n}\mint_{-\infty}^{\infty}\,\big|\mathbb{P}\big((2n)^{-1/2}U_{i}<x-w\big)-\mathbb{P}\big((2n)^{-1/2}Z_{i}<x-w\big)\big|d\mu_{W_{i}}(w)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\msum_{i=1}^{n}\mint_{-\infty}^{\infty}\,\big|\mathbb{P}\big((2n)^{-1/2}U_{i}<x-w\big)-\mathbb{P}\big((2n)^{-1/2}Z_{i}<x-w\big)\big|\,d\mu_{W_{i}}(w)

\displaystyle\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\msum_{i=1}^{n}\mint_{-\infty}^{\infty}J_{i}(w)\,d\mu_{W_{i}}(w)\;=\;\msum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\mint_{-\infty}^{x/2}J_{i}(w)\,d\mu_{W_{i}}(w)+\mint_{x/2}^{\infty}J_{i}(w)\,d\mu_{W_{i}}(w)\Big)\;.

We first focus on the case
x>\frac{2\sigma}{\sqrt{3}M}
, where
M
is the constant in the assumption
\sigma^{\nu/(\nu-2)}\geq Mn^{-1/2}
. Since
(2n)^{-1/2}U_{i}
is bounded in norm by
(2n)^{-1/2}(2x_{0})=\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{3n}\,\sigma^{\nu/(\nu-2)}}\leq\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{3}M}<\frac{x}{2}
almost surely, the probability
\mathbb{P}\big(n^{-1/2}U_{i}\geq x-w\big)
for
w<x/2
is zero. Therefore

\displaystyle\mint_{-\infty}^{x/2}J_{i}(w)

\displaystyle d\mu_{W_{i}}(w)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mint_{-\infty}^{x/2}\big|\mathbb{P}\big((2n)^{-1/2}U_{i}\geq x-w\big)-\mathbb{P}\big((2n)^{-1/2}Z_{i}\geq x-w\big)\big|d\mu_{W_{i}}(w)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mint_{-\infty}^{x/2}\mathbb{P}\big((2n)^{-1/2}Z_{i}\geq x-w\big)d\mu_{W_{i}}(w)\;\;\;\leq\;\;\;\mathbb{P}\Big((2n)^{-1/2}Z_{i}\geq\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{x}{2}$}}\Big)

\displaystyle\;\overset{(a)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \sigma}{\sqrt{\pi n} \, x}$}}e^{-\frac{nx^{2}}{4\sigma^{2}}}\;\overset{(b)}{\leq}\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \sigma}{\sqrt{\pi n} \, x}$}}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{8 \sigma^2}{nx^2}$}}e^{-\frac{nx^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}}}\;\overset{(c)}{\leq}\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3^{3/2} M^3 }{ \sqrt{\pi} \, n^{3/2}}$}}e^{-\frac{nx^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}}}\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3M^3 }{n^{3/2}}$}}e^{-\frac{nx^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}}}\;.

(45)

In
(a)
, we used a standard bound for the complementary error function. In
(b)
, we have noted that
y\coloneqq\frac{nx^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}}>\frac{n}{6M^{2}}\geq 1
since
n\geq 6M^{2}
and applied the bound
e^{-y}\leq\frac{1}{y}
for
y\geq 1
. In
(c)
, we have used
x>\frac{2\sigma}{\sqrt{3}M}
again. Now for the other integral, by the standard Berry-Esséen bound,

\displaystyle\mint_{x/2}^{\infty}J_{i}(w)d\mu_{W_{i}}(w)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\mean|U_i|^3}{n^{3/2} \sigma^3}$}}\mint_{x/2}^{\infty}d\mu_{W_{i}}(w)\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{10}{6^{3/2} n^{3/2} \sigma^{\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}\mathbb{P}(W_{i}\geq x/2)\;,

(46)

where we have used the moment formula from (
41
) to get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|U_{i}|^{3}\;=\;\frac{10}{6^{3/2}\sigma^{(6-2\nu)/(\nu-2)}}\;.

To handle the probability term, we first consider rewriting the sum
W_{i}=\xi_{i}+S_{i}
with a Gaussian component
\xi_{i}
and an independent non-Gaussian component
S_{i}
given by

\displaystyle\xi_{i}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{2n}}$}}\big(\msum_{j=1}^{n}Z_{i}+\msum_{j=i+1}^{n}Z^{\prime}_{i}\big)\sim\mathcal{N}\Big(0,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(2n-i)}{2n}$}}\sigma^{2}\Big)

and

\displaystyle S_{i}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{2n}}$}}\msum_{j=1}^{i-1}U_{i}\;.

We also write
W_{i}^{\prime}=\xi_{i}^{\prime}+S_{i}^{\prime}
, where
\xi^{\prime}_{i}
and
S^{\prime}_{i}
are i.i.d. copies of
\xi_{i}
and
S_{i}
, and denote the symmetrisation
\bar{W}_{i}=W_{i}-W^{\prime}_{i}
. Let
\mu_{W_{i}}
be the measure associated with
W_{i}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\bar{W}_{i}\geq

\displaystyle\,x/2)\;=\;\mint_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}(-W^{\prime}_{i}\geq x/2-t)\,d\mu_{W_{i}}(t)\;\geq\;\mint_{x/2}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}(-W^{\prime}_{i}\geq x/2-t)\,d\mu_{W_{i}}(t)

\displaystyle\;\geq

\displaystyle\;\big(\inf\nolimits_{t\in[\frac{x}{2},\infty)}\,\mathbb{P}(-W^{\prime}_{i}\geq x/2-t)\big)\mathbb{P}(W_{i}\geq x/2)\;=\;\mathbb{P}(-W^{\prime}_{i}\geq 0)\,\mathbb{P}(W_{i}\geq x/2)\;.

This rearranges to give

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(W_{i}\geq x/2)\;\leq\;\mathbb{P}(-W^{\prime}_{i}\geq 0)^{-1}\,\mathbb{P}(\bar{W}_{i}\geq x/2)\;,

(47)

The first probability can be lower bounded by

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(-W^{\prime}_{i}\geq 0)\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}(\xi_{i}+S_{i}\leq 0)\;\geq\;\mathbb{P}(\xi_{i}\leq 0)\,\mathbb{P}(S_{i}\leq 0)\;.

Note that
\mathbb{P}(\xi_{i}\leq 0)=1/2
since
\xi_{i}
is symmetric and
\mathbb{P}(S_{1}\leq 0)=1
. For
i>1
, by a Berry-Esséen bound and the assumption that
\sigma^{\nu/(\nu-2)}\geq Mn^{-1/2}
and
M\geq 10
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(S_{i}\leq 0)\;\geq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2^{3/2} \mean|U_i|^3}{n^{1/2} \sigma^3}$}}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{10}{3^{3/2} n^{1/2} \sigma^{\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}\;\geq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{10}{3^{3/2} M}$}}\;\geq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{4}$}}\;.

This implies that
\mathbb{P}(-W^{\prime}_{i}\geq 0)\geq\frac{1}{8}
and therefore

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(W_{i}\geq x/2)\;\leq\;8\,\mathbb{P}(\bar{W}_{i}\geq x/2)\;.

In other words, we have shown that
\mathbb{P}(W_{i}\geq x/2)
in the bound (
46
) can now be controlled in terms of the symmetric variable
\bar{W}_{i}
.

The final step is to compare
\bar{W}_{i}
to
\bar{Z}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{\theta^{2}(2n-1)}{n}\sigma^{2})
. We apply the standard Lindeberg’s argument with a smooth test function
h
. By
Lemma

15
with
\delta=x/4
and
m=3
, there is a three-times differentiable function
h
such that for some absolute constant
C^{\prime\prime}>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}_{\{t\geq x/2\}}\;

\displaystyle\leq\;h(t)\;\leq\;\mathbb{I}_{\{t\geq x/4\}}\;

and

\displaystyle\;|h^{\prime\prime\prime}(t)-h^{\prime\prime\prime}(s)|\;

\displaystyle\leq\;C^{\prime\prime}x^{-4}|t-s|\;.

Consider the symmetric variable
\bar{U}_{i}=\frac{\theta}{\sqrt{n}}(U_{i}-U^{\prime}_{i})
, where
\{U^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
is an independent copy of
\{U_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
. Write
Y_{k}=\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\bar{U}_{i}+\bar{\xi}_{i}
, where
\bar{\xi}_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}\big(0,\frac{\theta^{2}(4n-2k)}{n}\sigma^{2}\big)
is independent of
\bar{U}_{i}
. Also denote
\zeta_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}\big(0,\frac{2\theta^{2}}{n}\sigma^{2}\big)
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\bar{W}_{i}\geq x/2\big)\;\leq\;\mathbb{P}(\bar{Z}\geq x/4)+|\mathbb{E}[h(\bar{W}_{i})-h(\bar{Z})]|

(48)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\bar{Z}\geq x/4)+\msum_{k=1}^{i}|\mathbb{E}[h(Y_{k}+\bar{U}_{k})-h(Y_{k}+\zeta_{k})]|\eqqcolon\mathbb{P}(\bar{Z}\geq x/4)+\msum_{k=1}^{i}A_{i}.

Since
\bar{Z}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{\theta^{2}(2n-1)}{n}\sigma^{2})
, by noting
\frac{\theta^{2}(2n-1)}{n}\sigma^{2}\leq\sigma^{2}
and
x>\frac{2\sigma}{\sqrt{3}\,M}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\bar{Z}\geq x/4)\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma}{\sqrt{2\pi} \, (x/4) }$}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}}\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{6} \, M}{\sqrt{\pi} }$}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}}\;\leq\;2Me^{-\frac{x^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}}\;.

By performing two third-order Taylor expansions around
Y_{k}
, each
A_{i}
satisfies

\displaystyle A_{i}\;=\;\big|\mathbb{E}\big[h^{\prime}(Y_{k})(\bar{U}_{k}-\zeta_{k})+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}h^{\prime\prime}(Y_{k})(\bar{U}_{k}^{2}-\zeta_{k}^{2})+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{6}$}}h^{\prime\prime\prime}(Y_{k}+\tilde{U}_{k})\bar{U}_{k}^{3}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{6}$}}h^{\prime\prime\prime}(Y_{k}+\tilde{\zeta}_{k})\zeta_{k}^{3}\big]\big|

for some
\tilde{U}_{k}\in[0,U_{k}]
and
\tilde{\zeta}_{k}\in[0,\zeta_{k}]
that exist almost surely. The first two terms vanish by independence of
Y_{k}
and
(\bar{U}_{k},\zeta_{k})
as well as the fact that
\bar{U}_{k}
and
\zeta_{k}
match in mean and variance. Since
\bar{U}_{k}
and
\zeta_{k}
are both symmetric, they both have zero third moments. By adding and subtracting a third moment term and applying the Lipschitz property of
h^{\prime\prime\prime}
, we obtain

\displaystyle A_{i}\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{6}$}}\big|\mathbb{E}\big[\big(h^{\prime\prime\prime}(Y_{k}+\tilde{U}_{k})-h^{\prime\prime\prime}(Y_{k})\big)\bar{U}_{k}^{3}-\big(h^{\prime\prime\prime}(Y_{k}+\tilde{\zeta}_{k})-h^{\prime\prime\prime}(\zeta_{k})\big)\zeta_{k}^{3}\big]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{C''}{6 x^4}$}}\big(\mathbb{E}\,\bar{U}_{k}^{4}+\mathbb{E}\,\zeta_{k}^{4}\big)\;\overset{(a)}{\leq}\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{C'}{2 n^2 x^4}$}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{ \sigma^{ (8-2\nu)/\nu-2} }$}}+\sigma^{4}\Big)\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{C'}{n^2 x^4 \sigma^{(8-2\nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}\;,

where
C^{\prime}
is an absolute constant; in
(a)
, we have noted that

|U_{i}-U^{\prime}_{i}|^{4}\leq(|U_{i}|+|U^{\prime}_{i}|)^{4}\leq 8(|U_{i}|^{4}+|U^{\prime}_{i}|^{4})

and applied the moment bound from (
41
) in the proof of
Lemma

19
.
Combining the bounds gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(W_{i}\geq x/2)\;\leq\;8\mathbb{P}\big(\bar{W}_{i}\geq x/2\big)\;\leq\;2Me^{-\frac{x^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{C' }{n x^4 \sigma^{(8-2\nu)/(\nu-2)}}$}}\;,

and therefore

\displaystyle\mint_{x/2}^{\infty}J_{i}(w)d\mu_{W_{i}}(w)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{10 \, \P( W_i \geq x/2 )}{6^{3/2} n^{3/2} \sigma^{\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{20 M e^{-\frac{x^2}{ 2\sigma^2}} }{6^{3/2} n^{3/2} \sigma^{\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{10 C' }{6^{3/2} n^{5/2} x^4 \sigma^{(8-\nu)/(\nu-2)} }$}}\;.

Combining this with the bound for
\int_{-\infty}^{x/2}J_{i}(w)d\mu_{W_{i}}(w)
in (
45
), we get that for
x>\frac{2\sigma}{\sqrt{3}M}
,

\displaystyle|F_{n}(x)-F_{Z}(x)|\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\msum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\mint_{-\infty}^{x/2}J_{i}(w)d\mu_{W_{i}}(w)+\mint_{x/2}^{\infty}J_{i}(w)d\mu_{W_{i}}(w)\Big)

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3M^3 e^{- \frac{n x^2}{8\sigma^2}} }{n^{1/2}}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{20 M e^{-\frac{x^2}{ 2\sigma^2}} }{6^{3/2} n^{1/2} \sigma^{\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{10 C' }{6^{3/2} n^{3/2} x^4 \sigma^{(8-\nu)/(\nu-2)} }$}}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime}_{M}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{ n^{1/2} \sigma^{\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n^{3/2} x^4 \sigma^{(8-\nu)/(\nu-2)} }$}}\Big)\;,

where
C^{\prime}_{M}>0
is a constant that depends only on
M
. Now for
|x|\leq\frac{2\sigma}{\sqrt{3}\,M}
, we can use the standard Berry-Esséen bound directly and the moment bound from
Lemma

19
to get that

\displaystyle|F_{n}(x)-F_{Z}(x)|\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\mean|X|^3 }{n^{1/2} \sigma^3}$}}\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{a}{n^{1/2} \sigma^3}$}}\big(\sigma^{-\frac{6-2\nu}{\nu-2}}+1\big)\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{a \max\{1, \sigma^3\} }{n^{1/2}}$}}\sigma^{-\frac{\nu}{\nu-2}}

for some absolute constant
a>0
. Since in this case,
e^{-x^{2}/(8\sigma^{2})}\geq e^{-1/6M}
, we again obtain the desired bound that for some constant
C^{\prime\prime}_{M}>0
depending only on
M
,

\displaystyle|F_{n}(x)-F_{Z}(x)|\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{C'_{M} \max\{1, \sigma^3\} e^{-\frac{x^2}{ 8 \sigma^2}} }{n^{1/2} \sigma^{\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}\;\leq\;C^{\prime\prime}_{M}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\max\{1, \sigma^3\} e^{-\frac{x^2}{ 8 \sigma^2}} }{ n^{1/2} \sigma^{\nu/(\nu-2)}}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n^{3/2} x^4 \sigma^{(8-\nu)/(\nu-2)} }$}}\Big)\;.

The proof for
x<-\frac{2\sigma}{\sqrt{3}M}
is exactly analogous to the proof for
x>\frac{2\sigma}{\sqrt{3}M}
.
∎
